# SQLite documentation inventory for a strict-subset engine

Compiled 2026-11-10. This note inventories the SQL language as the official SQLite documentation describes it. It exists to help choose and order the small slices in which sql4s3 adds SQL features. It does not decide anything; see [../spec.md](../spec.md) for decisions.

**Legend**
- **[V]** verified from the text of the cited sqlite.org page (or from the release history at https://sqlite.org/changes.html).
- **[V-measured]** observed by running the stock SQLite 3.54.1 command-line shell (a Homebrew build on macOS arm64). The plain shell changes some connection settings compared with the library defaults (see §4.1).
- **[V-diagram]** read from the labels of a syntax diagram on the cited page.
- **[I]** inferred. Every [I] claim needs an oracle probe before anyone relies on it.

**Version.** The documentation was fetched on 2026-11-10 and describes SQLite **3.54.1** (released 2026-10-16), the newest entry on https://sqlite.org/changes.html. [V] Release 3.52.0 (2026-03-06) was withdrawn: "All of the features that were originally scheduled for the 3.52.0 release have been moved forward into version 3.53.0." [V https://sqlite.org/changes.html] Some pages still date features to 3.52.0; this note gives both versions where they differ.

**Method.** All pages listed below were downloaded and converted to text. The lead author read the type-system pages in full: datatype3, stricttables, withoutrowid, quirks, nulls, nulinstr, lang_expr, floatingpoint, rowvalue, and the schema-layer part of fileformat2. Four research agents each read one group of the remaining pages in full and wrote structured extracts: functions; statements; schema objects; and pragmas, compile options and result codes. Their extracts form most of §1 and the per-area lists in §2, §3 and §6, edited for consistency. The lead author spot-checked their decisive quotations against the page text. Release versions come from grepping the release history. In the agents' tables, the abbreviation "chg" means https://sqlite.org/changes.html.

**Pages read (82).** datatypes.html describes SQLite version 2 and was only skimmed. It has no bearing on 3.54.1.
- SQL language index and every page under it: lang, lang_aggfunc, lang_altertable, lang_analyze, lang_attach, lang_comment, lang_conflict, lang_corefunc, lang_createindex, lang_createtable, lang_createtrigger, lang_createview, lang_createvtab, lang_datefunc, lang_delete, lang_detach, lang_dropindex, lang_droptable, lang_droptrigger, lang_dropview, lang_explain, lang_expr, lang_indexedby, lang_insert, lang_keywords, lang_mathfunc, lang_naming, lang_reindex, lang_replace, lang_returning, lang_savepoint, lang_select, lang_transaction, lang_update, lang_upsert, lang_vacuum, lang_with, windowfunctions, json1, omitted, pragma.
- Type system and table kinds: datatype3, datatypes (the SQLite 2 page, historical only), stricttables, withoutrowid, rowidtable, autoinc, gencol, partialindex, expridx, foreignkeys, quirks, nulls, nulinstr, rowvalue, floatingpoint, deterministic, printf.
- Configuration and limits: compile, limits, rescode, isolation, vtab (SQL-visible parts only), fileformat2 (record format, sort order and schema table only), changes.
- C API pages that define SQL-visible behaviour: c3ref/c_abort, c3ref/c_abort_rollback, c3ref/errcode, c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive, c3ref/db_config, c3ref/c_limit_attached, c3ref/libversion, c3ref/blob_open, c3ref/create_collation, c3ref/c_deterministic, c3ref/create_function, c3ref/value_subtype, c3ref/column_name, c3ref/changes, c3ref/last_insert_rowid, c3ref/set_authorizer, c3ref/c_alter_table.

**How to read this note.** Each section stands alone.
- §1 is the feature inventory, one table row per feature.
- §2 lists every legacy or compatibility flag the docs raise, with a verdict.
- §3 collects what the docs say about STRICT and WITHOUT ROWID tables.
- §4 covers oracle configuration.
- §5 proposes a slice ladder.
- §6 lists the semantic areas where exact compatibility is hard.
- §7 lists conflicts between the docs and the project's decisions, contradictions inside the docs, and what the docs do not say.

**Contents**
1. [Feature inventory](#1-feature-inventory)
2. [Legacy and backward-compatibility flags](#2-legacy-and-backward-compatibility-flags)
3. [STRICT and WITHOUT ROWID](#3-strict-and-without-rowid)
4. [Oracle configuration](#4-oracle-configuration)
5. [Proposed slice ladder](#5-proposed-slice-ladder)
6. [Semantic areas where exact compatibility is hard](#6-semantic-areas-where-exact-compatibility-is-hard)
7. [Conflicts, contradictions and gaps](#7-conflicts-contradictions-and-gaps)

---

## 1. Feature inventory

Each table has these columns:
- **Feature**, with a one-line description.
- **Doc status wording**, quoted.
- **Introduced**, the version from the docs or the release history.
- **STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID**, how the feature differs for those tables.
- **Depends on**, the features that must exist first.
- **Failure / codes**, what happens when the feature fails.
- **URL**.

"-" means the docs say nothing.

### 1.1 Storage classes, data types and affinity

| Feature | Description | Doc status wording | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID | Depends on | Failure / codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Storage class NULL | The NULL value. [V] | - | - | STRICT: allowed in any column without NOT NULL; never allowed in a PRIMARY KEY column of a STRICT or WITHOUT ROWID table. [V] | - | NOT NULL violation: `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL` [I, see §1.7] | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#storageclasses |
| Storage class INTEGER | Signed integer, "stored in 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, or 8 bytes"; in memory always an 8-byte signed integer. Range -9223372036854775808 to +9223372036854775807. [V] | - | - | STRICT `INT`/`INTEGER` columns. [V] | - | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#storageclasses, https://sqlite.org/floatingpoint.html |
| Storage class REAL | "8-byte IEEE floating point number". Can be -0.0, +Inf, -Inf, NaN at the binary level. [V] | "Floating point values are approximate." [V] | - | STRICT `REAL` column; a REAL with no fractional part may be stored on disk as an integer, which is "completely invisible at the SQL level". [V] | - | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#storageclasses, https://sqlite.org/floatingpoint.html#fpapprox |
| Storage class TEXT | String "stored using the database encoding (UTF-8, UTF-16BE or UTF-16LE)". NUL characters may appear inside strings. [V] | NUL in strings: "not recommended". [V] | - | STRICT `TEXT` column. [V] | encoding | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#storageclasses, https://sqlite.org/nulinstr.html |
| Storage class BLOB | "stored exactly as it was input". [V] | - | - | STRICT `BLOB` column. [V] | - | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#storageclasses |
| Boolean | No storage class; 0 and 1. `TRUE`/`FALSE` are "really just alternative spellings for the integer literals 1 and 0". [V] | `TRUE`/`FALSE` resolve to same-named columns "for backwards compatibility". [V] | 3.23.0 (`TRUE`/`FALSE` keywords) [V] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#boolean, https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#booleanexpr |
| Date and time | No storage class; TEXT ISO-8601, REAL Julian day, or INTEGER Unix time. [V] | - | - | STRICT: pick TEXT, REAL or INTEGER column. [I] | date functions | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#datetime |
| Column affinity (TEXT, NUMERIC, INTEGER, REAL, BLOB) | Preferred storage class of a column; values converted on insert when lossless. "BLOB" affinity "used to be called NONE". [V] | "Historical note" (rename of NONE). [V] | - | STRICT: column type must be INT, INTEGER, REAL, TEXT, BLOB, ANY; content must be NULL or the declared type after the "usual affinity rules" coercion, else `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE`. [V] | - | `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE` (STRICT only) [V] | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affinity, https://sqlite.org/stricttables.html |
| Affinity name rules (substring "INT", "CHAR/CLOB/TEXT", "BLOB"/none, "REAL/FLOA/DOUB", else NUMERIC) | Declared type to affinity, "For tables not declared as STRICT". Also applied to the type name in CAST. [V] | - | - | STRICT allows only the six names, so for us the rule reduces to INT/INTEGER→INTEGER, REAL→REAL, TEXT→TEXT, BLOB→BLOB, ANY→(no coercion). [I] Still needed in full for `CAST(x AS type)` unless CAST type names are restricted. [I] | - | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affname |
| Expression affinity | Column references (also parenthesised) have the column's affinity; `CAST(x AS t)` has t's affinity; `COLLATE` keeps left operand's; everything else, including `+x`, has no affinity. [V] | - | - | STRICT columns still have affinity (INTEGER, REAL, TEXT, BLOB). [I: datatype3 §3.1 only says how affinity is determined for non-STRICT; stricttables says coercion uses "the usual affinity rules"] | columns | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#expraff |
| View / subquery column affinity | Columns of a view or FROM subquery take the affinity of the result expression. For a compound SELECT "it is indeterminate which of the SELECT statements will be used to determine affinity". [V] | "The choice might vary across different versions of SQLite." [V] | - | - | views, subqueries | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affview, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affcompoundview |
| Collating sequences BINARY, NOCASE, RTRIM | Built-in collations. NOCASE folds only the 26 ASCII letters; U+0000 acts as terminator for NOCASE. RTRIM ignores trailing spaces. [V] | - | - | Same in STRICT. [V "COLLATE clauses work the same"] | - | Unknown collation: `SQLITE_ERROR_MISSING_COLLSEQ` [I, see §1.7] | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#collation |

### 1.2 Lexical elements and literals

| Feature | Description | Doc status wording | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID | Depends on | Failure / codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Integer literal | Decimal digits; a literal outside the 64-bit range becomes a floating-point literal. [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#litvalue |
| Floating-point literal | Has "." and/or exponent (E or e); "." is always the decimal point. [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#litvalue |
| Digit separator `_` | "a single extra underscore ("_") character can be added between any two digits"; also allowed between hex digits per the syntax diagram. [V] | - | 3.46.0 [V] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#litvalue |
| Hexadecimal integer literal `0x…` | 64-bit two's complement; `0x8000000000000000` is -9223372036854775808. Only the parser understands it, not run-time conversions. [V] | "For backwards compatibility, the "0x" hexadecimal integer notation is only understood by the SQL language parser, not by the type conversions routines." [V] | 3.8.6 [V] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#hexint |
| String literal `'…'` | Single quotes; `''` escapes a quote; no backslash escapes. [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#litvalue |
| BLOB literal `X'…'` | Hex digits in quotes after x or X. [V] | - | - | - | - | Malformed: syntax error [I] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#litvalue |
| `NULL`, `TRUE`, `FALSE` | Literal tokens; `TRUE`/`FALSE` are 1/0 unless a same-named object exists. [V] | "for the sake of backwards compatibility". [V] | 3.23.0 (`TRUE`/`FALSE`) [V] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#booleanexpr |
| `CURRENT_TIME`, `CURRENT_DATE`, `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` | Literal-value keywords in the syntax diagram (time of the statement). [V syntax diagram] | - | - | Non-deterministic. [I; see §1.4] | date/time | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#litvalue |
| Parameters `?NNN`, `?`, `:AAAA`, `@AAAA`, `$AAAA` | Bind placeholders; unbound parameters are NULL. `$AAAA` may contain `::` and a `(...)` suffix (Tcl syntax). [V] | `?`: "provided for compatibility with other database engines … the use of this parameter format is discouraged". `$`: "results from the fact that SQLite is really a Tcl extension that has escaped into the wild". [V] | - | - | - | Number > `SQLITE_MAX_VARIABLE_NUMBER`: error [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#varparam |

### 1.3 Expressions and operators

| Feature | Description | Doc status wording | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID | Depends on | Failure / codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Operator precedence | Highest to lowest: unary `~ + -`; `COLLATE`; `\|\| -> ->>`; `* / %`; `+ -`; `& \| << >>`; `ESCAPE`; `< > <= >=`; `= == <> != IS IS NOT IS [NOT] DISTINCT FROM BETWEEN IN MATCH LIKE REGEXP GLOB ISNULL NOTNULL NOT NULL`; `NOT`; `AND`; `OR`. Binary operators are left-associative. [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops |
| Unary `+` | No-op returning the operand unchanged, but strips column affinity (and makes the operand "not a column" for affinity, but still a column for collation). [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#expraff, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#colrules |
| Unary `-`, `~` | Negation and bitwise NOT. [V syntax] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops |
| `+ - * /` | Integer arithmetic if both operands are integers "and no overflow would result", else IEEE 754 floating point. Integer divide truncates toward zero. Division by zero gives NULL. Non-numeric text converts to 0 or 0.0. [V] | - | - | - | type conversion | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html (§5) |
| `%` | Casts both operands to INTEGER, returns remainder; datatype3 says it "returns either INTEGER or REAL (or NULL) depending on the type of its operands". [V] (The two pages differ in wording; check with the oracle. [I]) | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html |
| `& \| << >>` | Integer operands (REAL cast like CAST AS INTEGER); always INTEGER or NULL. [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html (§5) |
| `\|\|` | String concatenation. The docs class it with `->` and `->>` as the binary operators that "can return values of any type"; all other binary operators return a numeric value or NULL. [V] Numbers are first rendered as text. [I] | - | - | - | text conversion of numbers | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops |
| `->`, `->>` | JSON extract operators (functions `"->"()` and `"->>"()`). [V] | - | 3.38.0 [V changes] | - | JSON | malformed JSON error [I, see §1.4] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#extract |
| `= == < <= > >= != <>` | Comparison after affinity rules; NULL operand gives NULL. [V] | - | - | Affinity from STRICT columns still applies. [I] | affinity, collation | - | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#comparisons |
| `IS`, `IS NOT`, `IS [NOT] DISTINCT FROM` | NULL-safe equality; never NULL. `IS TRUE`/`IS FALSE` evaluate the left side as boolean. [V] | Compact IS / IS NOT "are an SQLite extension". [V] | IS DISTINCT FROM: 3.39.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#isisnot, https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#isdf |
| `AND`, `OR`, `NOT` | Three-valued logic: `NULL AND false` = 0, `NULL OR true` = 1. [V] | - | - | - | boolean conversion | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops |
| `ISNULL`, `NOTNULL`, `NOT NULL` postfix | Null tests (syntax diagram). [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html |
| `BETWEEN` | `x>=y AND x<=z` with x evaluated once; affinity and collation applied as two separate comparisons. [V] | - | - | - | comparison | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#between, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#compaff |
| `IN`, `NOT IN` (list) | RHS values "have no affinity"; collation is that of the LHS. Empty list allowed (most engines reject). Full NULL matrix documented. [V] | Empty list: SQLite allows it "but most other SQL database engines and the SQL92 standard require the list to contain at least one element". [V] | - | - | comparison | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#in_op |
| `IN (SELECT …)`, `IN table-name`, `IN table-function(…)` | Subquery form, handled like `x=y`; RHS may be a bare table name meaning `(SELECT * FROM name)`. [V] | - | - | - | subqueries | Column count mismatch: error [I] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#in_op |
| `LIKE [ESCAPE]` | `%` and `_` wildcards, ASCII-only case folding by default; implemented by `like(Y,X)`/`like(Y,X,Z)`. [V] | Case sensitivity changeable by `case_sensitive_like` pragma (see §1.8). [V] | - | - | - | ESCAPE not one character: error [V "must evaluate to a string consisting of a single character"] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#like |
| `GLOB` | Unix glob wildcards, case sensitive; implemented by `glob(Y,X)`. [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#glob |
| `REGEXP` | Calls user function `regexp(Y,X)`; none defined by default, "will normally result in an error message". [V] | - | - | - | application function | `no such function` error [I] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#regexp |
| `MATCH` | Calls `match()`; default "raises an exception and is not really useful for anything". [V] | - | - | - | virtual tables (FTS) | error [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#match |
| `CASE` (with and without base) | Lazy evaluation; base form compares like `=` (affinity, collation, NULL rules). NULL WHEN is untrue. [V] | - | - | - | comparison | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#case |
| `CAST(expr AS type-name)` | Conversion that "always takes place even if the conversion is lossy"; result class by affinity of type-name: BLOB/none, TEXT, REAL, INTEGER (saturating), NUMERIC. [V] | Pre-3.8.2 overflow behaviour noted as historical. [V] | - | Type-name grammar accepts any name; STRICT restriction applies to column declarations only. [I] | affinity rules | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#castexpr |
| `COLLATE name` (postfix) | Assigns a collation; leftmost explicit COLLATE in a comparison wins. [V] | - | - | - | collations | unknown collation error [I] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#collateop, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#colrules |
| Column reference `[schema.][table.]column` | Names from CREATE TABLE, or `ROWID`/`OID`/`_ROWID_`, which "are not available on WITHOUT ROWID tables". [V] | - | - | WITHOUT ROWID: no rowid aliases. [V] | tables | `no such column` [I] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html (§9) |
| Scalar subquery `(SELECT …)` | First row of the result, NULL if none. [V] | - | - | - | SELECT | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#subq |
| `[NOT] EXISTS (SELECT …)` | Always 0 or 1. [V] | - | - | - | SELECT | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#exists_op |
| Correlated subquery | References outer columns; re-evaluated each time. [V] | - | - | - | SELECT | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#cosub |
| Row values `(a, b, …)` | Size ≥ 2; comparable with `< <= > >= = <> IS IS NOT IN NOT IN BETWEEN CASE`; RHS of row-value IN must be a subquery; `UPDATE … SET (a,b)=…`. NULL makes the result NULL only if a substitution could change it. [V] | - | 3.15.0 [V] | - | subqueries | size mismatch: error [I] | https://sqlite.org/rowvalue.html |
| Function call `name(args) [FILTER (WHERE …)] [OVER …]` | Simple, aggregate (DISTINCT, ORDER BY, FILTER), window (OVER, FILTER) forms. Same name may be simple and aggregate with different arity (`max(x)` vs `max(x,y,…)`). [V] | - | ORDER BY in aggregates: 3.44.0 [V changes] | - | functions | wrong arity / unknown function: error [I] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#*funcinexpr |
| `RAISE(IGNORE \| ROLLBACK,msg \| ABORT,msg \| FAIL,msg)` | Part of the expression grammar; used in trigger bodies. The message may be any expression since 3.47.0. [V syntax, §1.6] | - | - | - | triggers | `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT` with extended code `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_TRIGGER` [V, see §1.6, §1.7] | https://sqlite.org/lang_createtrigger.html |
| Boolean context conversion | Result cast to NUMERIC; 0 / 0.0 false, NULL stays NULL, others true; `'english'` is false, `'1english'` true. [V] | - | - | - | CAST NUMERIC | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#booleanexpr |

### 1.4 Built-in functions

Conventions used in the "One-line description" column:
- **Arity**: the argument counts the docs allow.
- **NULL**: what the docs say happens with NULL input.
- **Result**: the result type the docs state.
- **Det**: whether the function is deterministic. "[V]" means the docs state it for this function. "Det: yes [I]" means the docs only say that "Most built-in SQL functions in SQLite are deterministic" (https://sqlite.org/deterministic.html). The oracle can confirm each function's flags with `PRAGMA function_list`, which (since 3.31.0) shows "the function property flags SQLITE_DETERMINISTIC, SQLITE_DIRECTONLY, SQLITE_INNOCUOUS, and/or SQLITE_SUBTYPE" [V changes.html 3.31.0].
- "docs silent" means the pages read say nothing on that point. Each such point needs an oracle probe.

#### Core scalar functions (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced (version, per docs/changes) | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| abs(X) | Absolute value. Arity 1. NULL→NULL [V]. A string or blob that cannot convert to a number → 0.0 [V]. Result is numeric. The type follows the input (docs silent on INTEGER vs REAL) [I]. Det: yes [V: "the abs(X) function always returns the same answer"; c_deterministic: "The abs() function is deterministic"; also "an example of an innocuous function"]. | - | 2.0.3 (2001-10-13) "Added new functions: round() and abs()". Overflow detection in 3.3.5 [V changes] | - | - | abs(-9223372036854775808) "throws an integer overflow error since there is no equivalent positive 64-bit two's complement value" [V]. Message text not given; likely "integer overflow" as for sum() [I] | https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#abs |
| changes() | Number of rows changed, inserted or deleted by the most recently completed INSERT/DELETE/UPDATE, "exclusive of statements in lower-level triggers". It wraps sqlite3_changes64(). Arity 0. Result: integer [I]. Det: **NO** [V deterministic.html: "The answers from changes() and last_insert_rowid() depend on prior SQL statements"]. | - | sqlite_changes() C API 2.4.7. sqlite3_changes64 3.37.0 [V changes] | Long-standing sqlite3_changes() bug for WITHOUT ROWID tables fixed in 3.8.11.1 [V changes] | Connection state | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#changes |
| char(X1,...,XN) | String built from the Unicode codepoints of integer args. Arity 0..N. With zero args it returns '' [V changes 3.8.4: "returns an empty string rather than an "out of memory" error when called with zero arguments"]. Invalid codepoints: "the result is still a zero-terminated byte array, but that byte array might not be a valid Unicode string" [V]. NULL: docs silent. Result: text. Det: yes [I]. | - | 3.7.16 "Added SQL functions unicode(A) and char(X1,...,XN)" [V changes] | - | - | No error for invalid codepoints [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#char |
| coalesce(X,Y,...) | First non-NULL argument, or NULL if all are NULL. "Coalesce() must have at least 2 arguments." Det: yes [I]. Unused arguments are not evaluated [V changes 3.6.21: "implemented using in-line VDBE code ... so that unused arguments need never be evaluated"]. | - | 2.4.0 "Added support for new functions: coalesce(), lower(), upper(), and random()" [V changes] | - | - | Fewer than 2 args → error. Message text not on page [I] | https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#coalesce |
| concat(X,...) | Concatenates the string form of all non-NULL args. "If all arguments are NULL, then concat() returns an empty string." Arity ≥1 per signature. Result: text. Det: yes [I]. | "compatible with PostgreSQL, SQLServer, and MySQL" [V changes] | 3.44.0 [V changes] | - | Real-to-text rendering (FP_DIGITS, see D) [I] | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#concat |
| concat_ws(SEP,X,...) | Joins non-NULL args after the first, with the text of SEP as separator. SEP NULL → NULL. All other args NULL → ''. Empty strings ARE included [V changes 3.50.2: "Fix the concat_ws() SQL function so that it includes empty strings in the concatenation"]. Det: yes [I]. | - | 3.44.0. Behaviour changed in 3.50.2. Memory bug for SEP >2MB fixed in 3.49.1 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#concat_ws |
| format(FORMAT,...) | printf-style formatting; see A.5. FORMAT missing or NULL → NULL. Missing args are treated as NULL, "which is translated into 0 or 0.0 for numeric formats or an empty string for %s". "%n format is silently ignored and does not consume an argument". "%p format is an alias for %X" (but the printf page says %p "works like %x", see E). "%z format is interchangeable with %s". Result: text. Det: yes [I]. | Renamed from printf "for compatibility with other database engines" [V] | printf() in 3.8.3. Renamed to format() in 3.38.0 [V changes] | - | Real-to-text routines | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#format |
| glob(X,Y) | Same as "Y GLOB X". The arguments are reversed relative to the operator: X is the pattern. Case sensitive [V lang_expr: "GLOB is case sensitive, unlike LIKE"]. NULL: docs silent [I: NULL]. Result: integer 0/1 [I]. Det: yes [I]. | Can be overridden via sqlite3_create_function() [V] | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#glob |

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |

| FILTER (WHERE expr) on aggregates | "only rows for which the expr is true are included". | - | 3.30.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#aggfilter |
| ORDER BY inside an aggregate | Sets the input order. "If no ORDER BY clause is specified, the inputs to the aggregate occur in an arbitrary order that might change from one invocation to the next." | "arbitrary order that might change" | 3.44.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#aggorderby |
| ordered-set aggregate (WITHIN GROUP) | Notation with a single ORDER BY term, for functions flagged SQLITE_SELFORDER1 [V c_deterministic]. | "only available if SQLite is built using the -DSQLITE_ENABLE_ORDERED_SET_AGGREGATES" | - | - | Build option | "If the ordered-set aggregate notation is used on a function that lacks this flag, then an error is raised." [V] | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqliteselforder1 |
| avg(X) | Average of non-NULL X. Strings and blobs that do not look like numbers count as 0. Always REAL if there is at least one non-NULL input. NULL if no non-NULL inputs. "computed as total()/count()". | - | - | - | - | Never an integer overflow error (follows from total()) [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#avg |
| count(X) / count(*) | count(X) counts non-NULL X. count(*) counts rows in the group. Result: integer. | - | Aggregates in 1.0. count(*) on an empty table returns 0 since 2.0.5 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#count |
| group_concat(X) / group_concat(X,Y) | Joins non-NULL X. The separator is Y, or "," by default. "The order of the concatenated elements is arbitrary unless an ORDER BY argument is included immediately after the last parameter." Empty-group result: docs silent [I: NULL]. | - | Productized 3.5.4. Empty-string fixes in 3.46.1 (window version) and 3.47.0: "returns an empty string, not a NULL, if it receives a single input value which is an empty string" [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#group_concat |
| string_agg(X,Y) | Alias of group_concat(X,Y). | "compatible with PostgreSQL and SQL-Server and group_concat() is compatible with MySQL" | 3.44.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#group_concat |
| max(X) (aggregate) | "the value that would be returned last in an ORDER BY on the same column". NULL iff there are no non-NULL values. | - | - | - | Collation [I] | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#max_agg |
| min(X) (aggregate) | "the first non-NULL value that would appear in an ORDER BY of the column". NULL iff there are no non-NULL values. | - | - | - | Collation [I] | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#min_agg |
| median(X) | Same as percentile_cont(X,0.5). | Only "if the amalgamation is compiled using -DSQLITE_ENABLE_PERCENTILE" | 3.51.0 (built-in, opt-in) [V] | - | SQLITE_ENABLE_PERCENTILE | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#median |
| percentile(Y,P) | P in 0..100. "The value of P must be the same for all terms of the aggregate and may not be NULL." Y must be NULL or numeric. | same | 3.51.0. Extension since 3.8.0 [V] | - | SQLITE_ENABLE_PERCENTILE | "Any non-NULL Y input that is not numeric causes an error to be raised." [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#percentile |
| percentile_cont(Y,P) | P in 0.0..1.0. Weighted average of the closest inputs. | same | 3.51.0 [V] | - | same | same | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#percentile_cont |
| percentile_disc(Y,P) | Like percentile_cont, but returns an actual input value, "the smaller of the two possible choices". | same (the doc paragraph says "percentile_cont()" by copy-paste) | 3.51.0 [V] | - | same | same | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#percentile_disc |
| sum(X) | Sum of non-NULL values. NULL if there are no non-NULL rows. INTEGER if all non-NULL inputs are integers. NULL handling per SQL standard since 3.2.6. Infinity fixes in 3.43.1 and 3.45.3 [V changes] | - | - | "Sum() will throw an "integer overflow" exception if all inputs are integers or NULL and an integer overflow occurs at any point during the computation. | total(X) | Like sum, but returns 0.0 for no rows and is always REAL.
