#### Date and time functions (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html)

The date/time functions "are usually considered deterministic". They become non-deterministic when they use 'now' (including the implied 'now' when no args are given), 'localtime' or 'utc' [V https://sqlite.org/deterministic.html#dtexception].

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| date(time-value, modifier, ...) | Text YYYY-MM-DD. Same as strftime('%F', ...). With no args it means 'now'. | - | Experimental 2.8.6 [V changes] | - | Clock if 'now' | Bad input or modifier → NULL [V §3.1] | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#dttm |
| time(...) | Text HH:MM:SS, or HH:MM:SS.SSS with subsec. Same as strftime('%T', ...). | - | - | - | - | NULL on bad input | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#dttm |
| datetime(...) | Text YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS, with .SSS if subsec. Same as strftime('%F %T', ...). | - | - | - | - | NULL on bad input | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#dttm |
| julianday(...) | REAL fractional days since noon in Greenwich on November 24, 4714 B.C. (proleptic Gregorian). Same as CAST(strftime('%J', ...) as REAL), but without "inexactitude". | - | - | - | - | NULL on bad input | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#jlndy |
| unixepoch(...) | INTEGER seconds since 1970-01-01 UTC. REAL with subsec. Same as CAST(strftime('%s', ...) as INT). | - | 3.38.0 [V changes] | - | - | NULL on bad input | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#uepch |
| strftime(format, time-value, ...) | Formats per substitutions %d %e %f %F %G %g %H %I %j %J %k %l %m %M %p %P %R %s %S %T %U %u %V %w %W %Y %%. %H is "00-24". %Y is "0000-9999". | "Earlier versions of SQLite might not support all substitutions." | %e %F %I %k %l %p %P %R %T %u in 3.44.0. %G %g %U %V in 3.46.0 [V changes] | - | - | "If an undefined or unsupported substitution is seen, the result is NULL." [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#strftm |
| timediff(A,B) | Text "(+\|-)YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS.SSS" giving the shift that takes B to A. Invariant: datetime(A) = datetime(B, timediff(A,B)). Exactly 2 args, no modifiers. Only ISO-8601 and Julian-day inputs. | "designed to be human-readable". Different spans can give the same result. | 3.43.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmdif |
| time-value formats 1-7 | YYYY-MM-DD, then optionally [ T]HH:MM[:SS[.SSS]]. "T" is a literal separator. | "SQLite only supports the ones specifically enumerated above." | "T" separator 3.2.0 [V changes] | - | - | Others → NULL [I] | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval |
| time-value formats 8-10 | HH:MM[:SS[.SSS]]. "assume a date of 2000-01-01". | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval |
| time-value 'now' (format 11) | Current time from the VFS xCurrentTime, in UTC. "always returns exactly the same value for multiple invocations within the same sqlite3_step() call". | - | Same-step stability since 3.8.1 [V changes] | - | VFS clock (non-deterministic) | Exception in deterministic-only contexts [V deterministic.html] | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval |
| time-value DDDDDDDDDD (format 12) | Julian day as integer or float. It is read as unix time only if 'auto' or 'unixepoch' follows immediately. | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval |
| timezone suffix | Formats 2-10 may end in "[+-]HH:MM" or "Z". "Z" is a no-op. A non-zero offset is subtracted to get UTC. | - | "Z" accepted since 3.5.7 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval |
| fractional seconds | "only the first three digits are significant to the result". More or fewer digits are accepted. | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval |
| modifiers 1-6: NNN days/hours/minutes/seconds/months/years | Adds the amount. The trailing "s" is optional. "NNN value can be any floating point number, with an optional '+' or '-' prefix." | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#dtmods |
| modifiers 7-13: ±HH:MM[:SS[.SSS]], ±YYYY-MM-DD[ HH:MM[:SS[.SSS]]] | Time shift, applied year → month → day → time. "+"/"-" is required for 10-13 and optional for 7-9. | - | ±YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS.SSS in 3.43.0. HH:MM:SS modifier in 2.8.13 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmshf |
| ceiling / floor (14,15) | Resolves month/year shift ambiguity. "The default behavior is "ceiling"." floor resolves "to the last day of the previous month". Applies to the modifier just before it. | "There is no consensus on how to resolve this ambiguity" | Not in changes grep | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#dtambg |
| start of month/year/day (16-18) | Moves back to the start. | - | Bug fix 3.18.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#somod |
| end of month/year/day (19-21) | Moves forward to "the last millisecond of the subject month, year, or day". | - | 3.54.0 [V doc and changes] | - | - | "end  of day" (2 spaces) → NULL [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#eomod |
| weekday N (22) | Moves forward to the next weekday N (0=Sunday). Negative N moves backwards. N must be -6..+6 and an integer. | - | Negative N in 3.54.0 [V] | - | - | "weekday 5.1" and "weekday -7" → NULL [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#wdmod |
| unixepoch modifier (23) | Reads the preceding DDDDDDDDDD as unix seconds. | "behavior is undefined" if it does not immediately follow a numeric time-value | Full date span since 3.16.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#jdmod |
| julianday modifier (24) | Forces the numeric time-value to be read as a Julian day. "scarcely more than a no-op". | - | 3.38.0 [V changes] | - | - | "Any other use of the 'julianday' modifier is an error and causes the function to return NULL." [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#jdmod |
| auto modifier (25) | 0.0-5373484.499999 → Julian day. Up to -210866760000..253402300799 → unix time. Otherwise NULL. No-op for text. | "should be avoided for applications that might make use of dates in the opening months of 1970" | 3.38.0 [V changes] | - | - | Out of range → NULL | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#automod |
| localtime modifier (26) | Treats the value as UTC and converts it to local time. Uses localtime_r(), which "normally only works for years between 1970 and 2037". Years outside are mapped into that range. | "If "localtime" follows a time that is not UTC, then the behavior is undefined." | Preserves fractional seconds since 3.38.1. No-op if already local, since 3.46.0 [V changes] | - | **Host TZ / OS (environment dependency)**. Non-deterministic [V] | Exception in deterministic-only contexts [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#localtime |
| utc modifier (27) | Treats the value as local time and converts it to UTC. | "If the time to the left is not in localtime, then the result of "utc" is undefined." | No-op if already UTC since 3.10.0 and 3.46.0 [V changes] | - | Host TZ. Non-deterministic [V] | Same | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#localtime |
| subsec / subsecond (28,29) | Millisecond resolution for datetime(), time(), unixepoch() and strftime %s. It may be the first arg, which then implies 'now'. | "this might increase to a higher resolution in future releases" | 3.42.0 [V changes] | - | - | "SUBSECOND" → NULL ("Modifiers are case sensitive") [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#subsec |

#### Math functions (https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html)

All of them: they are "only active if the amalgamation is compiled using the -DSQLITE_ENABLE_MATH_FUNCTIONS compile-time option". Arguments may be integers, floats, or strings that look like numbers. "If any argument is NULL or a blob or a string that is not readily converted into a number, then the function will return NULL." "These functions also return NULL for domain errors". Introduced 3.35.0: "Added built-in SQL math functions(). (Requires the -DSQLITE_ENABLE_MATH_FUNCTIONS compile-time option.)" [V changes]. Result types are not stated per function. Integer vs REAL for ceil/floor/trunc is docs silent [I: probe the oracle]. Det: yes [I].

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| acos(X) | Arccosine, in radians | - | 3.35.0 | - | ENABLE_MATH_FUNCTIONS | |X|>1 → NULL [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#acos |
| acosh(X) | Hyperbolic arccosine | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | Domain → NULL | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#acosh |
| asin(X) | Arcsine, in radians | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | Domain → NULL | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#asin |
| asinh(X) | Hyperbolic arcsine | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#asinh |
| atan(X) | Arctangent, in radians | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#atan |
| atan2(Y,X) | Arctangent of Y/X, in the correct quadrant | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#atan2 |
| atanh(X) | Hyperbolic arctangent | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | Domain → NULL | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#atanh |
| ceil(X) / ceiling(X) | "first representable integer value greater than or equal to X" | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#ceil |
| cos(X) / cosh(X) | Cosine (radians) / hyperbolic cosine | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#cos |
| degrees(X) | Radians → degrees | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#degrees |
| exp(X) | e^X | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#exp |
| floor(X) | "first representable integer value less than or equal to X" | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#floor |
| ln(X) | Natural log | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | X≤0 → NULL [I, domain error] | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#ln |
| log(X) / log10(X) / log(B,X) | Base-10 log, or base-B log of X (the base comes FIRST) | "Compatibility note: SQLite works like PostgreSQL in that the log() function computes a base-10 logarithm. Most other SQL database engines compute a natural logarithm for log()." 2-arg order is "reversed from SQL Server" | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#log |
| log2(X) | Base-2 log | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#log2 |
| mod(X,Y) | Remainder. "similar to the '%' operator, except that it works for non-integer arguments" | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | Y=0: docs silent [I: NULL] | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#mod |
| pi() | Returns 3.141592653589793115997963468544185161590576171875 | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#pi |
| pow(X,Y) / power(X,Y) | X^Y | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#pow |
| radians(X) | Degrees → radians | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#radians |
| sin(X) / sinh(X) | Sine / hyperbolic sine | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#sin |
| sqrt(X) | Square root. "NULL is returned if X is negative." | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | NULL | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#sqrt |
| tan(X) / tanh(X) | Tangent / hyperbolic tangent | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#tan |
| trunc(X) | Rounds toward zero: "the integer part of X" | - | 3.35.0 | - | same | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#trunc |

#### format()/printf() formatter (https://sqlite.org/printf.html)

Syntax: `%[flags][width][.precision][length]type` [V #fmtdetail].

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| %% | Literal "%" | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html#fmtdetail |
| %d, %i | "signed integer ... displayed in decimal". The conversion of a REAL or TEXT argument is docs silent [I: probably truncation toward zero; probe] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html#fmtdetail |
| %u | Unsigned decimal | - | - | - | - | - | same |
| %f | Double in decimal | - | - | - | - | - | same |
| %e, %E | Exponential notation | - | - | - | - | - | same |
| %g, %G | Decimal or exponential | - | - | - | - | - | same |
| %x, %X | Hex, lower/upper case | - | - | - | - | - | same |
| %o | Octal | - | - | - | - | - | same |
| %s, %z | String. NULL → empty. "identical for the SQL printf() function" | "Caution: Recent versions of GNU printf use %z to render size_t" | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html#percentz |
| %c | In format(), "the argument is a string from which the first character is extracted". Precision N>1 repeats it N times ("non-standard extension found only in SQLite") | - | Precision >70 fix 3.8.7.x [V changes] | - | - | - | same |
| %p | "works like %x" in format() (printf page). lang_corefunc says "alias for %X". **The two pages conflict** | - | - | - | - | - | same |
| %n | Ignored in format() and does not consume an arg [V corefunc] | - | - | - | - | - | same |
| %q, %Q | Doubles single quotes. %Q adds surrounding quotes, and NULL → unquoted NULL. %#Q/%#q escape U+0001..U+001f and "\", and %#Q wraps the result in unistr('...') | "SQLite enhancements" | %Q 2.x. "#" form 3.50.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html#percentq |
| %w | Like %q, but doubles double-quotes, for identifiers | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html#percentw |
| %j, %J | JSON string literal. %J adds double quotes, and NULL → unquoted null. %j with NULL → no output. Precision counts bytes, or code points with "!" | "first appeared in SQLite 3.54.0" | 3.54.0 [V] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html#pj |
| length field (l, ll) | "The length is ignored for the format() SQL function which always uses 64-bit values." | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html |
| width / `*` width | Minimum width in **bytes**, or characters with "!". A negative `*` value means left-justify. Capped at SQLITE_PRINTF_PRECISION_LIMIT, "normally 2147483647". Above the cap: "a smaller width is used ... unspecified and can be 0" | "unspecified" | Limits removed 3.7.9 [V changes] | - | SQLITE_PRINTF_PRECISION_LIMIT | - | same |
| precision / `*` precision | Strings: bytes, or characters with "!". Integers: minimum digits. %e/%f: digits after the point. %g: significant digits, "rounded up to 1 if the specified precision is 0". Also capped by SQLITE_FP_PRECISION_LIMIT | "unspecified" substitute when over the limit | - | - | same | - | same |
| flag `-` | Left-justify. "In SQLite the left-justify flag is ignored if the zero-padding flag is present." | Legacy, see B | - | - | - | - | same |
| flag `+` / space | Sign or space before positive numbers | - | - | - | - | - | same |
| flag `0` | Zero pad. **Inf/NaN render as "9.0e+999" and "null"** with this flag, otherwise "Inf" and "NaN" | - | - | - | - | - | same |
| flag `#` (alt-form-1) | %#g/%#G strip trailing zeros. %#f suppresses "-" when all digits are zero ("-0.004" → "0.00"). Forces a decimal point. Adds 0/0x/0X prefixes. Also %#Q/%#q | - | "-0.00" suppression 3.51.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html#alt1 |
| flag `,` | Thousands separators left of the decimal point | "non-standard extension" | Integers 3.18.0. Floats 3.42.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html#comma |
| flag `!` (alt-form-2) | Strings: width/precision in code points. Floats: 26 significant digits (instead of 16), forces a decimal point and at least one digit after it | "non-standard extension that appears in no other printf() implementations" | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html#alt2 |
| float significant-digit cap | "limited in precision to 16 significant digits or 26 significant digits if the "!" ... flag is used" | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/printf.html |
| positional args | "does not handle posix positional referencing modifiers" | - | - | - | - | Unsupported [V] | https://sqlite.org/printf.html |

#### Window functions (https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OVER clause / window-func invocation | A function with OVER is a window function. "window functions cannot use the DISTINCT keyword". "may only appear in the result set and in the ORDER BY clause of a SELECT statement." | - | 3.25.0 [V] | Doc examples use `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY` and untyped columns, so they must be rewritten for STRICT/WITHOUT ROWID [I] | - | Misuse → error [I]. Not allowed in the recursive part of a CTE [V changes 3.25.3] | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html |
| WINDOW clause (named windows) | "comes after any HAVING clause and before any ORDER BY" | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html |
| PARTITION BY | Splits rows into partitions. Without it there is one partition | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#ptxn |
| window ORDER BY | Defines order and peers | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html |
| frame type ROWS / RANGE / GROUPS | ROWS counts rows. GROUPS counts peer groups ("Equivalent" means IS is true). RANGE: band of values. "The RANGE frame type requires that the ORDER BY clause of the window has exactly one term." | - | ROWS/RANGE 3.25.0. GROUPS 3.28.0 [V] | - | - | RANGE with <expr> and ≠1 ORDER BY term → error [I] | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#frametype |
| default frame | "RANGE BETWEEN UNBOUNDED PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW EXCLUDE NO OTHERS" | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#framespec |
| boundaries: UNBOUNDED PRECEDING, <expr> PRECEDING, CURRENT ROW, <expr> FOLLOWING, UNBOUNDED FOLLOWING | <expr> is "a non-negative constant numeric expression". ROWS/GROUPS need an integer. RANGE allows a real. "0 PRECEDING" = CURRENT ROW. A frame with no BETWEEN ends at CURRENT ROW. "The ending frame boundary may not take a form that appears higher in the above list than the starting frame boundary." | - | RANGE <expr> 3.28.0 [V] | - | - | Violations → error [I] | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#frameboundary |
| RANGE <expr> rule | Non-numeric Xi or Xc → first row where "Xi IS Xc". ASC → Xi>=Xc-<expr>. DESC → Xi<=Xc+<expr> | - | 3.28.0 | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#exprrange |
| EXCLUDE NO OTHERS / CURRENT ROW / GROUP / TIES | Frame exclusion. For EXCLUDE GROUP, rows count as peers "even if the frame type is ROWS" | - | 3.28.0 [V] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#wexcls |
| FILTER on aggregate window functions | Filtered rows are excluded from every frame | - | 3.30.0 (FILTER) [V changes] | - | - | "It is a syntax error to specify a FILTER clause as part of a built-in window function invocation." [V] | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html |
| window chaining | `OVER (base ...)` copies PARTITION BY and ORDER BY. The new window must not have PARTITION BY. It must not have ORDER BY if the base has one. The base may not have a frame. `OVER win` and `OVER (win)` differ (the latter fails if win has a frame) | - | 3.28.0 [V] | - | - | Violations → error [V wording "will fail"] | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#wchaining |
| aggregate window functions | "all of the built-in aggregate functions of SQLite can be used as an aggregate window function" | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#aggwinfunc |
| row_number() | 1-based row number in the partition. "arbitrary order" without ORDER BY. Result: integer | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#builtins |
| rank() | row_number of the first peer (with gaps). 1 without ORDER BY | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | same |
| dense_rank() | Peer-group number without gaps. 1 without ORDER BY | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | same |
| percent_rank() | (rank-1)/(partition-rows-1). 0.0 for a one-row partition. REAL | "Despite the name, this function always returns a value between 0.0 and 1.0" | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | same |
| cume_dist() | row-number of the last peer / partition-rows. REAL | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | same |
| ntile(N) | "Argument N is handled as an integer". Groups as even as possible, larger groups first | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | N≤0 or non-integer: docs silent [I: error] | same |
| lag(expr[,offset[,default]]) | Value from `offset` rows before. offset "must be a non-negative integer". 0 means the current row. Missing row → default or NULL. Ignores the frame | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | Negative offset → error [I] | same |
| lead(expr[,offset[,default]]) | Same as lag, but rows after | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | same | same |
| first_value(expr) | Value at the first row of the frame. Respects the frame | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | same |
| last_value(expr) | Value at the last row of the frame | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | - | same |
| nth_value(expr,N) | Value at row N (1-based) of the frame. NULL if absent | - | 3.25.0 | - | - | N≤0: docs silent [I: error] | same |
| user-defined window aggregates (xStep/xFinal/xValue/xInverse) | Via sqlite3_create_window_function() | Calls non-window aggregates "legacy aggregate function" (terminology only) | 3.25.0 | - | C API | Errors from xFinal during reset/finalize are "silently discarded" | https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#udfwinfunc |

#### JSON functions (https://sqlite.org/json1.html)

The page says "By default, SQLite supports thirty functions and two operators". "All of the functions listed below have the SQLITE_INNOCUOUS and SQLITE_DETERMINISTIC flags." [V #overview]. Built in since 3.38.0. They can be removed with -DSQLITE_OMIT_JSON. Before that they were opt-in via -DSQLITE_ENABLE_JSON1 [V #howtocompile].

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| JSON argument handling | SQL numbers and NULL map to JSON numbers and null. Text is parsed. Malformed text "will usually throw an error". Exceptions: json_valid(), json_quote(), json_error_position(). JSON5 is accepted on input. Output is always canonical | - | JSON5 input 3.42.0 [V] | Store JSON in a TEXT column, or JSONB in a BLOB column. A `JSON` column type is not available in STRICT [I] | !SQLITE_OMIT_JSON | Error. The text message is not quoted on the page. The only quoted message is "malformed JSON" (for malformed JSONB) [V §3.2.2] | https://sqlite.org/json1.html |
| nesting limit | "more than 1000 levels of nesting is considered invalid" | - | Lowered 2000→1000 in 3.42.0 [V changes] | - | - | Invalid → error | https://sqlite.org/json1.html |
| PATH arguments | Must be well-formed: "$" then `.label` or `[N]`, `[#-N]`, `[#]` | - | "#-N" 3.31.0 [V changes] | - | - | "must be well-formed or else the function will throw an error" [V] | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jsonpath |
| VALUE arguments (subtype) | TEXT becomes a quoted JSON string, UNLESS it comes "directly from the result of another JSON function or from the -> operator (but not the ->> operator)". This uses the subtype mechanism: changes 3.9.0 says sqlite3_value_subtype()/sqlite3_result_subtype() are "used by the json1 extension" [V] | - | - | A value read from a table column has no subtype (only NULL/int/real/text/blob can be stored) [I from #3 "only able to store values that are NULL, integers, floating-point numbers, text, and BLOBs"] | - | - | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#varg |
| JSONB | Binary format stored as a BLOB. Accepted wherever text JSON is accepted | "intended for internal use by SQLite only. Applications should not use JSONB outside of SQLite nor try to reverse-engineer the JSONB format." | 3.45.0 [V] | - | - | Malformed JSONB: "might abort with a "malformed JSON" error", or return a correct or "goofy" answer. "might change from one version of SQLite to the next" | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jsonbx |
| JSON BLOB input bug | A non-JSONB BLOB that looks like text JSON is accepted as text JSON | "long-standing bug ... For backwards bug-compatibility ... officially supported" | Fixed accidentally in 3.45.0, restored in 3.45.1 [V] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jblobbug |
| json(X) | Validates and minifies. JSON5 → canonical | Duplicate labels: "it is undefined whether or not the duplicates are preserved. The current implementation preserves duplicates. However, future enhancements to this routine may choose to silently remove duplicates." | 3.9.0 (json1) | - | - | Not well-formed → "throws an error" | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jmini |
| jsonb(X) | Returns JSONB. A JSONB-looking BLOB input is copied ("deep structure ... not validated") | - | 3.45.0 | - | - | Invalid TEXT → error | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jminib |
| json_array(...) / jsonb_array(...) | 0 or more args → array | - | 3.9.0 / 3.45.0 | - | - | "If any argument to json_array() is a BLOB then an error is thrown." | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jarray |
| json_array_insert / jsonb_array_insert (json,path,value,...) | Inserts into an array at the path | - | 3.53.0 [V changes] | - | - | Path not ending in "[…]" → "an error is raised" | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jarrayins |
| json_array_length(X[,P]) | Array length, or 0 for a non-array. NULL if the path is not found | - | 3.9.0 | - | - | Malformed X or P → error | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jarraylen |
| json_error_position(X) | 0 if well-formed (JSON or JSON5), else the 1-based char position of the first error. For BLOBs, an approximate byte position | - | 3.42.0 [V] | - | - | Never errors (exception list) [V] | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jerr |
| json_extract(X,P1,...) / jsonb_extract | One path: SQL value (null→NULL, number, false→0, true→1, dequoted text, array/object → JSON text). Several paths → JSON array text. jsonb_ returns JSONB for array/object | "subtle incompatibility" with MySQL | 3.9.0 / 3.45.0 | - | - | Malformed → error | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jex |
| -> and ->> operators | -> returns JSON text (never JSONB) or NULL. ->> returns an SQL value. The RHS can be a path, a label X (= '$.X') or an integer N (≥0 → '$[N]', negative → '$[#-K]'). A text RHS that looks like an integer stays text (3.46.0) | "strives to be compatible with both MySQL and PostgreSQL" | 3.38.0. Negative N 3.47.0 [V] | - | Overridable as functions "->"() and "->>"() [V lang_expr] | - | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jptr |
| json_insert / json_replace / json_set (+ jsonb_) | Odd arg count. Edits run left to right. insert = create only, replace = overwrite only, set = both | - | 3.9.0 / 3.45.0 | - | - | "throw an error if the first JSON argument is not well-formed or if any PATH argument is not well-formed or if any argument is a BLOB" | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jins |
| json_object(label,value,...) / jsonb_object | Builds an object from pairs | "currently allows duplicate labels without complaint, though this might change in a future enhancement" | 3.9.0 / 3.45.0 | - | - | BLOB arg → error | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jobj |
| json_patch(T,P) / jsonb_patch | RFC-7396 MergePatch. Arrays are atomic | - | 3.18.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jpatch |
| json_pretty(X[,indent]) | Like json() with indentation. Default indent is 4 spaces | - | 3.46.0 [V] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jpretty |
| json_remove(X,P,...) / jsonb_remove | Removes paths left to right. A missing path is ignored. With no paths it minifies. json_remove(x,'$') → NULL | - | 3.9.0 | - | - | Malformed → error | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jrm |
| json_type(X[,P]) | 'null','true','false','integer','real','text','array','object'. NULL if the path is missing | - | 3.9.0 | - | - | Malformed → error | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jtype |
| json_valid(X[,Y]) | 1/0. Flag bits: 1=RFC-8259 text, 2=JSON5, 4=superficial JSONB, 8=strict JSONB. Default 1. NULL X or Y → NULL | "For backwards compatibility, the json_valid(X) function without a "flags" argument continues to report false for inputs that are not canonical JSON". "future versions ... might be enhanced to accept flag values outside of this range" | 3.9.0. Flags arg 3.45.0 [V] | - | - | "Any Y value less than 1 or greater than 15 raises an error" | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jvalid |
| json_quote(X) | SQL value → JSON. A value that is already JSON is a no-op. NULL → 'null' | - | 3.14 [V changes] | - | - | Never errors (exception list) | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jquote |
| json_group_array(X) / jsonb_group_array | Aggregate. Includes NULLs. Empty group → empty array | Order is arbitrary without aggregate ORDER BY [V aggfunc] | 3.10.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jgrouparray |
| json_group_object(N,V) / jsonb_group_object | Aggregate. Rows with NULL N are ignored. Empty group → {} | - | 3.10.0. NULL-label fix 3.50.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jgroupobject |
| json_each / json_tree / jsonb_each / jsonb_tree (TVF) | Columns key, value, type, atom, id, parent, fullkey, path, plus HIDDEN json and root | "id" "might change in future releases". Hidden columns: "you might not get reliable answers" | json_* 3.9.0. jsonb_* 3.51.0 [V] | The doc examples use `big.rowid`, which does not exist on WITHOUT ROWID tables [I]. The TVF schema declares columns `ANY` [V] | Table-valued functions / virtual tables | Malformed input → error [V "requires well-formed JSON"] | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jeach |

#### Function flags and C API (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html, https://sqlite.org/c3ref/create_function.html, https://sqlite.org/c3ref/value_subtype.html)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SQLITE_DETERMINISTIC (0x800) | "always gives the same output when the input parameters are the same". Required for partial-index WHERE and generated columns. Can be factored out of loops | - | 3.8.3 [V changes] | Required in generated columns [V] | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqlitedeterministic |
| SQLITE_DIRECTONLY | Only top-level SQL. Not allowed in VIEWs, TRIGGERs, CHECK, DEFAULT, index expressions, partial indexes or generated columns | "recommended for any application-defined SQL function that has side-effects" | 3.30.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqlitedirectonly |
| SQLITE_INNOCUOUS | No side effects. Needed under trusted_schema=OFF for functions in views, triggers and schema | "Developers are advised to avoid using the SQLITE_INNOCUOUS flag for application-defined functions unless ..." | 3.31.0 [V changes] | - | SQLITE_DBCONFIG_TRUSTED_SCHEMA / PRAGMA trusted_schema | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqliteinnocuous |
| SQLITE_SUBTYPE | The function reads argument subtypes. Without it, sqlite3_value_subtype() "might sometimes be zero" | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqlitesubtype |
| SQLITE_RESULT_SUBTYPE | The function sets a result subtype. Without it the call "might become a no-op if the function is used as a term in an expression index" | - | 3.45.0. SQLITE_STRICT_SUBTYPE=1 raises an error [V changes] | - | - | Error under SQLITE_STRICT_SUBTYPE=1 [V changes] | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqliteresultsubtype |
| SQLITE_SELFORDER1 | The aggregate orders its 1st argument internally. Enables the ordered-set syntax | - | - | - | SQLITE_ENABLE_ORDERED_SET_AGGREGATES | Ordered-set syntax on an unflagged function → "an error is raised" | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqliteselforder1 |
| sqlite3_create_function* / create_window_function | Register or override functions. Name ≤255 UTF-8 bytes. nArg -1 = variadic up to SQLITE_LIMIT_FUNCTION_ARG. "Built-in functions may be overloaded". An app function defaults to non-deterministic [V deterministic.html] | "If the third parameter is less than -1 or greater than 127 then the behavior is undefined." | - | - | - | A name that is too long → SQLITE_MISUSE [V] | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/create_function.html |
| sqlite3_value_subtype | Reads the subtype of an argument | - | 3.9.0 [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/value_subtype.html |

### 1.5 Statements and clauses

Convention: "chg 3.x.y" cites the release notes of that version at https://sqlite.org/changes.html. Syntax-diagram contents were read from the diagram labels on each page. [V-diagram]

#### SELECT: overall structure (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| select-stmt (whole) | [V] "The result of a SELECT is zero or more rows of data where each row has a fixed number of columns. A SELECT statement does not make any changes to the database." | - | [V] Since 1.0. | - | expr, result-column | - | lang_select.html#simpleselect |
| Diagram vs. real grammar | [V] "Note that there are paths through the syntax diagrams that are not allowed in practice." Examples: [V] "a simple SELECT that consists of just a VALUES clause cannot be preceded by a WITH clause" (it can when VALUES is the first element of a compound that uses WITH); [V] "The WITH clause must occur on the first SELECT of a compound SELECT. It cannot follow a compound-operator." | - | - | - | - | [I] Syntax error (SQLITE_ERROR) | lang_select.html (section 1) |
| Processing model | [V] Described as four steps (FROM, WHERE, GROUP BY/HAVING/result exprs, DISTINCT), "purely illustrative - in practice neither SQLite nor any other SQL engine is required to follow this or any other specific process." | - | - | - | - | - | lang_select.html#simpleselect |
| Aggregate vs. non-aggregate classification | [V] "A simple SELECT statement is an aggregate query if it contains either a GROUP BY clause or one or more aggregate functions in the result-set." | - | - | - | aggregate functions | - | lang_select.html#simpleselect |
| SELECT with no FROM | [V] "the input data is implicitly a single row zero columns wide (i.e. N=1 and M=0)." | - | [V] Old. chg 3.8.5 (3.8.5): "OFFSET clause ignored on queries without a FROM clause" was fixed as a bug. | - | - | - | lang_select.html#fromclause |
| ALL / DISTINCT | [V] When neither is given, the behaviour "is as if ALL were specified". For DISTINCT, [V] "values are compared using the IS DISTINCT FROM operator. Thus two NULL values are considered to be equal. An integer is equal to a floating point number if they represent the same quantity. Text values are compared using an appropriate collation sequence... BLOB affinity is used for DISTINCT comparisons, hence no type coercions occur." | - | [V] Old. | - | collation, comparison | - | lang_select.html#distinct |
| result-column: expr [ [AS] column-alias ] | [V] Diagram: `expr [AS column-alias]` \| `*` \| `table-name . *`. [I] AS is optional: chg 2.5.4 (2.5.4) says "Make the "AS" keyword optional again." | - | - | - | expr | - | lang_select.html#resultset |
| result-column: `*` | [V] "If a result expression is the special expression "*" then all columns in the input data are substituted for that one expression." | - | - | [I] WITHOUT ROWID has no rowid, so `*` never includes a rowid. `*` also skips the hidden rowid on rowid tables. | FROM | [V] "It is an error to use a "*" or "alias.*" expression in any context other than a result expression list. It is also an error to use a "*" or "alias.*" expression in a simple SELECT query that does not have a FROM clause." | lang_select.html#resultset |
| result-column: `table.*` | [V] "If the expression is the alias of a table or subquery in the FROM clause followed by ".*" then all columns from the named table or subquery are substituted". | - | - | - | FROM | Same as above [V]. | lang_select.html#resultset |
| Column count | [V] "The number of columns in the rows returned by a simple SELECT statement is equal to the number of expressions in the result expression list after substitution of * and alias.* expressions." | - | - | - | - | - | lang_select.html#resultset |
| Result column naming | [V] "The name of a result column is the value of the "AS" clause for that column, if there is an AS clause. If there is no AS clause then the name of the column is unspecified and may change from one release of SQLite to the next." | "unspecified and may change from one release of SQLite to the next" | Changes over time: [V] chg 3.6.1 (3.6.0): compound subquery result names "simplified to show only the name of the column of the original table and omit the table name"; [V] chg 3.3.5 (3.3.5): "Choose the column names in a compound query from the left-most SELECT instead of the right-most."; [V] chg 3.22.0 (3.22.0): a fix for "Inconsistent result set column names between CREATE TABLE AS and a simple SELECT". | - | - | - | c3ref/column_name.html |

#### SELECT: FROM clause and joins

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FROM single table | [V] "the input data used by the SELECT statement is the contents of the named table." | - | 1.0 | [I] No difference in what is visible, except that no rowid exists on WITHOUT ROWID tables. | schema/catalog | [I] "no such table: X" (SQLITE_ERROR). Not on these pages. | lang_select.html#fromclause |
| table-or-subquery: `[schema-name.]table-name [[AS] table-alias] [INDEXED BY idx \| NOT INDEXED]` | [V] Diagram tokens: `schema-name . table-name AS table-alias`, `INDEXED BY index-name`, `NOT INDEXED`. | - | - | - | naming, INDEXED BY | - | lang_select.html (table-or-subquery diagram) |
| table-or-subquery: `( select-stmt ) [[AS] alias]` | [V] A subquery in FROM "is handled as if it was a table containing the data returned by executing the subquery statement. Each column of the subquery has the collation sequence and affinity of the corresponding expression in the subquery statement." | - | [V] chg 2.4.3/3670 (2.4.3/2.5.0): subqueries in FROM were used and fixed early. | - | full SELECT | - | lang_select.html#fromclause |
| table-or-subquery: `( table-or-subquery )` / `( join-clause )` | [V] Present in the diagram. | - | - | - | joins | - | lang_select.html (diagram) |
| table-or-subquery: `table-function-name ( expr, ... ) [[AS] alias]` | [V] "A virtual table that contains hidden columns can be used like a table-valued function in the FROM clause. The arguments to the table-valued function become constraints on the HIDDEN columns of the virtual table." | - | - | - | virtual tables | - | lang_select.html#tabfunc1 |
| Cartesian product basis | [V] "All joins in SQLite are based on the cartesian product of the left and right-hand datasets. The columns of the cartesian product dataset are, in order, all the columns of the left-hand dataset followed by all the columns of the right-hand dataset." | - | - | - | - | - | lang_select.html#fromclause |
| Comma join `,` | [V] "There is no difference between the "INNER JOIN", "JOIN" and "," join operators. They are completely interchangeable in SQLite." | - | 1.0 | - | - | - | lang_select.html#crossjoin |
| JOIN / INNER JOIN | [V] Same as above. With no ON/USING it is the plain cartesian product. | - | [V] chg 2.5.0 (2.5.0): "Add support for the full SQL-92 join syntax and LEFT OUTER JOINs." | - | - | - | lang_select.html#fromclause |
| CROSS JOIN | [V] Same result as INNER JOIN, "but is handled differently by the query optimizer in that it prevents the query optimizer from reordering the tables in the join." | [V] "Avoid using CROSS JOIN except in specific situations where manual control of the query optimizer is desired. Avoid using CROSS JOIN early in the development of an application as doing so is a premature optimization. The special handling of CROSS JOIN is an SQLite-specific feature and is not a part of standard SQL." | [V] chg 3.2.6 (3.2.6): "Use of the CROSS keyword in a join turns off the table reordering optimization" | - | - | - | lang_select.html#crossjoin |
| LEFT [OUTER] JOIN | [V] "after the ON or USING filtering clauses have been applied, an extra row is added to the output for each row in the original left-hand input dataset that does not match any row in the right-hand dataset. The added rows contain NULL values in the columns that would normally contain values copied from the right-hand input dataset." | - | [V] 2.5.0 (chg 2.5.0) | - | NULL handling | - | lang_select.html#fromclause |
| RIGHT [OUTER] JOIN | [V] Mirror image of LEFT JOIN. | - | [V] 3.39.0 (2022-06-25), chg 3.39.0: "Add (long overdue) support for RIGHT and FULL OUTER JOIN." | - | LEFT JOIN | - | lang_select.html#rjoin |
| FULL [OUTER] JOIN | [V] "a combination of a "LEFT JOIN" and a "RIGHT JOIN". Extra rows of output are added for each row in left dataset that matches no rows in the right, and for each row in the right dataset that matches no rows in the left. Unmatched columns are filled in with NULL." | - | [V] 3.39.0, chg 3.39.0 | - | LEFT and RIGHT JOIN | - | lang_select.html#fulljoin |
| NATURAL (any join) | [V] "an implicit USING clause is added to the join-constraints. The implicit USING clause contains each of the column names that appear in both the left and right-hand input datasets. If the left and right-hand input datasets feature no common column names, then the NATURAL keyword has no effect on the results of the join." | - | [I] 2.5.0 (SQL-92 join syntax) | - | USING | [V] "A USING or ON clause may not be added to a join that specifies the NATURAL keyword." [I] Error text not given. | lang_select.html#fromclause |
| ON expr | [V] "the ON expression is evaluated for each row of the cartesian product as a boolean expression. Only rows for which the expression evaluates to true are included". | - | - | - | boolean evaluation | - | lang_select.html#fromclause |
| USING (col, ...) | [V] "each of the column names specified must exist in the datasets to both the left and right of the join-operator. For each pair of named columns, the expression "lhs.X = rhs.X" is evaluated..." Collation/affinity: the left column "is considered to be on the left-hand side of the comparison operator (=)". Output column merging: the left column is kept for INNER/LEFT, the right column for RIGHT, and for FULL "both columns are omitted and the coalescence of the two columns (the equivalent of "coalesce(lhs.X,rhs.X)") is used." | - | - | - | comparison/affinity/collation rules | [V] Column must exist on both sides (error). [I] Exact text not given. | lang_select.html#fromclause |
| ON vs WHERE on outer joins | [V] "In an outer join, the extra NULL rows for non-matched rows on the other operand are added after ON clause processing but before WHERE clause processing." | - | - | - | outer joins | - | lang_select.html#whereclause |
| Join evaluation order | [V] "(A join-op-1 B join-op-2 C) is computed as ((A join-op-1 B) join-op-2 C)." | - | - | - | - | - | lang_select.html#fromclause |
| Comma-join vs JOIN precedence | [V] "This is not the case in SQLite, where all joins have the same precedence." (In standard SQL, JOIN binds tighter than comma.) | Listed under "Deviations From Standard SQL" | - | - | - | - | lang_select.html#precedence_of_comma_joins_and_cross_join |
| "Strange JOIN names" | [V] "blah blah blah JOIN": 1 to 3 of CROSS, FULL, INNER, LEFT, NATURAL, OUTER, RIGHT "in any order"; "OUTER LEFT NATURAL JOIN" = "NATURAL LEFT OUTER JOIN"; "LEFT RIGHT JOIN" = "FULL JOIN"; "INNER OUTER JOIN" is disallowed. | [V] "you can use these non-standard join types but you ought not. Stick to using standard JOIN syntax for portability" | - | - | - | [V] Some combinations are "specifically disallowed". [I] Error text not given. | lang_select.html#strange_join_names |
| Flexible join syntax | [V] SQLite "will accept and process an ON or USING clause on a comma-join or CROSS JOIN, and will let you omit the ON or USING clause from any join at all". Only restrictions: no ON/USING on NATURAL, and not both ON and USING. An outer join without ON is "unconstrained (as if the ON clause where "ON true") which make the outer join behave like an inner join." | Deviation from standard SQL | - | - | - | [V] The two restrictions above are errors. | lang_select.html#flexible_join_syntax |

#### SELECT: WHERE, GROUP BY, HAVING, WINDOW, bare columns

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WHERE | [V] "Rows are excluded from the result if the WHERE clause evaluates to either false or NULL." | - | 1.0 | - | boolean evaluation | - | lang_select.html#whereclause |
| Result alias visible in WHERE | [V] chg 2.2.4 (2.2.4): "The label to the right of an AS in the column list of a SELECT can now be used as part of an expression in the WHERE, ORDER BY, GROUP BY, and/or HAVING clauses." [V] chg 3.7.17 (3.7.17): "Only consider AS names from the result set as candidates for resolving identifiers in the WHERE clause if there are no other matches. In the ORDER BY clause, AS names take priority over any column names." | Not on lang_select. Only in changes. | 2.2.4; refined 3.7.17 | - | name resolution | - | changes.html |
| GROUP BY expr, ... | [V] Each GROUP BY expression "is evaluated for each row of the dataset according to the processing rules stated below for ORDER BY expressions" (so integer K and output-alias forms apply [I]). "For the purposes of grouping rows, NULL values are considered equal." "The usual rules for selecting a collation sequence ... apply". GROUP BY expressions "do not have to be expressions that appear in the result" and "may not be aggregate expressions". | - | [V] 1.0 (chg 1.0). [V] chg 3.2.7 (3.2.7): "GROUP BY now considers NULLs to be equal again, as it should". | - | ORDER BY term resolution, collation | [V] An aggregate in GROUP BY is an error. [I] Text not given. | lang_select.html#resultset |
| GROUP BY alias binding | [V] chg 3.8.0 (3.8.0): "Bare identifiers in ORDER BY clauses bind more tightly to output column names, but identifiers in expressions bind more tightly to input column names. Identifiers in GROUP BY clauses always prefer output column names, however." | Only in changes | 3.8.0 | - | name resolution | - | changes.html |
| HAVING expr | [V] "evaluated once for each group of rows as a boolean expression. If the result of evaluating the HAVING clause is false, the group is discarded." A non-aggregate HAVING "is evaluated with respect to an arbitrarily selected row from the group". It "may refer to values, even aggregate functions, that are not in the result." | - | [V] 1.0. [V] chg 3.39.0 (3.39.0): "The HAVING clause of a SELECT statement is now allowed on any aggregate query, even queries that do not have a GROUP BY clause." | - | aggregates | [I] HAVING on a non-aggregate query is an error. Text not given. | lang_select.html#resultset |
| Aggregate with no GROUP BY | [V] "always returns exactly one row of data, even if there are zero rows of input data." Non-aggregate expressions are evaluated "for an arbitrarily selected row" or, with zero rows, "against a row consisting entirely of NULL values." | - | - | - | aggregates | - | lang_select.html#resultset |
| WINDOW window-name AS window-defn, ... | [V] In the select-core diagram, after HAVING. window-defn tokens: `( [base-window-name] [PARTITION BY expr,...] [ORDER BY ordering-term,...] [frame-spec] )`. | - | [V] Window functions: 3.25.0 (chg 3.25.0). [V] "window chaining" (base-window-name): 3.28.0 (chg 3.28.0). [I] The WINDOW clause probably arrived with window functions in 3.25.0; https://sqlite.org/changes.html has no separate line for it. | - | window functions (separate page) | - | lang_select.html (select-core diagram) |
| Bare columns in aggregate query | [V] A bare column "will be the value for "b" in one of the input rows that form the aggregate." "you usually do not know which input row is used ... in many cases the value for "b" is undefined." | [V] "The ability to include bare columns in a query is an SQLite-specific extension. This is considered a feature, not a bug." | - | - | aggregates | [V] None (allowed) | lang_select.html#bareagg |
| Bare columns with min()/max() | [V] "If there is exactly one min() or max() aggregate in the query, then all bare columns in the result set take values from an input row which also contains the minimum or maximum." Limitations: ties are "arbitrary" and "might be different for different bare columns within the same query"; with two or more min/max the choice is arbitrary; this works "only ... for the built-in implementation of those aggregates." | Same as above | - | - | min/max aggregates | - | lang_select.html#bareagg |
| Bare columns over empty aggregate | [V] "If the aggregate is empty ... then the bare column can take on any arbitrary value, including values that are not found anywhere in the tables of the FROM clause." | - | - | - | - | - | lang_select.html#bareagg (2.5.1 Notes) |

#### SELECT: compound, ORDER BY, LIMIT, VALUES

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| UNION ALL | [V] Returns all rows from both sides. | - | [V] 1.0 (chg 1.0) | - | - | [V] "all the constituent SELECTs must return the same number of result columns." [I] Text: "SELECTs to the left and right of UNION ALL do not have the same number of result columns". Not in these docs. | lang_select.html#compound |
| UNION | [V] Same as UNION ALL, but "duplicate rows are removed from the final result set." | - | 1.0 | - | duplicate detection | Same | lang_select.html#compound |
| INTERSECT | [V] "returns the intersection of the results of the left and right SELECTs." Duplicates removed. | - | 1.0 | - | - | Same | lang_select.html#compound |
| EXCEPT | [V] "returns the subset of rows returned by the left SELECT that are not also returned by the right-hand SELECT." Duplicates removed. | - | 1.0 | - | - | Same | lang_select.html#compound |
| Compound duplicate rules | [V] "NULL values are considered equal to other NULL values and distinct from all non-NULL values." Collation is chosen "as if the columns of the left and right-hand SELECT statements were the left and right-hand operands of the equals (=) operator, except that greater precedence is not assigned to a collation sequence specified with the postfix COLLATE operator. No affinity transformations are applied". | - | - | - | collation rules | - | lang_select.html#compound |
| Compound associativity | [V] "(A op B op C) is processed as ((A op B) op C)." (No precedence for INTERSECT over UNION.) | - | - | - | - | - | lang_select.html#compound |
| Compound members may not have ORDER BY/LIMIT | [V] "As the components of a compound SELECT must be simple SELECT statements, they may not contain ORDER BY or LIMIT clauses." | - | - | - | - | [I] Error, e.g. "ORDER BY clause should come after UNION not before". Text not in docs. | lang_select.html#compound |
| ORDER BY | [V] Without ORDER BY, the order of a multi-row result "is undefined". Ties: "The order in which two rows for which all ORDER BY expressions evaluate to equal values are returned is undefined." The default is ASC. | - | 1.0 | - | comparison | - | lang_select.html#orderby |
| ORDER BY ASC / DESC | [V] "ASC (smaller values are returned first) or DESC (larger values are returned first)". | - | - | - | - | - | lang_select.html#orderby |
| NULL order default | [V] "SQLite considers NULL values to be smaller than any other values for sorting purposes. Hence, NULLs naturally appear at the beginning of an ASC order-by and at the end of a DESC order-by." | - | - | - | - | - | lang_select.html#nullslast |
| NULLS FIRST / NULLS LAST | [V] ordering-term: `expr [COLLATE collation-name] [ASC\|DESC] [NULLS FIRST\|NULLS LAST]`. | - | [V] 3.30.0 (2019-10-04), chg 3.30.0 | - | ordering-term | - | lang_select.html#nullslast |
| ORDER BY integer K | [V] "If the ORDER BY expression is a constant integer K then the expression is considered an alias for the K-th column of the result set (columns are numbered from left to right starting with 1)." | - | - | - | - | [I] Out-of-range K is an error ("1st ORDER BY term out of range - should be between 1 and N"). Not in these docs. | lang_select.html#orderby |
| ORDER BY output alias | [V] "If the ORDER BY expression is an identifier that corresponds to the alias of one of the output columns, then the expression is considered an alias for that column." [V] chg 3.5.4 (3.5.4): prefer the result alias over a table column of the same name. | - | - | - | name resolution | - | lang_select.html#orderby |
| ORDER BY arbitrary expr | [V] Allowed in a simple SELECT. In a compound SELECT, terms "that are not aliases to output columns must be exactly the same as an expression used as an output column." | - | - | - | - | [V] Compound: "If no matching expression can be found in the result columns of any constituent SELECT, it is an error." | lang_select.html#orderby |
| ORDER BY collation choice | [V] In order: (1) explicit postfix COLLATE on the term; (2) COLLATE on the aliased expression; (3) "if the ORDER BY expression is a column or an alias of an expression that is a column, then the default collation sequence for the column is used"; (4) "Otherwise, the BINARY collation sequence is used." | - | - | - | collation | - | lang_select.html#orderby |
| ORDER BY on compound | [V] "only the last or right-most simple SELECT may have an ORDER BY clause. That ORDER BY clause will apply across all elements of the compound." The left-most SELECT is searched first for alias/expression matches, then the next to the right. Each term is matched separately. | - | - | - | - | [V] Not allowed if the right-most element is VALUES. | lang_select.html#orderby |
| LIMIT expr | [V] "Any scalar expression may be used in the LIMIT clause, so long as it evaluates to an integer or a value that can be losslessly converted to an integer." A negative value means "no upper bound". | - | [V] 2.1.0 (chg 2.1.0: "Implement the "LIMIT ... OFFSET ..." clause"); [V] 3.1.2 (chg 3.1.2): "Allow "?" parameters in the LIMIT clause." | - | expr eval, integer conversion | [V] "If the expression evaluates to a NULL value or any other value that cannot be losslessly converted to an integer, an error is returned." [I] Likely SQLITE_MISMATCH "datatype mismatch". Must check against the oracle. | lang_select.html#limitoffset |
| OFFSET expr | [V] It must also be an integer or losslessly convertible. Skips the first M rows. "If the OFFSET clause evaluates to a negative value, the results are the same as if it had evaluated to zero." | - | 2.1.0 | - | LIMIT | Same as LIMIT [V] | lang_select.html#limitoffset |
| `LIMIT x, y` comma form | [V] "the first expression is used as the OFFSET expression and the second as the LIMIT expression. This is counter-intuitive..." | [V] "This reversal of the offset and limit is intentional - it maximizes compatibility with other SQL database systems. However, to avoid confusion, programmers are strongly encouraged to use the form of the LIMIT clause that uses the "OFFSET" keyword and avoid using a LIMIT clause with a comma-separated offset." | - | - | LIMIT/OFFSET | - | lang_select.html#limitoffset |
| LIMIT on compound | [V] "only the last or right-most simple SELECT may contain a LIMIT clause ... the LIMIT clause applies to the entire compound". | - | [V] chg 2.8.5 (2.8.5): "Make LIMIT work on a compound SELECT statement." | - | - | [V] "If the right-most simple SELECT is a VALUES clause then no LIMIT clause is allowed." | lang_select.html#limitoffset |
| VALUES (row), (row), ... | [V] ""VALUES(expr-list)" means the same thing as "SELECT expr-list"". Multi-row means "SELECT ... UNION ALL ... UNION ALL SELECT ...", "except that the number of SELECT statements in a compound is limited by SQLITE_LIMIT_COMPOUND_SELECT whereas the number of rows in a VALUES clause has no arbitrary limit." | - | [V] Multi-row INSERT VALUES: 3.7.11 (chg 3.7.11). [V] "Allow a VALUES clause to be used anywhere a SELECT statement is valid": 3.8.3 (chg 3.8.3). Can prefix SELECT, INSERT, DELETE, UPDATE. | - | [V] 3.8.3 (2014-02-03), chg 3.8.3: "Added support for common table expressions and the WITH clause." | - | SELECT | - | lang_with.html |

| Recursive CTE | [V] Rules: the body must be a compound; at least one recursive and one non-recursive SELECT; all non-recursive ones first; recursive ones separated by UNION or UNION ALL, all with "the same operator"; [V] "Recursive SELECT statements may not use aggregate functions or window functions." The recursive table "must appear exactly once in the FROM clause of each top-level SELECT statement in the recursive-select and must not appear anywhere else ... including subqueries." | - | [V] 3.8.3. Not in docs. | lang_with.html#recursivecte |

OFFSET N: "prevents the first N rows from being added", but they "are still processed by the recursive-select". ORDER BY sets the queue extraction order. | [V] Without ORDER BY "the queue becomes a FIFO ... but applications should not depend on that fact since it might change." | - | - | - | - | lang_with.html#recursivecte |
| UNION vs UNION ALL in recursion | [V] With UNION, "only add rows to the queue if no identical row has been previously added to the queue. NULLs compare equal for this. | - | - | - | duplicate detection | - | lang_with.html#recursivecte |

Do not use the MATERIALIZED or NOT MATERIALIZED keywords on a common table expression unless you have a compelling reason to do so." | [V] 3.35.0 (2021-03-12), page + chg 3.35.0. chg 3.35.0 also says: "The default behavior was formerly NOT MATERIALIZED, but is now changed to MATERIALIZED for CTEs that are used more than once." | - | CTE | - | lang_with.html#materialization_hints |

[WHERE expr] )`, repeatable. | [V] "UPSERT is not standard SQL. UPSERT in SQLite follows the syntax established by PostgreSQL, with generalizations." | [V] 3.24.0 (2018-06-04) (page + chg 3.24.0) | [I] The WITHOUT ROWID PK is a uniqueness constraint and can be a conflict target. | INSERT, unique constraints | - | lang_upsert.html |

Not in docs. | lang_upsert.html |
| Clause selection | [V] "The ON CONFLICT clauses are checked in the order specified." "Only a single ON CONFLICT clause, specifically the first ON CONFLICT clause with a matching conflict target, may run for each row of the INSERT." Multi-row: "the upsert decision is made separately for each row". | - | 3.35.0 | - | - | - | lang_upsert.html |
| Scope: uniqueness only | [V] "UPSERT does not intervene for failed NOT NULL, CHECK, or foreign key constraints or for constraints that are implemented using triggers." | - | - | [I] STRICT type errors are not uniqueness errors, so UPSERT does not catch them. | - | Those still raise SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_* [I] | lang_upsert.html |
| DO NOTHING | [V] The insert is omitted. | - | 3.24.0 | - | - | - | lang_upsert.html |

The WHERE's "only use ... is to optionally change the DO UPDATE into a no-op". The target may be referred to unqualified or as `table.col` ("PostgreSQL requires the second form, but SQLite accepts either"). | - | 3.24.0 | - | UPDATE semantics | - | lang_upsert.html |
| `excluded.` qualifier | [V] Refers to "the value that would have been inserted had the constraint not failed". | - | 3.24.0 | - | - | - | lang_upsert.html |
| Parsing ambiguity with INSERT ... SELECT | [V] "The parser might not be able to tell if the "ON" keyword is introducing the UPSERT or if it is the ON clause of a join. | DO UPDATE conflict algorithm | [V] "always ABORT ... If the DO UPDATE clause encounters any constraint violation, the entire INSERT statement rolls back and halts. | UPDATE ... ORDER BY / LIMIT / OFFSET | [V] Only "If SQLite is built with the SQLITE_ENABLE_UPDATE_DELETE_LIMIT compile-time option". LIMIT is cast to an integer; a negative value means "no limit". #### ON CONFLICT clause (https://sqlite.org/lang_conflict.html)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| conflict-clause | [V] `ON CONFLICT (ROLLBACK\|ABORT\|FAIL\|IGNORE\|REPLACE)` on constraints in CREATE TABLE. For INSERT/UPDATE it is written `OR <alg>`. "allowed on UNIQUE, NOT NULL, and PRIMARY KEY constraints only ... not allowed on CHECK and FOREIGN KEY constraints." | [V] "a non-standard extension specific to SQLite"; "Do not confuse these two separate uses of the "ON CONFLICT" phrase." | [V] "since before version 3.0.0 (2004-06-18)" | [I] The STRICT datatype check is not a UNIQUE/NOT NULL/PK constraint, so presumably OR IGNORE etc. do not apply to it. Check with the oracle. | constraints | - | lang_conflict.html |
| ABORT (default) | [V] "aborts the current SQL statement with an SQLITE_CONSTRAINT error and backs out any changes made by the current SQL statement; but changes caused by prior SQL statements within the same transaction are preserved and the transaction remains active. This is the default behavior and the behavior specified by the SQL standard." | - | - | - | statement journal | SQLITE_CONSTRAINT [V] | lang_conflict.html |
| ROLLBACK | [V] "aborts the current SQL statement with an SQLITE_CONSTRAINT error and rolls back the current transaction. If no transaction is active (other than the implied transaction ...) then ... works the same as the ABORT algorithm." | - | - | - | transactions | SQLITE_CONSTRAINT [V] | lang_conflict.html |
| FAIL | [V] Aborts "with an SQLITE_CONSTRAINT error. But the FAIL resolution does not back out prior changes of the SQL statement that failed nor does it end the transaction." "The FAIL behavior only works for uniqueness, NOT NULL, and CHECK constraints. A foreign key constraint violation causes an ABORT." | - | - | - | - | SQLITE_CONSTRAINT [V] | lang_conflict.html |
| IGNORE | [V] "skips the one row that contains the constraint violation and continues processing subsequent rows". "No error is returned for uniqueness, NOT NULL, and UNIQUE constraint errors". "However, the IGNORE conflict resolution algorithm works like ABORT for foreign key constraint errors." | - | - | - | - | none, or SQLITE_CONSTRAINT for FK [V] | lang_conflict.html |
| REPLACE | [V] On UNIQUE/PK violation it "deletes pre-existing rows that are causing the constraint violation prior to inserting or updating the current row". On a NOT NULL violation it "replaces the NULL value with the default value for that column, or if the column has no default value, then the ABORT algorithm is used." CHECK or FK violation: "works like ABORT". | [V] "The exceptional behaviors defined in this paragraph might change in a future release." (applies to the hook/change-counter paragraph) | - | [V] chg 3.16.2 (3.16.2): "Fix the REPLACE statement for WITHOUT ROWID tables that lack secondary indexes so that it works correctly with triggers and foreign keys." | defaults, deletes | SQLITE_CONSTRAINT for CHECK/FK/NOT NULL-no-default [V] | lang_conflict.html |
| REPLACE and delete triggers | [V] "When the REPLACE conflict resolution strategy deletes rows in order to satisfy a constraint, delete triggers fire if and only if recursive triggers are enabled." | - | [V] 3.6.18 (chg 3.6.18) | - | PRAGMA recursive_triggers | - | lang_conflict.html |
| REPLACE side effects on hooks/counters | [V] "The update hook is not invoked for rows that are deleted by the REPLACE conflict resolution strategy. Nor does REPLACE increment the change counter." | [V] "might change in a future release" | - | - | changes() | - | lang_conflict.html |
| REPLACE and FK actions | [I] The pages read do not say whether rows deleted by REPLACE run ON DELETE FK actions or FK checks. They say only that a FK violation by the new row works like ABORT, and that changes() does not count "foreign key actions or REPLACE constraint resolution". Check foreignkeys.html or the oracle. | - | - | - | FKs | - | lang_conflict.html, c3ref/changes.html |
| Precedence | [V] "The algorithm specified in the OR clause of an INSERT or UPDATE overrides any algorithm specified in a CREATE TABLE. If no algorithm is specified anywhere, the ABORT algorithm is used." | - | [V] chg 2.8.1 (2.8.1): an old ON CONFLICT on BEGIN statements (historical) | - | - | - | lang_conflict.html |

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| returning-clause | [V] `RETURNING (expr [[AS] column-alias] \| *), ...` on top-level DELETE, INSERT, UPDATE. One row "for each database row that is deleted, inserted, or updated." | [V] "RETURNING is not standard SQL. It is an extension. SQLite's syntax for RETURNING is modelled after PostgreSQL." | [V] 3.35.0 (2021-03-12) (page + chg 3.35.0). [V] 3.35.4 (chg 3.35.4): an unknown table in RETURNING now raises an error "instead of silently ignoring that error". | - | DML, expr | - | lang_returning.html |
| `*` in RETURNING | [V] "expands into a list of all non-hidden columns of the table being deleted, inserted, or updated." | - | - | - | - | - | lang_returning.html#details |
| Values seen | [V] INSERT/UPDATE: the value "after the change has been applied". DELETE: "the value before the delete occurs". Triggers: "do not reflect any subsequent value changes made by triggers". | - | - | - | - | - | lang_returning.html#details, #limitations_and_caveats |
| Rows reported | [V] "only returns rows that are directly modified ... does not report any additional database changes caused by foreign key constraints or triggers." "A RETURNING clause for an UPSERT reports both inserted and updated rows." | - | - | - | UPSERT | - | lang_returning.html#details |
| Processing order | [V] "all of the database changes occur during the first invocation of sqlite3_step()" and the output is "embargoed until after all database modification actions are completed." The order is "arbitrary". | - | - | - | - | - | lang_returning.html#processing_order |
| Self-referential subqueries | [V] A subquery on the modified table "might depend on unspecified behavior and hence could vary from one invocation of the query to the next." | "Indeterminate" | - | - | - | - | lang_returning.html#self_referential_subqueries_are_indeterminate |
| Limitations | [V] (1) Not available on DELETE/UPDATE of virtual tables ("might be removed in future versions"). (2) Top-level only; not in triggers. (3) "cannot be used as a subquery" (no CTE use like PostgreSQL). (4) Order is arbitrary, and ORDER BY on DELETE/UPDATE does not constrain it. (5) Pre-trigger values. (6) "may not contain top-level aggregate functions or window functions" (subqueries may). (7) "may only reference the table being modified. In an UPDATE FROM statement, the auxiliary tables named in the FROM clause may not participate in the RETURNING clause." | "might be added in a future release" (CTE use) | - | - | - | error [I] for (2),(3),(6),(7) | lang_returning.html#limitations_and_caveats |
