It may also give SQLITE_BUSY because of other readers; then "the transaction remains active". [I] COMMIT with no transaction is an error ("cannot commit - no transaction is active"), not in these docs. | lang_transaction.html#implicit_versus_explicit_transactions |
| rollback-stmt | [V] `ROLLBACK [TRANSACTION] [TO [SAVEPOINT] savepoint-name]` | - | - | - | - | [V] Pending statements "will often be aborted, causing them to return an SQLITE_ABORT or SQLITE_ABORT_ROLLBACK error"; since 3.8.8 "a pending read will continue functioning after the ROLLBACK as long as the ROLLBACK does not modify the database schema." [V] A ROLLBACK after an automatic rollback "will fail with an error, but no harm is caused by this." | lang_transaction.html |
| Autocommit / implicit transactions | [V] "Any command that accesses the database (basically, any SQL command, except a few PRAGMA statements) will automatically start a transaction if one is not already in effect." It is committed "when the last active statement finishes". "The only way to ensure that a statement has "finished" is to invoke sqlite3_reset() or sqlite3_finalize()". | - | - | - | - | - | lang_transaction.html#implicit_versus_explicit_transactions |
| No nesting with BEGIN | [V] "Transactions created using BEGIN...COMMIT do not nest. For nested transactions, use the SAVEPOINT and RELEASE commands." | - | - | - | - | see BEGIN | lang_transaction.html |
| Read vs write transactions | [V] A read transaction is "started by a SELECT statement"; a write transaction by "CREATE, DELETE, DROP, INSERT, or UPDATE". Upgrade fails "with SQLITE_BUSY" if another connection has modified or is modifying the database. | - | - | - | locking | SQLITE_BUSY [V] | lang_transaction.html#read_transactions_versus_write_transactions |
| DEFERRED (default) | [V] "the transaction does not actually start until the database is first accessed ... BEGIN DEFERRED statement merely sets a flag on the database connection that turns off the automatic commit". | - | - | - | - | SQLITE_BUSY on later upgrade [V] | lang_transaction.html#immediate |
| IMMEDIATE | [V] "start a new write immediately ... might fail with SQLITE_BUSY if another write transaction is already active". isolation: "If the BEGIN IMMEDIATE operation succeeds, then no subsequent operations in that transaction will ever fail with an SQLITE_BUSY error." | - | [V] chg 3.34.0 (3.34.0): works with read-only attached databases. | - | - | SQLITE_BUSY [V] | lang_transaction.html#immediate; isolation.html |
| EXCLUSIVE | [V] "EXCLUSIVE and IMMEDIATE are the same in WAL mode, but in other journaling modes, EXCLUSIVE prevents other database connections from reading the database while the transaction is underway." | - | - | - | journal_mode | SQLITE_BUSY [I] | lang_transaction.html#immediate |
| Errors that may auto-rollback | [V] SQLITE_FULL, SQLITE_IOERR, SQLITE_INTERRUPT, SQLITE_NOMEM. SQLite "attempts to undo just the one statement ... However ... it might be necessary for SQLite to rollback and cancel the entire transaction." | [V] "Future versions of SQLite may extend the list... might change the error response." | - | - | - | listed codes [V] | lang_transaction.html#response_to_errors_within_a_transaction |
| journal_mode=OFF | [V] "the behavior of the ROLLBACK command is undefined." | "undefined" | - | - | PRAGMA journal_mode | - | lang_transaction.html |
| SAVEPOINT name | [V] `SAVEPOINT savepoint-name`. "The transaction names need not be unique." Outside BEGIN it behaves "the same as BEGIN DEFERRED TRANSACTION". | - | [V] chg 3.6.8 (3.6.8): "Added support for nested transactions" [I] (savepoints) | - | transactions | - | lang_savepoint.html |
| RELEASE [SAVEPOINT] name | [V] It removes all savepoints "back to and including the most recent savepoint with a matching name". When this empties the stack, "RELEASE is the same as COMMIT". An inner RELEASE writes nothing to disk. | - | 3.6.8 [I] | - | - | [V] "If the savepoint-name in a RELEASE command does not match any savepoint currently in the transaction stack, then no savepoints are released, the database is unchanged, and the RELEASE command returns an error." [I] Text "no such savepoint: X". | lang_savepoint.html |
| ROLLBACK TO [SAVEPOINT] name | [V] "does not cancel the transaction. Instead of cancelling the transaction, the ROLLBACK TO command restarts the transaction again at the beginning. All intervening SAVEPOINTs are canceled". "The SAVEPOINT with the matching name remains on the transaction stack". | - | 3.6.8 [I] | - | - | [V] A non-matching name "fails with an error and leaves the state of the database unchanged." | lang_savepoint.html#transaction_nesting_rules |
| COMMIT within savepoints | [V] "The COMMIT command may be used to release all savepoints and commit the transaction even if the transaction was originally started by a SAVEPOINT command". Plain ROLLBACK "rolls backs all transactions and leaves the transaction stack empty." | - | - | - | - | - | lang_savepoint.html |
| Isolation between connections | [V] "Except in the case of shared cache database connections with PRAGMA read_uncommitted turned on, all transactions in SQLite show "serializable" isolation." In WAL mode there is "snapshot isolation" for readers. An upgrade from a stale snapshot "fails with an SQLITE_BUSY_SNAPSHOT error". | - | WAL: 3.7.0 [V] | - | journal modes | SQLITE_BUSY_SNAPSHOT [V] | isolation.html |
| Same-connection visibility | [V] "there is no isolation between operations that occur within the same database connection." Changes made while a SELECT is running: "this behavior is undefined". | "undefined" | - | - | - | - | isolation.html |

#### ATTACH / DETACH / naming (https://sqlite.org/lang_attach.html, https://sqlite.org/lang_detach.html, https://sqlite.org/lang_naming.html)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ATTACH [DATABASE] expr AS schema-name | [V] The filename is an expression, with sqlite3_open() semantics. ":memory:" gives an in-memory database and "an empty string results in a new temporary database". URI filenames only if enabled: "The default behavior is for URI filenames to be disabled, however that might change in a future release". | "might change in a future release" | [V] 2.8.1 (chg 2.8.1). [V] Arbitrary expressions as filename: 3.8.3 (chg 3.8.3). [V] Allowed inside a transaction since 3.21.0 (chg 3.21.0). | - | VFS, file layer | [V] Limit SQLITE_LIMIT_ATTACHED. [V] In shared cache mode, attaching the same file twice "results in an error" (detach page). | lang_attach.html |
| Reserved schema names main / temp | [V] "The schema-names 'main' and 'temp' refer to the main database and the database used for temporary tables. The main and temp databases cannot be attached or detached." | - | - | - | - | error [I] | lang_attach.html |
| Unqualified name resolution | [V] lang_naming: "The temp database is searched first, followed by the main database, followed by all attached databases in the order that they were attached. The reference resolves to the first match found." lang_attach: with duplicates, "the table chosen is the one in the database that was least recently attached." | - | - | - | - | - | lang_naming.html; lang_attach.html |
| Qualified name | [V] The schema "must be either "main", or "temp" or the schema-name of an attached database. Like other SQL identifiers, schema names are case-insensitive. If a schema name is specified, then only that one schema is searched". | - | - | - | - | error if unknown [I] ("unknown database X") | lang_naming.html |
| Object type filtering | [V] "objects of types that cannot be used in the context of the reference are always ignored." REINDEX may resolve to more than one type. | - | - | - | - | - | lang_naming.html |
| Multi-db atomicity | [V] "atomic, assuming that the main database is not ":memory:" and the journal_mode is not WAL." Otherwise atomic only per file. | - | - | - | journaling | - | lang_attach.html |
| DETACH [DATABASE] schema-name | [V] Detaches by name. Outside shared cache mode the same file can be attached several times under different names. | - | 2.8.1 | - | ATTACH | [I] Error for main/temp/unknown names. | lang_detach.html |

#### VACUUM / ANALYZE / REINDEX / EXPLAIN (https://sqlite.org/lang_vacuum.html etc.)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VACUUM [schema-name] | [V] "rebuilds the database file, repacking it into a minimal amount of disk space." It works on main by default, or on a named attached schema. | [V] "Compatibility Warning: The ability to vacuum attached databases was added in version 3.15.0 (2016-10-14). Prior to that, a schema-name added to the VACUUM statement would be silently ignored and the "main" schema would be vacuumed." | [V] 1.0 (chg 1.0), reactivated 2.8.1 (chg 2.8.1). Schema name: 3.15.0. | [V] "The VACUUM command may change the ROWIDs of entries in any tables that do not have an explicit INTEGER PRIMARY KEY." [I] So it has no visible effect on WITHOUT ROWID tables. | storage engine | [V] "A VACUUM will fail if there is an open transaction on the database connection". VACUUM (not INTO) is a write, so it fails if another connection blocks writes. [I] Text "cannot VACUUM from within a transaction". | lang_vacuum.html#howvacuumworks |
| VACUUM [schema] INTO filename-expr | [V] It writes a vacuumed copy. "The file named by the INTO clause must not previously exist, or else it must be an empty file, or the VACUUM INTO command will fail with an error." It gives "a consistent snapshot". | - | [V] 3.27.0 (chg 3.27.0). [V] Read-only dbs: 3.28.0 (chg 3.28.0). [V] Honors PRAGMA synchronous: 3.40.0 (chg 3.40.0). [V] reserve=N URI param: 3.53.0 (chg 3.53.0). | - | file layer | error if the target exists and is non-empty [V] | lang_vacuum.html#vacuuminto |
| ANALYZE [schema \| table-or-index \| schema.table-or-index] | [V] It gathers statistics into "sqlite_stat1" (plus sqlite_stat4 with SQLITE_ENABLE_STAT4). With no arguments, main and all attached databases. "The use of ANALYZE is never required." | [V] "all recent versions of SQLite ignore the sqlite_stat2 and sqlite_stat3 tables" | [V] 3.2.3 (chg 3.2.3). [V] Index-name argument: 3.7.6 (chg 3.7.6). [V] analysis_limit: 3.32.0 (chg 3.32.0). | [V] chg 3.8.6 (3.8.6): stat4 samples fixed for WITHOUT ROWID. | stat tables, planner | - | lang_analyze.html |
| Statistics tables via SQL | [V] They "can be queried using SELECT and can be changed using the DELETE, INSERT, and UPDATE commands. The DROP TABLE command works on statistics tables as of SQLite version 3.7.9 ... The ALTER TABLE command does not work on statistics tables." "ANALYZE sqlite_schema" forces a reload. | [V] "one should not modify the content of the statistics tables by any mechanism other than invoking the ANALYZE command" | - | - | - | - | lang_analyze.html#statanal (section 3) |
| PRAGMA optimize / analysis_limit (as ANALYZE drivers) | [V] See section D. | [V] analysis_limit default 0 "for backwards compatibility"; its details "are subject to change in future versions of SQLite." | 3.18.0 / 3.46.0 / 3.32.0 [V] | - | - | - | lang_analyze.html#pragopt, #approx |
| REINDEX [collation \| [schema.]table-or-index \| EXPRESSIONS] | [V] "delete and recreate indexes from scratch". With no argument, every index in every attached db. With a collation name, the indexes that use it. With a table, its indexes. With an index, that index. EXPRESSIONS: every index on expressions. | [V] "For a command of the form "REINDEX name", a match against collation-name takes precedence over a match against index-name or table-name. This ambiguity in the syntax may be avoided by always specifying a schema-name". | [V] 3.1.0 (chg 2.8.16). [V] EXPRESSIONS: 3.53.0 (2026-04-09) (page + chg 3.53.0). | - | indexes, collations | [I] Error for an unknown name. | lang_reindex.html |
| EXPLAIN stmt | [V] Returns "the sequence of virtual machine instructions it would have used". | [V] "The details of the output format are subject to change from one release of SQLite to the next. Applications should not use EXPLAIN or EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN since their exact behavior is variable and only partially documented." | - | - | VDBE | - | lang_explain.html |
| EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN stmt | [V] Returns "high-level information regarding the query plan". | Same as EXPLAIN. [V] chg 3.2.6 (3.2.6): "Added the experimental and undocumented EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN capability". Its format changed in 3.24.0, 3.36.0, 3.48.0 (chg 3.24.0, L831, L324). | 3.2.6 | - | planner | - | lang_explain.html |
| EXPLAIN is run-time | [V] "actions that occur during sqlite3_prepare() are unaffected by EXPLAIN". Some PRAGMAs act at prepare time; "For consistent results, avoid using EXPLAIN on PRAGMA statements." "The authorizer callback is invoked regardless of the presence of EXPLAIN". | - | - | - | - | - | lang_explain.html#explain_operates_at_run_time_not_at_prepare_time |

#### Lexical: comments, keywords, quoting; statement list (https://sqlite.org/lang_comment.html, https://sqlite.org/lang_keywords.html, https://sqlite.org/lang.html)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--` comment | [V] Starts with two "-" (0x2d) and runs "up to and including the next newline character (ASCII 0x0a) or until the end of input, whichever comes first." | - | - | - | tokenizer | - | lang_comment.html |
| `/* */` comment | [V] Runs "up to and including the next "*/" character pair or until the end of input, whichever comes first." "Comments do not nest." | - | - | - | tokenizer | [I] An unterminated /* is accepted, because end of input ends it. | lang_comment.html |
| Comments as whitespace | [V] "Comments are treated as whitespace by the parser. Comments can begin anywhere whitespace can be found". | - | [V] SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_COMMENTS: 3.49.0 (chg 3.49.0). "Comments are enabled by default" (c3ref_c_dbconfig_defensive). | - | - | [I] With comments disabled, a comment is an error. | lang_comment.html |
| 'keyword' | [V] "A keyword in single quotes is a string literal." | - | - | - | - | - | lang_keywords.html |
| "keyword" | [V] "A keyword in double-quotes is an identifier." | - | [V] chg 2.5.0 (2.5.0): "Double-quoted strings interpreted as column names not text literals." | - | - | - | lang_keywords.html |
| [keyword] | [V] "A keyword enclosed in square brackets is an identifier." | [V] "This is not standard SQL. This quoting mechanism is used by MS Access and SQL Server and is included in SQLite for compatibility." | [V] 2.3.3 (chg 2.3.3) | - | - | - | lang_keywords.html |
| \`keyword\` | [V] "A keyword enclosed in grave accents (ASCII code 96) is an identifier." | [V] "This is not standard SQL. This quoting mechanism is used by MySQL and is included in SQLite for compatibility." | [V] 3.2.3 (chg 3.2.3) | - | - | - | lang_keywords.html |
| Fallback: 'x' as identifier | [V] "If a keyword in single quotes (ex: 'key' or 'glob') is used in a context where an identifier is allowed but where a string literal is not allowed, then the token is understood to be an identifier instead of a string literal." | [V] "For resilience when confronted with historical SQL statements"; "Programmers are cautioned not to use the two exceptions"; "they exist only so that old and ill-formed SQL statements will run correctly. Future versions of SQLite might raise errors instead of accepting the malformed statements". | - | - | - | - | lang_keywords.html |
| Fallback: "x" as string | [V] "If a keyword in double quotes (ex: "key" or "glob") is used in a context where it cannot be resolved to an identifier but where a string literal is allowed, then the token is understood to be a string literal instead of an identifier." | Same | - | - | - | - | lang_keywords.html |
| Keyword list | [V] 147 keywords ("any identifier that is not on the following 147-element list is not a keyword"). Some may be left out in some builds. Runtime discovery is through sqlite3_keyword_count/name/check (3.24.0, chg 3.24.0). | [V] "SQLite adds new keywords from time to time"; "you should normally quote any identifier that is an English language word". | - | [V] https://sqlite.org/stricttables.html: "The STRICT keyword may still be used as an identifier" (it is not in the 147 list). | - | - | lang_keywords.html |
| Unquoted keywords as identifiers | [V] quirks.html section 9: "Many keywords can be used as identifiers without needing to be quoted, as long as those keywords are used in a context where it is clear that they are intended to be an identifier." Examples: `CREATE TABLE union(true INT, with BOOLEAN);` is valid. `CREATE TRIGGER AFTER INSERT ON tableX ...` makes a trigger named "AFTER" that is a BEFORE trigger. `CREATE TABLE tableZ(INTEGER PRIMARY KEY)` makes a column named "INTEGER" with no type. [V] chg 3.42.0 (3.42.0): functions may be named CROSS, FULL, INNER, LEFT, NATURAL, OUTER, RIGHT. | [V] "The ability to use keywords as identifiers promotes backwards compatibility... However, the ability to use a keyword as an identifier sometimes leads to surprising outcomes." | - | - | parser fallback | - | quirks.html#keywords_can_often_be_used_as_identifiers |
| sql-stmt-list / sql-stmt | [V] "a semicolon-separated list of statements". sql-stmt = `[EXPLAIN [QUERY PLAN]]` followed by one of: alter-table, analyze, attach, begin, commit, create-index, create-table, create-trigger, create-view, create-virtual-table, delete, delete-stmt-limited, detach, drop-index, drop-table, drop-trigger, drop-view, insert, pragma, reindex, release, rollback, savepoint, select, update, update-stmt-limited, vacuum. | - | - | - | - | - | lang.html; lang_explain.html |
| Omitted features | [V] ALTER TABLE has only RENAME TABLE, ADD COLUMN, RENAME COLUMN, DROP COLUMN. No FOR EACH STATEMENT triggers. "VIEWs in SQLite are read-only." No GRANT/REVOKE. | - | - | - | - | [I] Errors such as "cannot modify X because it is a view" | omitted.html |

#### C-API counters visible from SQL (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/changes.html, https://sqlite.org/c3ref/last_insert_rowid.html)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| sqlite3_changes / changes() | [V] "the number of rows modified, inserted or deleted by the most recently completed INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE statement". "Executing any other type of SQL statement does not modify the value". CREATE TABLE AS SELECT "does not count". | [V] With more than an int's worth of rows, sqlite3_changes() "is undefined". | [V] changes64: 3.37.0 (chg 3.37.0). [V] 3.8.11.1 (chg 3.8.11.1): "Fix a long-standing problem in sqlite3_changes() for WITHOUT ROWID tables". | [V] There was a WITHOUT ROWID bug, since fixed. | DML | - | c3ref/changes.html |
| changes(): what is excluded | [V] "Only changes made directly by the INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE statement are considered - auxiliary changes caused by triggers, foreign key actions or REPLACE constraint resolution are not counted." INSTEAD OF on views: "always zero". | - | - | - | triggers, FKs, REPLACE | - | c3ref/changes.html |
| changes() inside triggers | [V] The value is saved before a trigger and restored after it. In a trigger, the first statement sees "the value as set when the calling statement began executing"; later statements see "the number of rows modified by the previous INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE statement within the same trigger." | - | - | - | triggers | - | c3ref/changes.html |
| sqlite3_last_insert_rowid / last_insert_rowid() | [V] It "usually returns the rowid of the most recent successful INSERT into a rowid table or virtual table". "Inserts into WITHOUT ROWID tables are not recorded. If no successful INSERTs into rowid tables have ever occurred on the database connection D, then sqlite3_last_insert_rowid(D) returns zero." | - | [V] SQL function: 2.4.7 (chg 2.4.7). [V] sqlite3_set_last_insert_rowid: 3.18.0 (chg 3.18.0). | [V] WITHOUT ROWID inserts are not recorded. [I] In sql4s3, where every table is WITHOUT ROWID, last_insert_rowid() is always 0 (unless a virtual table writes to rowid tables, which is not relevant). | rowid tables | - | c3ref/last_insert_rowid.html |
| last_insert_rowid: failure / REPLACE / rollback / triggers | [V] A failed INSERT "does not change the value": "INSERT OR FAIL, INSERT OR IGNORE, INSERT OR ROLLBACK, and INSERT OR ABORT make no changes to the return value of this routine when their insertion fails." "INSERT OR REPLACE will always change the return value". "an INSERT is considered to be successful even if it is subsequently rolled back." In a trigger the value applies "as long as the trigger is running" and then "reverts". | - | - | (WITHOUT ROWID: none of this applies [I]) | - | - | c3ref/last_insert_rowid.html |
| rowid aliases | [V] "The rowid is always available as an undeclared column named ROWID, OID, or _ROWID_ as long as those names are not also used by explicitly declared columns. If the table has a column of type INTEGER PRIMARY KEY then that column is another alias for the rowid." | - | [V] 1.0.28 (chg 1.0.28) | [V] "except for WITHOUT ROWID tables". [V] chg 3.54.0 (3.54.0): ALTER TABLE raises an error on adding/dropping/renaming columns named ROWID, _ROWID_, OID. | - | - | c3ref/last_insert_rowid.html |

### 1.6 Schema objects, constraints and table options

Abbreviations: CT = https://sqlite.org/lang_createtable.html, AT = https://sqlite.org/lang_altertable.html, FK = https://sqlite.org/foreignkeys.html, WR = https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html, ST = https://sqlite.org/stricttables.html, GC = https://sqlite.org/gencol.html, CHG = https://sqlite.org/changes.html.

#### Syntax diagrams (verbatim label sequences extracted from SVG)

- create-table-stmt [V-diagram, CT#syntax]: `CREATE | TEMP | TEMPORARY | TABLE | IF | NOT | EXISTS | schema-name | . | table-name | ( | column-def | table-constraint | , | ) | table-options | , | AS | select-stmt`. Coordinates show `table-options` follows the `)` of the column-list branch only. `AS select-stmt` is an alternative branch after table-name, so CREATE TABLE ... AS SELECT cannot take STRICT or WITHOUT ROWID [I from coordinates: `(` y=261, `table-options` x=614 y=261; `AS` x=457 y=147].
- column-def [V-diagram]: `column-name | type-name | column-constraint`.
- type-name [V-diagram]: `name | ( | signed-number | , | signed-number | ) | ( | signed-number | )`.
- column-constraint [V-diagram]: `CONSTRAINT name | PRIMARY KEY [ASC|DESC] conflict-clause [AUTOINCREMENT] | NOT NULL conflict-clause | UNIQUE conflict-clause | CHECK ( expr ) | DEFAULT ( expr ) | DEFAULT literal-value | DEFAULT signed-number | COLLATE collation-name | foreign-key-clause | [GENERATED ALWAYS] AS ( expr ) [VIRTUAL|STORED]`. The column CHECK has no conflict-clause in the diagram.
- table-constraint [V-diagram]: `CONSTRAINT name | PRIMARY KEY ( indexed-column [AUTOINCREMENT] , ... ) conflict-clause | UNIQUE ( indexed-column , ... ) conflict-clause | CHECK ( expr ) | FOREIGN KEY ( column-name , ... ) foreign-key-clause`. The table-level PRIMARY KEY branch also contains `AUTOINCREMENT` [V-diagram: AUTOINCREMENT x=561 y=78 inside the PRIMARY KEY row]. The diagram shows no conflict-clause after table CHECK, but the prose says table CHECK may have one ("For historical compatibility only"; see B) [I: diagram/prose mismatch; test against the oracle].
- conflict-clause [V-diagram]: `ON CONFLICT ROLLBACK|ABORT|FAIL|IGNORE|REPLACE` (the whole clause is optional).
- foreign-key-clause [V-diagram]: `REFERENCES foreign-table [( column-name , ... )] { ON DELETE|UPDATE { SET NULL | SET DEFAULT | CASCADE | RESTRICT | NO ACTION } | MATCH name }* [ [NOT] DEFERRABLE [INITIALLY DEFERRED | INITIALLY IMMEDIATE] ]`.
- indexed-column [V-diagram]: `column-name | expr` then `[COLLATE collation-name] [ASC|DESC]`.
- table-options [V-diagram]: `WITHOUT ROWID | STRICT` separated by `,`.
- signed-number [V-diagram]: `[+|-] numeric-literal`.
- raise-function [V-diagram]: `RAISE ( IGNORE | ROLLBACK , expr | ABORT , expr | FAIL , expr )`.
- alter-table-stmt [V-diagram, AT#overview]: `ALTER TABLE [schema-name .] table-name` followed by one of:
  - `RENAME TO new-table-name`
  - `RENAME [COLUMN] column-name TO new-column-name`
  - `ADD [COLUMN] column-def`
  - `ADD [CONSTRAINT constraint-name] CHECK ( expr ) [conflict-clause]`
  - `DROP [COLUMN] column-name`
  - `DROP CONSTRAINT constraint-name`
  - `ALTER [COLUMN] column-name SET NOT NULL [conflict-clause]`
  - `ALTER [COLUMN] column-name DROP NOT NULL`

  The labels are verified. Which keywords are optional, and whether conflict-clause attaches to ADD CHECK, are [I] from coordinates (COLUMN and CONSTRAINT sit on bypass rows). The ADD CHECK and DROP CONSTRAINT forms appear only in the diagram: the page has no prose for them, only CHG 3.53.0 "Enhance ALTER TABLE to permit adding and removing NOT NULL and CHECK constraints."
- create-index-stmt [V, https://sqlite.org/lang_createindex.html#syntax]: `CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX [IF NOT EXISTS] [schema-name .] index-name ON table-name ( indexed-column , ... ) [WHERE expr]`.
- create-view-stmt [V, https://sqlite.org/lang_createview.html#syntax]: `CREATE [TEMP|TEMPORARY] VIEW [IF NOT EXISTS] [schema-name .] view-name [( column-name , ... )] AS select-stmt`.
- create-trigger-stmt [V, https://sqlite.org/lang_createtrigger.html#syntax]: `CREATE [TEMP|TEMPORARY] TRIGGER [IF NOT EXISTS] [schema-name .] trigger-name [BEFORE|AFTER|INSTEAD OF] {DELETE|INSERT|UPDATE [OF column-name , ...]} ON table-name [FOR EACH ROW] [WHEN expr] BEGIN { update-stmt | insert-stmt | delete-stmt | select-stmt ; }+ END`.
- create-virtual-table-stmt [V-diagram, https://sqlite.org/lang_createvtab.html]: `CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE [IF NOT EXISTS] [schema-name .] table-name USING module-name [( module-argument , ... )]`.
- drop-table-stmt / drop-index-stmt / drop-view-stmt / drop-trigger-stmt [V-diagram]: `DROP TABLE|INDEX|VIEW|TRIGGER [IF EXISTS] [schema-name .] name`.

#### CREATE TABLE and table-level features

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CREATE TABLE | Creates a table: name, schema, columns, declared types, defaults, collations, optional PK, constraints, optional generated column, WITHOUT ROWID, STRICT [V] | - | ancient (CREATE TABLE AS SELECT 2.3.3; IF NOT EXISTS 3.3.0) [V CHG] | All sql4s3 tables need `) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID` (or the reverse order) [I] | name resolution, type-name parsing | Error if the name starts with "sqlite_": "It is an error to attempt to create a table with a name that starts with "sqlite_"" [V]. Error if an index or view of the same name exists [V]. 3.44.0: "Many errors associated with CREATE TABLE are now raised when the CREATE TABLE statement itself is run, rather than being deferred" [V CHG] | CT#syntax |
| TEMP / TEMPORARY table | Creates the table in the temp database [V] | - | - | - | temp schema | "It is an error to specify both a schema-name and the TEMP or TEMPORARY keyword, unless the schema-name is "temp"" [V] | CT |
| schema-name qualifier | "must be either "main", "temp", or the name of an attached database" [V] | - | - | - | ATTACH | error otherwise [I] | CT |
| IF NOT EXISTS (table) | No-op if a table or view of the same name exists [V] | - | 3.3.0 [V CHG "IF EXISTS and IF NOT EXISTS clauses on CREATE/DROP TABLE/INDEX."] | - | - | "An error is still returned if the table cannot be created because of an existing index, even if the "IF NOT EXISTS" clause is specified." [V]. Same name as a trigger is allowed: "It is not an error to create a table that has the same name as an existing trigger." [V] | CT |
| CREATE TABLE ... AS SELECT | Creates and fills a table from a SELECT result [V] | 3.41.1: CAST(7 AS INT) column type "continues to be INT and is not NUM, for historical compatibility" [V CHG] | 2.3.3 [V CHG] | Produces a rowid table with "no PRIMARY KEY and no constraints of any kind" [V]. It cannot be WITHOUT ROWID (no PK) and the diagram gives it no table-options, so it can never be STRICT [I] | SELECT, expression affinity | - | CT#createtabas |
| CTAS declared types | Expression affinity TEXT→"TEXT", NUMERIC→"NUM", INTEGER→"INT", REAL→"REAL", BLOB/NONE→"" (empty string) [V] | - | 3.6.15 simplified types [V CHG] | Type names NUM and "" are not valid STRICT types [I] | affinity | - | CT#createtabas |
| CTAS defaults / rowids | "The default value of each column is NULL. The default collation sequence for each column of the new table is BINARY." "Rows are assigned contiguously ascending rowid values, starting with 1, in the order that they are returned" [V] | - | - | rowid-only | - | - | CT#createtabas |
| Column definition | name, optional type, zero or more constraints. COLLATE and DEFAULT count as "column constraints" [V] | - | 1.0.18: "Do not require commas between constraints" [V CHG] | STRICT: "Every column definition must specify a datatype" [V ST] | - | - | CT#tablecoldef |
| SQLITE_MAX_COLUMN / SQLITE_MAX_LENGTH | Column-count limit and row byte limit. Can be lowered with sqlite3_limit() [V] | - | - | - | - | error [I] | CT#tablecoldef |
| Declared type → affinity | "SQLite uses dynamic typing. The declared type of a column is used to determine the affinity of the column only." [V] | - | - | STRICT overrides (see C) | datatype3 | - | CT |
| table-options list | Comma-separated list after the final `)`. Only STRICT and WITHOUT ROWID exist. "If there are multiple options, they can be specified in any order." [V] | "the current parser accepts duplicate options without complaining, but that might change in future releases, so applications should not rely on it" [V] | STRICT 3.37.0; WITHOUT ROWID 3.8.2 | STRICT and WITHOUT ROWID can be combined: `) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID` [V ST#other_table_options + diagram] | - | - | ST#other_table_options |
| WITHOUT ROWID | Table with no rowid. The PK is a clustered index [V] | "an optimization. It provides no new capabilities." [V] | 3.8.2 (2013-12-06) [V CT, WR, CHG] | See C | PRIMARY KEY | Error if no PRIMARY KEY: "An error is raised if a CREATE TABLE statement with the WITHOUT ROWID clause lacks a PRIMARY KEY." [V] | WR#syntax |
| STRICT | Rigid typing for that table [V] | - | 3.37.0 (2021-11-27) [V ST, CHG] | See C | - | SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE on a type violation [V ST] | ST#strict_tables |
| Rowid / oid / _rowid_ | 64-bit signed integer key of rowid tables, accessible by those 3 names unless a declared column uses the name [V] | rowidtable: "The designer of SQLite offers his sincere apology for the current mess." [V] | - | "The rowid (and "oid" and "_rowid_") is omitted in WITHOUT ROWID tables." [V CT#rowid] | - | 3.54.0: "The ALTER TABLE command raises an error on any attempt to add, drop, or rename columns with the special names "ROWID", "_ROWID_", or "OID"." [V CHG] | CT#rowid, https://sqlite.org/rowidtable.html |

#### Column constraints and clauses

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CONSTRAINT name | Optional name prefix for any constraint [V-diagram] | - | 3.3.9: "Allow constraint names on the DEFAULT clause" [V CHG] | - | - | - | CT#syntax |
| PRIMARY KEY (column) | Single-column PK. At most one PK per table [V] | NULLs allowed "due to a bug in some early versions" (see B) | - | Required for WITHOUT ROWID. Implicit NOT NULL in WITHOUT ROWID and STRICT [V] | unique index (rowid tables) | "An error is raised if more than one PRIMARY KEY clause appears in a CREATE TABLE statement." [V]. Duplicate → SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_PRIMARYKEY [V cross-page rescode#constraint_primarykey: "a PRIMARY KEY constraint failed"] | CT#primkeyconst |
| PRIMARY KEY ASC/DESC | Sort order on a column PK [V-diagram] | "PRIMARY KEY DESC" on INTEGER → not a rowid alias: "This quirk is not by design. It is due to a bug in early versions of SQLite." [V] | - | In WITHOUT ROWID no rowid alias exists, so the quirk does not apply [I] | - | - | CT#rowid |
| PK conflict-clause | Default conflict algorithm for the PK [V] | - | - | - | ON CONFLICT | - | CT |
| AUTOINCREMENT | Prevents rowid reuse; uses sqlite_sequence [V] | "imposes extra CPU, memory, disk space, and disk I/O overhead and should be avoided if not strictly needed. It is usually not needed." [V autoinc#summary] | 3.1.0 [V CHG "AUTOINCREMENT keyword supported on INTEGER PRIMARY KEY"] | "AUTOINCREMENT is not allowed on WITHOUT ROWID tables or on any table column other than INTEGER PRIMARY KEY." STRICT: "AUTOINCREMENT works the same" [V] | INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, rowid | "Any attempt to use AUTOINCREMENT on a WITHOUT ROWID table or on a column other than the INTEGER PRIMARY KEY column results in an error." Rowid exhaustion → SQLITE_FULL [V] | https://sqlite.org/autoinc.html#the_autoincrement_keyword |
| NOT NULL | Column may not hold NULL. Column-level only: "may only be attached to a column definition, not specified as a table constraint" [V] | - | 2.3.0 "NOT NULL constraints are honored." [V CHG] | Same in STRICT [V ST] | - | Constraint violation → SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL [V rescode]. 3.42.0: "Detect and raise an error when a NaN value is stored in a NOT NULL column." [V CHG] | CT#notnullconst |
| NOT NULL conflict-clause | Default conflict algorithm for NOT NULL [V] | - | - | - | - | - | CT#syntax |
| UNIQUE (column) | Unique values. NULLs are distinct [V] | - | 2.0 era [V CHG 2.0-alpha-3] | Same in STRICT [V] | unique index | SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE [V rescode] | CT#uniqueconst |
| CHECK (column or table) | Expression evaluated on INSERT/UPDATE and cast to NUMERIC. 0 or 0.0 → violation. NULL or non-zero → OK. No subqueries [V] | Table CHECK conflict clause "For historical compatibility only ... has no effect" [V] | 3.3.0 [V CHG "CHECK constraints"] | Same in STRICT [V] | expressions, CAST | SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_CHECK [V rescode]. 3.7.12: "Report the name of specific CHECK constraints that fail." [V CHG]. The conflict algorithm is always ABORT [V] | CT#ckconst |
| DEFAULT literal / signed-number | NULL, string, blob, signed-number used directly [V] | - | - | Same in STRICT [V]. [I] In STRICT the default value must still pass the type check at INSERT | literals | - | CT#dfltval |
| DEFAULT ( expr ) | "any constant expression enclosed in parentheses", evaluated once per inserted row [V] | - | 3.2.3 "Allow the DEFAULT value of a column to call functions that have constant operands" [V CHG] | - | constant-expression check | Non-constant (subquery, column ref, bound parameter, double-quoted string) → error [I] | CT#dfltval |
| DEFAULT CURRENT_TIME / CURRENT_DATE / CURRENT_TIMESTAMP | UTC text "HH:MM:SS", "YYYY-MM-DD", "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS" [V] | - | 3.1.0 [V CHG] | - | clock | - | CT#dfltval |
| DEFAULT TRUE/FALSE | ALTER TABLE accepts true/false as DEFAULT [V CHG] | - | 3.24.0 [V CHG] | - | - | - | CHG |
| DEFAULT large hex | "Allow large hexadecimal literals to be used as the DEFAULT value" [V CHG] | - | 3.46.0 [V CHG] | - | - | - | CHG |
| COLLATE (column) | Default collation for the column. BINARY if absent [V] | - | - | Same in STRICT [V] | collations | unknown collation → error [I] | CT#collateclause |
| REFERENCES (column FK) | Column-level foreign key [V] | see FK table | 3.6.19 enforcement [V FK] | Same in STRICT [V] | FK | see FK | CT, FK |
| GENERATED ALWAYS AS (expr) VIRTUAL/STORED | Generated column [V] | - | 3.31.0 (2020-01-22) [V CT, GC, CHG] | STRICT: "Generated columns work the same" [V ST]. 3.51.0: "Enforce STRICT typing on computed columns." [V CHG] | deterministic scalar functions | see A.6 | CT, GC |

#### Table constraints

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PRIMARY KEY ( cols ) | Composite PK. "must contain only column names — the use of expressions in an indexed-column of a PRIMARY KEY is not supported" [V] | - | - | Required for WITHOUT ROWID. Each column NOT NULL in WITHOUT ROWID and STRICT [V] | - | Expressions → error [I]. More than one PK → error [V] | CT#primkeyconst |
| PRIMARY KEY (x AUTOINCREMENT) | AUTOINCREMENT inside the table PK [V-diagram] | - | - | Not on WITHOUT ROWID [V autoinc] | - | - | CT#syntax |
| UNIQUE ( cols ) | Composite unique. "must contain only column names" [V] | - | - | - | unique index | SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE | CT#uniqueconst |
| CHECK ( expr ) | Same as column CHECK: "In practice it makes no difference." [V] | conflict clause allowed but ignored "For historical compatibility only" [V] | 3.3.0 | - | - | - | CT#ckconst |
| FOREIGN KEY ( cols ) REFERENCES | Composite FK [V] | - | 3.6.19 | - | FK | see FK | FK#fk_composite |
| Logical equivalence | `t1(a, b UNIQUE)`, `t1(a, b PRIMARY KEY)` and `t1(a,b)` plus `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX t1b ON t1(b)` are "logically equivalent". "(The exceptions are INTEGER PRIMARY KEY and PRIMARY KEYs on WITHOUT ROWID tables.)" [V] | - | - | WITHOUT ROWID PK is not a separate index [V] | - | - | CT#uniqueconst |

| ON CONFLICT ROLLBACK/ABORT/FAIL/IGNORE/REPLACE on PK, UNIQUE, NOT NULL | Per-constraint default conflict algorithm. ABORT if absent [V] | - | 2.3.0 [V CHG] | STRICT: "ON CONFLICT clauses work the same." [V] | lang_conflict | - | CT (section 4.1 "Response to constraint violations") |

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `[GENERATED ALWAYS] AS (expr)` | "Only the "AS" keyword and the parenthesized expression are required." [V] | - | 3.31.0 | - | - | - | GC#syntax |
| VIRTUAL (default) | "If the trailing "VIRTUAL" or "STORED" keyword is omitted, then VIRTUAL is the default." Computed when read [V] | - | 3.31.0 | - | - | - | GC#virtual_versus_stored_columns |
| STORED | Computed when the row is written. Cannot be added with ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN [V] | - | 3.31.0 | - | - | ADD COLUMN of STORED → error [V AT] | GC |
| Datatype on generated column | "SQLite attempts to transform the result of the generating expression into that datatype using the same affinity rules as for ordinary columns." [V] | - | - | 3.51.0 "Enforce STRICT typing on computed columns." [V CHG]. [I] So a STRICT INTEGER generated column whose expression yields 'abc' fails with SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE, probably on write for STORED and on read for VIRTUAL (unverified) | affinity | [I] | GC#capabilities |
| Constraints on generated columns | NOT NULL, CHECK, UNIQUE and FK are allowed [V] | - | - | - | - | - | GC#capabilities |
| Indexes on generated columns | STORED → ordinary index. VIRTUAL → expression index [V] | - | - | - | expridx | - | GC#capabilities |
| Limit: no DEFAULT | "Generated columns may not have a default value" [V] | - | - | - | - | error [I] | GC#limitations |
| Limit: not in PRIMARY KEY | "Generated columns may not be used as part of the PRIMARY KEY. (Future versions of SQLite might relax this constraint for STORED columns.)" [V] | "might relax" | - | Matters for WITHOUT ROWID PK design [I] | - | error [I] | GC#limitations |
| Limit: expression content | "may only reference constant literals and columns within the same row, and may only use scalar deterministic functions. The expression may not use subqueries, aggregate functions, window functions, or table-valued functions." [V] | - | - | - | deterministic | error [I] | GC#limitations |
| Limit: no cycles | "no generated column can depend upon itself, either directly or indirectly" [V] | - | - | - | - | error [I] | GC#limitations |
| Limit: no ROWID reference | "may not directly reference the ROWID, though it can reference the INTEGER PRIMARY KEY column" [V] | - | - | WITHOUT ROWID has no rowid anyway [I] | - | error [I] | GC#limitations |
| Limit: one non-generated column | "Every table must have at least one non-generated column." [V] | - | - | - | - | error [I] | GC#limitations |
| Limit: type/collation | Only the column definition's type and COLLATE matter. The expression's type and collation are ignored for the column [V] | - | - | - | - | - | GC#limitations |
| PRAGMA table_info vs table_xinfo | Generated columns are left out of table_info and shown in table_xinfo [V] | - | - | - | - | - | GC#limitations |
| Quirk: INSERT INTO dest SELECT * FROM src | Accepted although `*` includes generated columns ("Technically, this INSERT statement is incorrect") [V] | "Quirks" | - | - | xfer optimization | - | GC#quirks |

#### Foreign keys

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FK enforcement | Child row needs a matching parent row unless any child key column is NULL [V] | "Foreign key constraints are disabled by default (for backwards compatibility)" [V] | Parsed since 3.0.0 ("Parse (but do not implement) foreign keys") [V CHG]. Enforced since 3.6.19 (2009-10-14) [V FK, CHG] | STRICT: "FOREIGN KEY constraints work the same." [V] | PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON; build without SQLITE_OMIT_FOREIGN_KEY / SQLITE_OMIT_TRIGGER | Docs CLI shows "SQL error: foreign key constraint failed" [V]. Extended code SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY (787) [V cross-page rescode]. [I] the current message text is "FOREIGN KEY constraint failed" | FK#fk_basics |
| Satisfaction rule | "for each row in the child table either one or more of the child key columns are NULL, or there exists a row in the parent table for which each parent key column contains a value equal to the value in its associated child key column" [V] | - | - | - | - | - | FK#parentchild |
| Comparison rule | Parent column's collation is always used. The parent's affinity is applied to the child value before comparing [V] | - | - | [I] With STRICT on both sides and equal types, affinity has little effect | affinity, collation | - | FK#parentchild |
| Parent key requirement | Must be the parent PK, or columns under a UNIQUE constraint/index with the CREATE TABLE collations. Exact column-set match. Not the rowid [V] | - | - | Parent may be WITHOUT ROWID. 3.19.0 fixed foreign_key_check "with foreign keys on WITHOUT ROWID tables" [V CHG] | - | DML error "foreign key mismatch" (or "no such table") [V] | FK#fk_indexes |
| REFERENCES parent (no column list) | Maps to the parent PK. Column count must match [V] | - | - | - | - | mismatch → DML error [V] | FK#fk_indexes |
| DDL vs DML FK errors | Errors visible from the child definition alone → CREATE TABLE fails (DDL error), "regardless of whether or not foreign key constraints are enabled". Errors that need the parent are reported when preparing DML that uses the FK [V] | - | 3.7.16: "Improved error messages for "foreign key mismatch" showing the names of the two tables involved." [V CHG] | - | - | "foreign key mismatch" / "no such table" [V] | FK#fk_indexes |
| Composite FK | "Parent and child keys must have the same cardinality." [V] | - | - | - | - | DDL error [I] | FK#fk_composite |
| Immediate (default) | Checked at the end of each statement. A violation reverts the statement [V] | - | 3.6.19 | - | - | SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY [I code] | FK#fk_deferred |
| DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED | Only this exact form makes a deferred FK. Checked at COMMIT [V] | - | 3.6.19 | - | transactions | "COMMIT will fail as long as foreign key constraints remain in violation." The failed COMMIT leaves the transaction open: "The transaction remains open." [V] | FK#fk_deferred |
| NOT DEFERRABLE [INITIALLY DEFERRED/IMMEDIATE], DEFERRABLE [INITIALLY IMMEDIATE] | All give immediate constraints [V] | - | - | - | - | - | FK#fk_deferred |
| Deferred FK and SAVEPOINT | A nested savepoint may be RELEASEd while in violation. A transaction savepoint RELEASE fails like COMMIT. Nested savepoints stay open on failure [V] | - | - | - | SAVEPOINT | error [V] | FK#fk_deferred |
| ON DELETE / ON UPDATE NO ACTION (default) | No special action [V] | - | 3.6.19 | - | - | - | FK#fk_actions |
| RESTRICT | Error as soon as the parent key changes, even for deferred FKs [V] | - | 3.6.19. 3.12.0: "PRAGMA defer_foreign_keys=ON statement now also disables RESTRICT actions" [V CHG] | - | - | immediate error | FK#fk_actions |
| SET NULL | Child keys set to NULL [V] | - | - | - | - | - | FK#fk_actions |
| SET DEFAULT | Child keys set to the column default. The FK must still be satisfied afterwards [V] | - | - | - | DEFAULT | violation if the default has no parent [V] | FK#fk_actions |
| CASCADE | Delete or update propagates to children [V] | - | - | - | - | - | FK#fk_actions |
| ON UPDATE only if changed | "An ON UPDATE action is only taken if the values of the parent key are modified so that the new parent key values are not equal to the old." [V] | - | - | - | - | - | FK#fk_actions |
| Event order | BEFORE triggers → local (non-FK) constraints → parent row update/delete → FK actions → AFTER triggers [V] | - | - | - | triggers | - | FK#fk_actions |
| MATCH clause | Parsed, not enforced. "All foreign key constraints in SQLite are handled as if MATCH SIMPLE were specified." [V] | "No support for the MATCH clause." [V] | - | - | - | no syntax error [V] | FK#fk_unsupported |
| SET CONSTRAINT toggling | Not supported. Deferral is fixed at creation [V] | "SQLite does not support this." | - | - | - | - | FK#fk_unsupported |
| Recursion limit | FK actions count as trigger programs for SQLITE_MAX_TRIGGER_DEPTH / SQLITE_LIMIT_TRIGGER_DEPTH. "The PRAGMA recursive_triggers setting does not affect the operation of foreign key actions. It is not possible to disable recursive foreign key actions." [V] | - | - | - | - | - | FK#fk_unsupported |
| Cross-schema FK | "Foreign keys may not cross schema boundaries." [V] | - | - | - | - | - | FK#fk_unsupported |
| CREATE TABLE and FKs | "A CREATE TABLE command operates the same whether or not foreign key constraints are enabled. The parent key definitions of foreign key constraints are not checked when a table is created." [V] | - | - | - | - | none at CREATE [V] | FK#fk_schemacommands |
| ALTER ADD COLUMN with REFERENCES | With FKs on, the default must be NULL [V] | - | - | - | - | "Attempting to do so returns an error." [V] | FK#fk_schemacommands |
| ALTER RENAME TO a parent | Child FK text in sqlite_schema is rewritten to the new name [V] | - | 3.26.0: always, unless legacy_alter_table=ON [V AT] | - | - | - | FK#fk_schemacommands, AT#altertabrename |
| DROP TABLE with FKs on | Implicit DELETE FROM first. Triggers are dropped before it. FK actions run [V] | - | - | - | - | Immediate violation → DROP fails. Deferred → error at COMMIT. "Any "foreign key mismatch" errors encountered as part of an implicit DELETE are ignored." [V] | FK#fk_schemacommands, https://sqlite.org/lang_droptable.html |
| Exception: parent unique by CREATE INDEX only | Dropping that index or the parent may leave orphans with no error [V] | - | - | - | - | none [V] | FK#fk_schemacommands |
| Child-key index | Not required, "almost always beneficial" [V] | - | - | - | - | - | FK#fk_indexes |

| CREATE VIEW | Named SELECT usable in FROM [V] | - | 2.4.0 [V CHG] | - | SELECT | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_createview.html |
| TEMP VIEW | Visible only to this connection, deleted at close [V] | - | - | - | - | schema-name + TEMP error unless "temp" [V] | lang_createview#description |
| View column-name list | Names the view columns. "The use of column-name list is recommended." [V] | Auto-generated names "might change in future releases of SQLite" [V] | 3.9.0 [V] | - | - | - | lang_createview#description |
| Views are read-only | "You cannot DELETE, INSERT, or UPDATE a view." Use INSTEAD OF triggers [V] | - | - | - | - | error [I] | lang_createview#description |
| Views with undefined objects | 3.9.0: "A VIEW may now reference undefined tables and functions when initially created. Missing tables and functions are reported when the VIEW is used in a query." [V CHG] | - | 3.9.0 | - | - | error at use time [V] | CHG |
| rowid of a view | 3.36.0: "An error is raised on any attempt to access the rowid of a VIEW or subquery." -DSQLITE_ALLOW_ROWID_IN_VIEW "restore[s] the legacy behavior" [V CHG] | "legacy behavior" | 3.36.0 | - | - | error [V] | CHG |
| Parameters in WITH of views | 3.23.0: "Disallow parameters in the WITH clause of triggers and views." [V CHG] | - | 3.23.0 | - | - | error | CHG |
| DROP VIEW [IF EXISTS] | Removes the definition only. Not found and no IF EXISTS → error [V] | - | 3.3.8 [V CHG] | - | - | error [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_dropview.html |

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CREATE TRIGGER | Runs DML when DELETE/INSERT/UPDATE happens on a table or view [V] | - | 2.5.0 row triggers [V CHG] | - | DML | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_createtrigger.html#description |
| BEFORE (default) / AFTER | Timing. "BEFORE is the default when neither keyword is present." [V] | BEFORE cautions: undefined results (see E). "programmers are encouraged to prefer AFTER triggers" [V] | - | - | - | - | lang_createtrigger#description |
| INSTEAD OF | Only on views. "BEFORE and AFTER triggers work only on ordinary tables. INSTEAD OF triggers work only on views." [V] | - | (2.x) [V CHG 2.8.14 mentions INSTEAD OF] | - | views | error otherwise [I] | lang_createtrigger#instead_of_triggers |
| DELETE / INSERT / UPDATE events | One event per trigger [V] | - | - | - | - | - | lang_createtrigger#description |
| UPDATE OF col-list | Fires only if a named column is on the left side of SET [V] | "Due to an historical oversight, columns named in the "UPDATE OF" clause do not actually have to exist ... silently ignored ... we have resisted fixing the problem for fear of breaking legacy applications." [V] | - | - | - | no error for unknown column [V] | lang_createtrigger#description |
| FOR EACH ROW | Optional. Only row triggers exist [V] | - | 3.3.15: "The FOR EACH STATEMENT clause on a trigger now causes a syntax error. It used to be silently ignored." [V CHG] | - | - | FOR EACH STATEMENT → syntax error [V CHG] | lang_createtrigger#description |
| WHEN expr | Actions run only if WHEN is true [V] | - | - | - | - | - | lang_createtrigger#description |
| NEW / OLD references | INSERT: NEW. UPDATE: NEW and OLD. DELETE: OLD [V] | - | - | - | - | wrong reference → error [I] | lang_createtrigger#description |
| NEW.rowid in BEFORE INSERT | "undefined ... in which the rowid is not explicitly set to an integer" [V] | undefined | - | Not relevant for WITHOUT ROWID [I] | - | - | lang_createtrigger#undef_before |
| Trigger body restrictions | Unqualified target table names. Non-TEMP triggers use the same database. No `INSERT ... DEFAULT VALUES`. No INDEXED BY / NOT INDEXED. No ORDER BY / LIMIT on UPDATE/DELETE. No top-level CTEs (allowed inside sub-selects) [V] | - | - | - | - | error [I] | lang_createtrigger#syntax_restrictions_on_update_delete_and_insert_statements_within_triggers |
| ON CONFLICT inside trigger | Allowed. An outer statement's ON CONFLICT overrides it [V] | - | - | - | - | - | lang_createtrigger#description |
| RAISE(IGNORE) | Abandons the rest of this trigger, the triggering statement and later triggers. No rollback [V] | - | 3.5.3 bug fix [V CHG] | - | - | - | lang_createtrigger#raise |
| RAISE(ROLLBACK/ABORT/FAIL, msg) | Applies that ON CONFLICT behaviour and ends the query [V] | - | Message as expression: 3.47.0 (2024-10-21). Before that it had to be a string literal [V] | - | - | "An error code of SQLITE_CONSTRAINT is returned to the application, along with the specified error message." [V]. Extended: SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_TRIGGER [V rescode: "a RAISE function within a trigger fired"] | lang_createtrigger#raise |
| TEMP trigger | Lives in temp. May target a table in another database. Seen only by this connection [V] | - | 2.8.1 [V CHG]. 3.53.0: "The body of TEMP triggers may now modify and/or query tables in the main schema." [V CHG] | - | ATTACH | - | lang_createtrigger#temptrig |
| Trigger lifetime | Dropped with its table. Not changed when tables it references are dropped [V] | - | - | - | - | - | lang_createtrigger#description |
| changes() and INSTEAD OF | "sqlite3_changes() and sqlite3_total_changes() interfaces do not count INSTEAD OF trigger firings" [V] | - | - | - | - | - | lang_createtrigger#instead_of_triggers |
| IF NOT EXISTS (trigger/view) | - | - | 3.3.8 "Added support for IF EXISTS on CREATE/DROP TRIGGER/VIEW" [V CHG] | - | - | - | CHG |
| Triggers on virtual tables | Not allowed [V] | - | - | - | - | error [I] | https://sqlite.org/vtab.html#introduction |
| DROP TRIGGER [IF EXISTS] | Removes it from sqlite_schema / sqlite_temp_schema [V] | - | 3.3.8 | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/lang_droptrigger.html |
| SQLITE_LIMIT_TRIGGER_STEPS | New limit [V CHG] | - | 3.54.0 | - | - | - | CHG |

| ALTER TABLE ... RENAME COLUMN | Renames in the table, indexes, triggers and views [V] | - | 3.25.0 [V CHG] | - | - | "If the column name change would result in a semantic ambiguity in a trigger or view, then the RENAME COLUMN fails with an error and no changes are applied." [V] | AT#altertabmvcol |
| ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN | Appends a column [V] | - | 3.2.0 [V CHG] | [I] In STRICT tables the new column needs a STRICT type | - | Restrictions: no PRIMARY KEY or UNIQUE. No STORED generated columns (VIRTUAL is fine) [V] | AT#altertabaddcol |

Fixes in 3.35.1 and 3.35.5 | - | schema reparse | Fails if the column is a PK or part of one; has UNIQUE; is indexed; is in a partial-index WHERE; is in another column's or the table's CHECK; is used in an FK; is used in a generated column; appears in a trigger or view [V] | AT#altertabdropcol |
| ALTER COLUMN SET NOT NULL [conflict-clause] / DROP NOT NULL | Adds or removes NOT NULL [V] | Redundant NOT NULLs: DROP removes "one or more of them, but not necessarily all of them" [V] | 3.53.0 (2026-04-09) [V] | - | - | [I] SET NOT NULL presumably fails if existing rows have NULL (not stated) | AT#alter_table_alter_column |
| ADD [CONSTRAINT name] CHECK(expr) / DROP CONSTRAINT name | Adds or drops a CHECK (or named constraint) [V-diagram + CHG 3.53.0] | no prose on the page | 3.53.0 [V CHG] | - | - | [I] ADD CHECK presumably validates existing rows | AT#overview |
| ROWID/_ROWID_/OID names in ALTER | Error on add, drop or rename of columns with those names [V CHG] | - | 3.54.0 | - | - | error [V] | CHG |

| 12-step generalized procedure | foreign_keys=OFF; BEGIN; save the index/trigger/view SQL; CREATE new_X; INSERT INTO new_X SELECT ... FROM X; DROP TABLE X; ALTER TABLE new_X RENAME TO X; recreate indexes, triggers, views; fix views; PRAGMA foreign_key_check; COMMIT; foreign_keys back on [V] | "Caution: Take care to follow the procedure above precisely." Renaming the old table first "does not always work" [V] | - | - | - | - | AT#otheralter, AT#caution |

| WITHOUT ROWID virtual tables | The declared schema may say WITHOUT ROWID. The PK is not enforced by SQLite [V] | - | 3.14.0 [V] | - | - | multi-column PK + xUpdate → rejected [V] | vtab.html#worid |

Ordinary DML can modify it. It does not track UPDATE-driven rowid changes [V] | "Make sure you know what you are doing" [V] | 3.1.0 | Never exists when tables are WITHOUT ROWID (AUTOINCREMENT forbidden) [I] | AUTOINCREMENT | - | autoinc.html#the_autoincrement_keyword |
| Default rowid choice | max(rowid)+1, or 1 if empty. At the max value, random positive rowids are tried [V] | - | - | Rowid tables only | - | SQLITE_FULL [V] | autoinc.html#background |

### 1.7 Result codes

Tags: [V] = read in the docs (URL given). [I] = inference. [V-measured] = observed by running the Homebrew `sqlite3` CLI 3.54.1 on PATH. CLI measurements can differ from library defaults, because the CLI changes some settings (see G.0).

"Introduced" values come from changes.html. When the changelog only gives a first mention rather than a clear "Added ...", the cell says "first changelog mention".

#### Result-code framework (rescode.html, c3ref/c_abort.html, c3ref/c_abort_rollback.html, c3ref/errcode.html)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Result codes vs error codes | "There are only a few non-error result codes: SQLITE_OK, SQLITE_ROW, and SQLITE_DONE. The term "error code" means any result code other than these three." [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html |
| Primary vs extended | "Result codes are signed 32-bit integers. The least significant 8 bits of the result code define a broad category and are called the "primary result code". More significant bits provide more detailed information about the error and are called the "extended result code."" [V] "the primary result code is always a part of the extended result code" [V] | "For historic compatibility, the C-language interfaces return primary result codes by default." [V] | Extended codes: 3.3.8 (2006-10-09): "Introduce extended error codes" [V changes] | - | sqlite3_extended_result_codes(), sqlite3_extended_errcode() | - | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#pve |
| Stability of codes | "The names and numeric values for existing result codes are fixed and unchanging. However, new result codes, and especially new extended result codes, might appear in future releases of SQLite." [V]; c3ref: "New error codes may be added in future versions of SQLite." [V] | quoted at left | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html ; https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_abort.html |
| Count of codes | rescode.html: "The 31 result codes", "The 74 extended result codes", "all 105 result code values" [V]. c3ref/c_abort_rollback.html: "In its default configuration, SQLite API routines return one of 30 integer result codes." [V]. The c3ref #define list has 8 more extended codes than rescode.html lists (see A.3) [V]. | docs disagree with each other (30 vs 31; 74 vs 82 in header) [I] | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#extrc |
| Error message text (sqlite3_errmsg) | "return English-language text that describes the error, as either UTF-8 or UTF-16 respectively, or NULL if no error message is available." [V] "the error string might be overwritten or deallocated by subsequent calls" [V] | **I found no statement anywhere in the downloaded docs that the error message text is stable.** Changelog 3.20.0 (2017-08-01): "Update the text of error messages returned by sqlite3_errmsg() for some error codes." [V changes], so message text has changed between releases. [I] Treat message text as unstable: compare codes, not messages, or pin messages to the oracle version. | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/errcode.html |
| sqlite3_errstr(E) | "returns the English-language text that describes the result code E" [V] | - | 3.7.15 (2012-12-12) [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/errcode.html |
| sqlite3_error_offset() | "If the most recent error references a specific token in the input SQL, the sqlite3_error_offset() interface returns the byte offset of the start of that token ... assumes that the input SQL is UTF-8 ... Otherwise ... returns -1." [V] | - | 3.38.0 (2022-02-22) [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/errcode.html |
| sqlite3_errcode / extended_errcode | "sqlite3_extended_errcode() interface is the same except that it always returns the extended result code even when extended result codes are disabled." [V] | "If an interface fails with SQLITE_MISUSE ... the error code and message may or may not be set." [V] | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/errcode.html |

#### Primary result codes (one row each)

Relevance column = "Subset" note in the description: **MUST** = a subset SQL engine will raise it for ordinary SQL; **MAYBE** = only with features or APIs it may not have; **NO** = VFS, OS, file or C-API misuse only. All relevance labels are [I].

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SQLITE_OK (0) | "the operation was successful and that there were no errors" [V]. Subset: MUST | - | - | - | - | 0 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#ok |
| SQLITE_ERROR (1) | "a generic error code that is used when no other more specific error code is available." [V] Parse errors, "no such column", and similar errors use it [I; measured CLI messages "Parse error ..."]. Subset: MUST (the main code for "rejected" input) | - | - | sqlite3_blob_open fails with SQLITE_ERROR if "Table zTable is a WITHOUT ROWID table" [V] | - | 1 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#error |
| SQLITE_INTERNAL (2) | "indicates an internal malfunction ... an application should never see this result code" [V]. Subset: NO (bug indicator) | - | - | - | - | 2 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#internal |
| SQLITE_PERM (3) | "the requested access mode for a newly created database could not be provided" [V]. Subset: NO | - | - | - | - | 3 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#perm |
| SQLITE_ABORT (4) | "an operation was aborted prior to completion, usually by application request" [V]; sqlite3_exec callback non-zero gives SQLITE_ABORT [V]; "If a ROLLBACK operation occurs on the same database connection as a pending read or write, then the pending read or write may fail with an SQLITE_ABORT or SQLITE_ABORT_ROLLBACK error." [V] It is also a conflict-resolution mode returned by sqlite3_vtab_on_conflict() [V]. Expired blob handles fail with SQLITE_ABORT [V blob_open]. Subset: MAYBE (ROLLBACK with pending statements) | - | - | - | transactions | 4 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#abort |
| SQLITE_BUSY (5) | "the database file could not be written (or in some cases read) because of concurrent activity by some other database connection" [V]; "BEGIN IMMEDIATE ... if it succeeds, then SQLite guarantees that no subsequent operations on the same database through the next COMMIT will return SQLITE_BUSY." [V] Subset: MAYBE (multi-connection) | - | - | - | locking | 5 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#busy |
| SQLITE_LOCKED (6) | "a write operation could not continue because of a conflict within the same database connection or ... shared cache" [V]. Example: "a DROP TABLE statement cannot be run while another thread is reading from that table on the same database connection" [V]. Subset: MAYBE | - | - | - | - | 6 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#locked |
| SQLITE_NOMEM (7) | "SQLite was unable to allocate all the memory it needed" [V]. Also raised when SQLITE_LIMIT_VDBE_OP is exceeded [V c3ref limit]. Subset: MAYBE | - | - | - | - | 7 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#nomem |
| SQLITE_READONLY (8) | "returned when an attempt is made to alter some data for which the current database connection does not have write permission" [V]. PRAGMA query_only: "any attempt to CREATE, DELETE, DROP, INSERT, or UPDATE will result in an SQLITE_READONLY error" [V]. Subset: MAYBE (read-only open) | - | - | - | - | 8 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#readonly |
| SQLITE_INTERRUPT (9) | "an operation was interrupted by the sqlite3_interrupt() interface" [V]. Subset: MAYBE (context cancellation in Go [I]) | - | - | - | - | 9 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#interrupt |
| SQLITE_IOERR (10) | "the operating system reported an I/O error" [V]. Subset: NO (storage layer) | - | - | - | - | 10 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#ioerr |
| SQLITE_CORRUPT (11) | "the database file has been corrupted" [V]. Subset: NO / storage | - | - | - | - | 11 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#corrupt |
| SQLITE_NOTFOUND (12) | file-control opcode unknown; xSetSystemCall; sqlite3_vtab_rhs_value [V]. Subset: NO | - | - | - | - | 12 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#notfound |
| SQLITE_FULL (13) | "a write could not complete because the disk is full" [V]. Exceeding max_page_count: "An attempt to insert new data that would cause the database file to grow larger than this will return SQLITE_FULL." [V limits]. Subset: MAYBE | - | - | - | - | 13 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#full |
| SQLITE_CANTOPEN (14) | "unable to open a file" [V]. Subset: NO | - | - | - | - | 14 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#cantopen |
| SQLITE_PROTOCOL (15) | WAL locking race lost repeatedly [V]. Subset: NO | - | - | - | - | 15 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#protocol |
| SQLITE_EMPTY (16) | "not currently used" [V]. Subset: NO | "not currently used" | - | - | - | 16 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#empty |
| SQLITE_SCHEMA (17) | "the database schema has changed"; with sqlite3_prepare_v2() "the statement is automatically re-prepared if the schema changes, up to SQLITE_MAX_SCHEMA_RETRY times (default: 50)" [V]. Changing foreign_keys / recursive_triggers: "Any existing statements prepared using the legacy sqlite3_prepare() interface may fail with an SQLITE_SCHEMA error" [V pragma]. Subset: MAYBE | "legacy sqlite3_prepare()" | - | - | prepared statements | 17 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#schema |
| SQLITE_TOOBIG (18) | "a string or BLOB was too large ... exceeds the compile-time or run-time limit"; "can also result when an oversized SQL statement is passed into one of the sqlite3_prepare_v2() interfaces" [V]. printf(): "any printf() result with a width or precision more than the SQLITE_LIMIT_LENGTH will cause an SQLITE_TOOBIG error" [V compile]. Subset: MUST (string concat, zeroblob, printf, randomblob, SQL length) | - | - | - | limits | 18; measured message "string or blob too big" [V-measured] | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#toobig |
| SQLITE_CONSTRAINT (19) | "an SQL constraint violation occurred ... Additional information about the failed constraint can be found by consulting the accompanying error message ... or by looking at the extended error code." [V] Also returned by xBestIndex to reject a plan [V]. Trigger RAISE gives SQLITE_CONSTRAINT [V lang_createtrigger]. Subset: MUST | - | - | STRICT type failures use SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE (see A.3) | constraints | 19 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#constraint |
| SQLITE_MISMATCH (20) | "a datatype mismatch ... SQLite is normally very forgiving ... But in a few cases, SQLite is strict about types." "The rowid of a table must be an integer. Attempt to set the rowid to anything other than an integer (or a NULL which will be automatically converted into the next available integer rowid) results in an SQLITE_MISMATCH error." [V]. Subset: MAYBE. [I] With all tables WITHOUT ROWID there is no rowid, so the rowid case cannot occur. Other sources of MISMATCH are not listed on these pages. | - | - | rowid tables only, per this text | rowid | 20 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#mismatch |
| SQLITE_MISUSE (21) | "the application uses any SQLite interface in a way that is undefined or unsupported ... Misuse detection is probabilistic. Applications should never depend on an SQLITE_MISUSE return value." [V]. Subset: NO (API-level) | "Applications should never depend on" | - | - | - | 21 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#misuse |
| SQLITE_NOLFS (22) | no large-file support [V]. Subset: NO | - | - | - | - | 22 | https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#nolfs |
