Minimum set a subset SQL engine plausibly must reproduce [I]: OK, ROW, DONE, ERROR (parse errors, unknown names, refused syntax), CONSTRAINT with CHECK / NOTNULL / PRIMARYKEY / UNIQUE / FOREIGNKEY / DATATYPE, TOOBIG, RANGE (bind index), ERROR_MISSING_COLLSEQ (unknown collation). Later: CONSTRAINT_TRIGGER and CONSTRAINT_PINNED (triggers), ABORT / ABORT_ROLLBACK (ROLLBACK with pending statements), BUSY / BUSY_SNAPSHOT / LOCKED (concurrency), READONLY (read-only open), INTERRUPT (cancellation), NOMEM / FULL (resource limits), SCHEMA. MISMATCH should not occur, because there are no rowid tables. Whether the oracle reports the extended code depends on the binding: "the C-language interfaces return primary result codes by default" [V], so the harness should read sqlite3_extended_errcode() [I].

### 1.8 PRAGMA statements

sql4s3 refuses every PRAGMA (decided). The table records what each one does, because the oracle must be pinned and because some defaults change ordinary SQL (§4).

#### PRAGMA statement framework (pragma.html)

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PRAGMA statement | "an SQL extension specific to SQLite and used to modify the operation of the SQLite library or to query the SQLite library for internal (non-table) data" [V] | "Specific pragma statements may be removed and others added in future releases of SQLite. There is no guarantee of backwards compatibility." [V] | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax |
| Unknown pragma | "No error messages are generated if an unknown pragma is issued. Unknown pragmas are simply ignored. This means if there is a typo in a pragma statement the library does not inform the user of the fact." [V] Measured: `pragma no_such_pragma; select 1;` gives `1` with no error [V-measured] | quoted | - | - | - | none (silently ignored) | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax |
| Prepare vs step timing | "Some pragmas take effect during the SQL compilation stage ... Whether or not the pragma runs during sqlite3_prepare() or sqlite3_step() depends on the pragma and on the specific release of SQLite." [V] | release-dependent | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax |
| EXPLAIN on PRAGMA | "PRAGMA statements that take effect during sqlite3_prepare() will behave the same way regardless of whether or not they are prefaced by "EXPLAIN"." [V] | - | - | - | EXPLAIN | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax |
| SQLITE_FCNTL_PRAGMA | VFS may "add new PRAGMA statements or to override the meaning of built-in PRAGMA statements" [V] | - | - | - | VFS | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax |
| Syntax | `PRAGMA [schema-name.]pragma-name [= pragma-value | (pragma-value)]`; value is signed-number, name or signed-literal; "The two syntaxes yield identical results." [V] Booleans: "1 yes true on / 0 no false off"; "Keyword arguments can optionally appear in quotes." "many pragmas return the number rather than the keyword." [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax |
| Schema prefix | "If the optional schema name is omitted, "main" is assumed. In some pragmas, the schema name is meaningless and is simply ignored." [V] | - | - | - | ATTACH | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax |
| PRAGMA functions (pragma_xxx table-valued functions) | "PRAGMAs that return results and that have no side-effects can be accessed from ordinary SELECT statements as table-valued functions ... same name as the PRAGMA with a 7-character "pragma_" prefix. The PRAGMA argument and schema, if any, are passed as arguments to the table-valued function, with the schema as an optional, last argument." [V] "Table-valued functions exist only for built-in PRAGMAs" [V]. Measured: read-only forms of flag pragmas also exist (e.g. `select * from pragma_foreign_keys`, `pragma_trusted_schema`, `pragma_encoding`) [V-measured] | changelog 3.16.0: "Added experimental support for PRAGMA functions." [V changes] | 3.16.0 (2017-01-02) [V both]; integrity_check, quick_check and foreign_key_check functions added in 3.20.0 [V changes] | - | table-valued functions, SELECT | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragfunc |
| Deprecated marker | "Pragmas whose names are struck through are deprecated. Do not use them. They exist for historical compatibility." (footnote 1): case_sensitive_like, count_changes, data_store_directory, default_cache_size, empty_result_callbacks, full_column_names, short_column_names, temp_store_directory [V] | quoted | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#toc |
| Non-standard-build marker | footnote 2: "only available in builds using non-standard compile-time options": parser_trace, vdbe_addoptrace, vdbe_debug, vdbe_listing, vdbe_trace [V] | quoted | - | - | SQLITE_DEBUG | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#toc |
| Testing-only marker | footnote 3: "used for testing SQLite and are not recommended for use in application programs": schema_version, stats, writable_schema [V] | quoted | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#toc |

#### PRAGMAs that change SQL semantics or results (one row each)

"Changes semantics?" is written at the start of the description.

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PRAGMA case_sensitive_like = boolean | YES. "installs a new application-defined LIKE function that is either case sensitive or insensitive". Default: off ("by default 'a' LIKE 'A' is true"). Overloads LIKE and GLOB via sqlite3_create_function(). It is write-only: there is no query form [V syntax only shows `= boolean`]. | "This pragma is deprecated and exists for backwards compatibility only. New applications should avoid using this pragma. Older applications should discontinue use of this pragma at the earliest opportunity. This pragma may be omitted from the build when SQLite is compiled using SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED." "the use of the case_sensitive_like pragma is discouraged." "can cause the database to appear to be corrupt" [V] | 3.2.3 (2005-08-21) [V changes] | - | LIKE, GLOB | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_case_sensitive_like |
| PRAGMA count_changes | YES (DML statements return a result row). "When count-changes is set, each of these commands returns a single row of data consisting of one integer value - the number of rows inserted, modified or deleted ... does not include any insertions, modifications or deletions performed by triggers, any changes made automatically by foreign key actions, or updates caused by an upsert." Default: not set [V]. Measured 0 [V-measured] | "deprecated and exists for backwards compatibility only ... may be omitted from the build when SQLite is compiled using SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED." [V] | 2.0.5 (2001-10-15) [V changes] | - | DML | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_count_changes |
| PRAGMA defer_foreign_keys | YES. "When the defer_foreign_keys PRAGMA is on, enforcement of all foreign key constraints is delayed until the outermost transaction is committed." Default OFF; "automatically switched off at each COMMIT or ROLLBACK". "Caution: Setting "defer_foreign_keys=0" resets the internal foreign-key tracking state of the database connection, so that it becomes possible to commit the current transaction even if there are foreign key violations." [V] | "Caution" | 3.8.0 (2013-08-26) [V changes] | - | foreign keys | CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY at COMMIT [I] | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_defer_foreign_keys |
| PRAGMA empty_result_callbacks | API-only (sqlite3_exec): "affects the sqlite3_exec() API only"; callback is invoked once with NULL when there are zero rows [V]. Default cleared. Measured 0 [V-measured] | "deprecated and exists for backwards compatibility only ... SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED." [V] | 2.0.6 (2001-10-19) [V changes] | - | sqlite3_exec | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_empty_result_callbacks |
| PRAGMA encoding | YES (storage encoding). Query returns 'UTF-8', 'UTF-16le' or 'UTF-16be'. Setting it only affects creation of a new main db. "It is not possible to change the text encoding of a database after it has been created and any attempt to do so will be silently ignored." Default: "determined by the API used to open the connection." "An attempt to ATTACH a database with a different text encoding from the "main" database will fail." [V]. Measured: UTF-8 [V-measured] | - | first changelog mention not found [V: no match] | - | ATTACH | silently ignored; ATTACH fails | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_encoding |
| PRAGMA foreign_keys | YES. "Query, set, or clear the enforcement of foreign key constraints." "This pragma is a no-op within a transaction". "Changing the foreign_keys setting affects the execution of all statements prepared using the database connection, including those prepared before the setting was changed." Default: "As of SQLite version 3.6.19, the default setting for foreign key enforcement is OFF. However, that might change in a future release of SQLite." "applications should set the foreign key enforcement flag as required by the application and not depend on the default setting." [V]. Measured 0 [V-measured] | "might change in a future release" | 3.6.19 (2009-10-14): "Foreign key constraints are disabled by default." [V changes] | - | SQLITE_DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS, DBCONFIG_ENABLE_FKEY | SQLITE_SCHEMA for legacy sqlite3_prepare statements [V] | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_foreign_keys |
| PRAGMA full_column_names | YES (result column names). The naming rules are: 1. AS clause; 2. "If the result is a general expression ... a copy of the expression text"; 3. short_column_names ON gives COLUMN; 4. "If both pragmas short_column_names and full_column_names are OFF then case (2) applies"; 5. TABLE.COLUMN [V]. Measured default 0 [V-measured] | "deprecated and exists for backwards compatibility only ... SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED." [V] | 2.0.2 (2001-10-09) [V changes] | - | result naming | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_full_column_names |
| PRAGMA short_column_names | YES (result column names), see full_column_names. Measured default 1 [V-measured] | "deprecated and exists for backwards compatibility only ... SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED." [V] | not found in changes | - | result naming | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_short_column_names |
| PRAGMA ignore_check_constraints = boolean | YES. "enables or disables the enforcement of CHECK constraints. The default setting is off, meaning that CHECK constraints are enforced by default." [V]. Measured 0 [V-measured] | - | not found in changes | - | CHECK | suppresses CONSTRAINT_CHECK | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_ignore_check_constraints |
| PRAGMA legacy_alter_table | YES (ALTER TABLE RENAME). On: "the ALTER TABLE RENAME command ... works as it did in SQLite 3.24.0 (2018-06-04) and earlier ... only rewrites the initial occurrence of the table name". Default OFF. "per-connection setting ... does not persist." "this flag is turned on temporarily while the sqlite3_module.xRename method is being run." [V]. Measured 0 [V-measured] | "provided as a work-around for older programs that contain code that expect the incomplete behavior of ALTER TABLE RENAME found in older versions of SQLite. New applications should leave this flag turned off." [V] | 3.25.2 (2018-09-25) [V changes; 3.26.0 also lists it] | - | ALTER TABLE, DBCONFIG_LEGACY_ALTER_TABLE | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_legacy_alter_table |
| PRAGMA legacy_file_format | NO (no-op). "This pragma no longer functions. It has become a no-op. The capabilities formerly provided by PRAGMA legacy_file_format are now available using the SQLITE_DBCONFIG_LEGACY_FILE_FORMAT option" [V] | "no longer functions ... no-op" | Made a no-op in 3.31.0 (2020-01-22) [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_legacy_file_format |
| PRAGMA query_only | YES. "any attempt to CREATE, DELETE, DROP, INSERT, or UPDATE will result in an SQLITE_READONLY error. However, the database is not truly read-only." [V] Default: off [I]; measured 0 [V-measured] | - | 3.8.0 (2013-08-26) [V changes] | - | - | SQLITE_READONLY | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_query_only |
| PRAGMA read_uncommitted | Only between connections that share a cache: "SERIALIZABLE will still be used except between connections that share a common page and schema cache." Default isolation SERIALIZABLE; "Cache sharing is disabled by default." [V] | - | not found | - | shared cache | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_read_uncommitted |
| PRAGMA recursive_triggers | YES. "Query, set, or clear the recursive trigger capability." "Support for recursive triggers was added in version 3.6.18 but was initially turned OFF by default, for compatibility. Recursive triggers may be turned on by default in future versions of SQLite." Depth limit SQLITE_MAX_TRIGGER_DEPTH / SQLITE_LIMIT_TRIGGER_DEPTH [V]. Measured 0 (even though compile_options lists DEFAULT_RECURSIVE_TRIGGERS, see G.j) [V-measured] | "initially turned OFF by default, for compatibility"; "may be turned on by default in future versions" | 3.6.18 (2009-09-11) [V both] | - | triggers | SQLITE_SCHEMA for legacy prepare [V] | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_recursive_triggers |
| PRAGMA reverse_unordered_selects | YES (row order when there is no ORDER BY). "causes many SELECT statements without an ORDER BY clause to emit their results in the reverse order ... the query planner may sometimes choose an algorithm that is not easily reversed". "SQLite makes no guarantees about the order of results if a SELECT omits the ORDER BY clause." [V]. Measured 0 [V-measured] | debugging aid | 3.6.12 (2009-03-31) [V changes] | - | DBCONFIG_REVERSE_SCANORDER | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_reverse_unordered_selects |
| PRAGMA trusted_schema | YES (can reject schema expressions). Decides "whether or not SQL functions and virtual tables that have not been security audited are allowed to be run by views, triggers, or in expressions of the schema such as CHECK constraints, DEFAULT clauses, generated columns, expression indexes, and/or partial indexes." [V] | "In order to maintain backwards compatibility, this setting is ON by default. ... all applications are encouraged to switch this setting off on every database connection as soon as that connection is opened." [V] | 3.31.0 (2020-01-22) [V changes] | - | SQLITE_INNOCUOUS functions | error when a non-innocuous function is used in schema [I] | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_trusted_schema |
| PRAGMA writable_schema = boolean / RESET | YES. "When this pragma is on, and the SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE flag is off, then the sqlite_schema table can be changed using ordinary UPDATE, INSERT, and DELETE statements." RESET disables it and reloads the schema [V] | testing-only (footnote 3); "Warning: misuse of this pragma can easily result in a corrupt database file." [V]; 3.44.0: "DEFENSIVE setting now prevents PRAGMA writable_schema from being turned on" [V changes] | first mention 3.28.0 (DBCONFIG verb) [V changes] | - | DEFENSIVE | measured: silently stays 0 under defensive [V-measured] | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_writable_schema |
| PRAGMA schema_version (write) | YES (dangerous). "Subverting this mechanism ... may cause SQL statement to run using an obsolete schema, which can lead to incorrect answers and/or database corruption ... attempts to change the value of schema_version are a silent no-op when defensive mode is enabled" [V] | "Warning: Misuse of this pragma can result in database corruption." testing-only footnote 3 [V] | 3.3.10 first mention [V changes] | - | DEFENSIVE | silent no-op under defensive | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_schema_version |
| PRAGMA automatic_index | Plan only (results unchanged, but [I] the row order without ORDER BY may change). "Automatic indexing is enabled by default as of version 3.7.17 (2013-05-20), but this might change in future releases of SQLite." [V]. Measured 1 [V-measured] | "might change in future releases" | SQLITE_DEFAULT_AUTOMATIC_INDEX 3.8.0 [V changes] | - | planner | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_automatic_index |
| PRAGMA cell_size_check | NO SQL semantics change (corruption detection). "turned off by default" [V]. Measured 0 [V-measured] | - | 3.8.11 (2015-07-27) [V changes] | - | - | SQLITE_CORRUPT earlier [I] | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_cell_size_check |
| PRAGMA journal_mode (OFF) | Partly YES: with OFF, "The ROLLBACK command no longer works; it behaves in an undefined way." "Because OFF journaling mode allows the database file to be corrupted using ordinary SQL, it is disabled when SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE is enabled." In-memory db: "either MEMORY or OFF". Default DELETE [V]. Full row in A.7. | "undefined way" | 3.5.9 (2008-05-14) [V changes] | - | DEFENSIVE | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_journal_mode |

[V] | "deprecated and exists for backwards compatibility only ... SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED." [V] | - | - | - | error if bad directory | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_temp_store_directory |
| PRAGMA threads | sqlite3_limit(SQLITE_LIMIT_WORKER_THREADS); default 0 [V] | - | 3.8.7 (2014-10-17) [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_threads |
| PRAGMA vdbe_addoptrace | debug only [V] | footnote 2 | - | - | SQLITE_DEBUG | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_vdbe_addoptrace |
| PRAGMA vdbe_debug | shorthand for the three other vdbe_* pragmas [V] | footnote 2 | - | - | SQLITE_DEBUG | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_vdbe_debug |
| PRAGMA vdbe_listing | debug only [V] | footnote 2 | - | - | SQLITE_DEBUG | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_vdbe_listing |
| PRAGMA vdbe_trace | debug only [V] | footnote 2 | - | - | SQLITE_DEBUG | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_vdbe_trace |
| PRAGMA wal_autocheckpoint | auto-checkpoint interval; default 1000 [V] | - | not found | - | WAL | - | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_wal_autocheckpoint |
| PRAGMA wal_checkpoint[(PASSIVE|FULL|RESTART|TRUNCATE|NOOP)] | runs a checkpoint and returns 3 integer columns (busy, log pages, checkpointed pages; -1 if not WAL) [V] | - | 3.7.6 (2011-04-12) enhancement [V changes] | - | WAL | busy flag | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_wal_checkpoint |

### 1.9 sqlite3_db_config options, limits and compile-time options

#### sqlite3_db_config options (c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html, c3ref/db_config.html)

General: "Calls to sqlite3_db_config() return SQLITE_OK if and only if the call is considered successful." [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/db_config.html] "New configuration options may be added in future releases of SQLite. Existing configuration options might be discontinued." [V]. Most options take (int, int*): 1 enables, 0 disables, -1 leaves the setting unchanged; the pointer receives 0 or 1 [V #dbconfigarguments]. Measured defaults: the CLI run with `-unsafe-testing` (closest to the library defaults) vs. the plain CLI [V-measured].

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_MAINDBNAME (1000) | rename the "main" schema; one const char* argument [V] | - | 3.15.0 (2016-10-14) [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigmaindbname |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_LOOKASIDE (1001) | lookaside allocator buf/sz/cnt; returns SQLITE_BUSY if lookaside is in use [V] | - | 3.6.1 (db_config itself) [V changes] | - | - | BUSY | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfiglookaside |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_FKEY (1002) | "enable or disable the enforcement of foreign key constraints. This is the same setting that is enabled or disabled by the PRAGMA foreign_keys statement." [V] Default: not stated on this page; same as PRAGMA foreign_keys (OFF unless SQLITE_DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS) [I]. Measured off [V-measured] | - | not found | - | foreign keys | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigenablefkey |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_TRIGGER (1003) | enable/disable triggers; "since SQLite version 3.35.0, TEMP triggers are still allowed even if this option is off" [V]. Measured on [V-measured] | "Originally this option disabled all triggers." | 3.35.0 change [V changes] | - | triggers | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigenabletrigger |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_VIEW (1015) | enable/disable views (TEMP views still allowed since 3.35.0) [V]. Measured on [V-measured] | - | first mention 3.30.0 [V changes] | - | views | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigenableview |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_FTS3_TOKENIZER (1004) | two-argument fts3_tokenizer(); "disabled by default" [V] | - | 3.12.0 [V compile] | - | FTS3 | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigenablefts3tokenizer |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_LOAD_EXTENSION (1005) | enable only the C-API sqlite3_load_extension() [V]. Measured CLI: on (the CLI enables it) [V-measured] | - | 3.13.0 (2016-05-18) [V changes] | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigenableloadextension |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_NO_CKPT_ON_CLOSE (1006) | disable checkpoint on close; "zero (the default)" [V] | - | 3.16.0 [V changes] | - | WAL | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfignockptonclose |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_QPSG (1007) | query planner stability guarantee: "a single SQL query statement will always use the same algorithm regardless of values of bound parameters" [V]. Default off (SQLITE_ENABLE_QPSG makes it on) [V compile]. Measured off [V-measured] | - | 3.20.0 (2017-08-01) [V changes] | - | planner | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigenableqpsg |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_TRIGGER_EQP (1008) | include trigger programs in EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN; default clear [V] | - | not found | - | EXPLAIN | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigtriggereqp |

| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE (1010) | "When the defensive flag is enabled, language features that allow ordinary SQL to deliberately corrupt the database file are disabled. The disabled features include but are not limited to the following: The PRAGMA writable_schema=ON statement. The PRAGMA journal_mode=OFF statement. The PRAGMA schema_version=N statement. Writes to the sqlite_dbpage virtual table. Direct writes to shadow tables." [V]. Library default not stated on this page; measured: off with `-unsafe-testing`, on in the plain CLI [V-measured] | - | 3.26.0 (2018-12-01) [V changes]; extended in 3.39.4, 3.40.0, 3.44.0 [V changes] | - | - | silent no-ops (schema_version) [V pragma] | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigdefensive |

Measured off [V-measured] | "legacy ... misfeature" | 3.29.0 (2019-07-10): "Both default to "on" for legacy compatibility" [V changes] | - | SQLITE_DQS | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigdqsdml |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DQS_DDL (1014) | "activates or deactivates the legacy double-quoted string literal misfeature for DDL statements, such as CREATE TABLE and CREATE INDEX. Measured off [V-measured] | "legacy ... misfeature" | 3.29.0 [V changes] | - | SQLITE_DQS | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigdqsddl |

#### Limits (limits.html, c3ref/c_limit_attached.html)

"Some limits can be changed at run-time on a per-connection basis using the sqlite3_limit() interface" [V]. Run-time limits can only lower the value ("can be lowered at run-time") [V]. "versions of SQLite since about release 3.5.8 (2008-04-16) have well-defined limits" [V]; sqlite3_limit() was added in 3.5.8 [V changes]. Measured values come from `.limit` in the oracle CLI [V-measured]. Measured error messages came from lowering each limit with `.limit` [V-measured].

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SQLITE_MAX_LENGTH / SQLITE_LIMIT_LENGTH | max bytes in a string or BLOB, default 1,000,000,000; "The current implementation will only support a string or BLOB length up to 2^31-3 or 2147483645"; "the SQLITE_MAX_LENGTH parameter also determines the maximum number of bytes in a row" [V]. c3ref: "The maximum size of any string or BLOB or table row, in bytes." Measured 1000000000 | "some built-in functions such as hex() might fail well before that point" [V] | - | applies to whole rows [V] | - | SQLITE_TOOBIG [V rescode]; measured "string or blob too big" (randomblob(200), printf width 300 with limit 100). Note: with limit 5, `select 'abcdef'` and `zeroblob(10)` still returned values [V-measured]; [I] there may be a minimum or exemptions for literals and zeroblob, so do not assume every value is checked | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_length |
| SQLITE_MAX_SQL_LENGTH / SQLITE_LIMIT_SQL_LENGTH | max bytes of SQL text, default 1,000,000,000 [V]. Measured 1000000000 | - | - | - | - | SQLITE_TOOBIG [V rescode]; measured "string or blob too big (18)" | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_sql_length |
| SQLITE_MAX_COLUMN / SQLITE_LIMIT_COLUMN | upper bound on columns in a table, index or view, SET terms, result columns, GROUP BY / ORDER BY terms, and "The number of values in an INSERT statement"; default 2000, max 32767 [V]. Measured 2000 | - | - | - | - | measured "too many columns in result set" (SQLITE_ERROR [I]); with limit 2, even schema load failed: "malformed database schema (sqlite_master) - too many columns on sqlite_master (11)" [V-measured] | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_column |
| Tables in a join | "SQLite does not support joins containing more than 64 tables." "there is no mechanism to raise or lower the limit" [V] | - | - | - | joins | error [I] | https://sqlite.org/limits.html |
| SQLITE_MAX_EXPR_DEPTH / SQLITE_LIMIT_EXPR_DEPTH | max expression tree depth, default 1000; 0 = unlimited (then sqlite3_limit is a no-op) [V]. c3ref adds "and the maximum nesting depth for subqueries and VIEWs" [V]. Measured 1000 | recommended build option uses SQLITE_MAX_EXPR_DEPTH=0 [V compile] | - | - | - | measured "Expression tree is too large (maximum depth 3)" | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_expr_depth |
| SQLITE_MAX_FUNCTION_ARG / SQLITE_LIMIT_FUNCTION_ARG | max function arguments; "For many years, the default value was about 100 but the default was raised to 1000 beginning with SQLite version 3.48.0"; hard bound 32767 [V]. Measured 1000 | - | 1000 since 3.48.0 (2025-01-14) [V limits] | - | - | measured "too many arguments on function max" | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_function_arg |
| SQLITE_MAX_COMPOUND_SELECT / SQLITE_LIMIT_COMPOUND_SELECT | max terms in a compound SELECT, default 500 [V]. Measured 500 | - | - | - | compound SELECT; [I] multi-row VALUES? (compile.html says multi-row INSERT VALUES "is implemented internally as a compound SELECT" [V #omit_compound_select]) | measured "too many terms in compound SELECT" | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_compound_select |
| SQLITE_MAX_LIKE_PATTERN_LENGTH / SQLITE_LIMIT_LIKE_PATTERN_LENGTH | max LIKE/GLOB pattern bytes, default 50000 [V]. Measured 50000 | - | - | - | LIKE, GLOB | measured runtime "LIKE or GLOB pattern too complex" | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_like_pattern_length |
| SQLITE_MAX_VARIABLE_NUMBER / SQLITE_LIMIT_VARIABLE_NUMBER | max host parameter number; "defaults to 999 for SQLite versions prior to 3.32.0 (2020-05-22) or 32766 for SQLite versions after 3.32.0." [V]. **Measured 250000 in the Homebrew build** (compile option MAX_VARIABLE_NUMBER=250000) [V-measured] | - | 32766 since 3.32.0 [V changes] | - | parameters | measured "variable number must be between ?1 and ?2" | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_variable_number |
| SQLITE_MAX_TRIGGER_DEPTH / SQLITE_LIMIT_TRIGGER_DEPTH | default 1000; "only meaningful if recursive triggers are enabled" [V]. c3ref: "The maximum depth of recursion for triggers, and the maximum nesting depth for separate triggers." Measured 1000 | - | 3.6.18 [V] | - | triggers | not stated | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_trigger_depth |
| SQLITE_MAX_ATTACHED / SQLITE_LIMIT_ATTACHED | default 10; "cannot be increased above 125" [V]. Measured 10 | - | max raised 30 to 62 in 3.7.6 [V changes] | - | ATTACH | not stated | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_attached |
| SQLITE_MAX_PAGE_COUNT | max pages; largest and (since 3.45.0) default 4294967294; about 17.5 TB at 4096-byte pages, about 281 TB at 65536 [V]. Measured 0xfffffffe | - | default changed in 3.45.0 [V changes] | - | max_page_count pragma | SQLITE_FULL [V] | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_page_count |
| Rows in a table | "The theoretical maximum number of rows in a table is 2^64 ... This limit is unreachable" [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/limits.html |
| Max database size | 4294967294 pages, about 2.8e+14 bytes; "This particular upper bound is untested" [V] | "untested" | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/limits.html |
| Tables in a schema / SQLITE_LIMIT_SCHEMA | "Beginning in SQLite version 3.54.0 (2026-10-09) the number of objects in the schema can be capped using the SQLITE_LIMIT_SCHEMA value to sqlite3_limit(). The default maximum is 10 million" [V]. c3ref: "maximum number of objects (tables, indexes, triggers, and views)". Measured 10000000 | - | 3.54.0 [V both] | - | - | not stated | https://sqlite.org/limits.html ; https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_limit_attached.html#sqlitelimitschema |
| SQLITE_LIMIT_VDBE_OP | "maximum number of instructions in a virtual machine program ... an SQLITE_NOMEM error is returned." [V]. Not on limits.html. Measured 250000000 | - | - | - | - | SQLITE_NOMEM [V] | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_limit_attached.html#sqlitelimitvdbeop |
| SQLITE_LIMIT_PARSER_DEPTH | "maximum depth of the LALR(1) parser stack" [V]. Default not documented; measured 2500 | - | 3.53.0 (2026-04-09) [V changes] | - | parser | not stated | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_limit_attached.html#sqlitelimitparserdepth |
| SQLITE_LIMIT_WORKER_THREADS | max auxiliary threads per statement [V]. Measured 0 (MAX_WORKER_THREADS=8) | - | 3.8.7 [V changes] | - | PRAGMA threads | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_limit_attached.html#sqlitelimitworkerthreads |
| SQLITE_LIMIT_TRIGGER_STEPS | "maximum number of SQL statements that can be contained within a single trigger" [V]. Default not documented; measured 65000 | - | 3.54.0 [V changes] | - | triggers | not stated | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_limit_attached.html#sqlitelimittriggersteps |
| YYSTACKDEPTH | max LALR(1) stack depth, default 100 [V compile]; not changeable by sqlite3_limit [V] | - | - | - | parser | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#yystackdepth |
| SQLITE_JSON_MAX_DEPTH | max JSON nesting depth, default 1000 [V] | - | - | - | JSON | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#json_max_depth |
| SQLITE_FTS3_MAX_EXPR_DEPTH | default 12 [V] | - | 3.8.0 [V changes] | - | FTS3 | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#fts3_max_expr_depth |
| SQLITE_MAX_ALLOCATION_SIZE | max single allocation, default 2,147,483,391 (0x7ffffeff) [V] | - | 3.40.0 [V changes] | - | - | NOMEM [I] | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#max_allocation_size |
| SQLITE_MAX_MEMORY | total memory cap; "will result in an out-of-memory error" [V] | - | 3.18.0 [V changes] | - | MEMSTATUS | NOMEM | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#max_memory |

This gives a storage range of -9223372036854775808 to +9223372036854775807, inclusive." [V] Changelog: "Allow the value -9223372036854775808 as an integer literal" [V changes]; CAST of a float above +9223372036854775807 gives "the largest possible integer" [V changes] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/floatingpoint.html |

#### Compile-time options (compile.html) — one row each

Columns are compressed. "Sem" in the description means: YES = changes SQL semantics or accepted syntax; no = performance, platform or API only.

**A.10.1 Recommended options (exact wording, https://sqlite.org/compile.html#rcmd)**

Intro: "The following compile-time options are recommended for applications that are able to use them, in order to minimize the number of CPU cycles and the bytes of memory used by SQLite. Not all of these compile-time options are usable by every application." "It is impossible to test every possible combination of compile-time options for SQLite. But the following set of compile-time options is one configuration that is always fully tested." [V]

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SQLITE_DQS=0 (recommended) | "This setting disables the double-quoted string literal misfeature." Sem YES [V] | "misfeature" | recommended since 3.29.0 [V changes] | - | DBCONFIG_DQS_* | SQLITE_ERROR "no such column" [V-measured] | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#dqs |
| SQLITE_THREADSAFE=0 (recommended) | "causes all of the mutex and thread-safety logic in SQLite to be omitted ... about 2% faster ... SQLite can never be used by more than a single thread at a time" [V]. Sem no | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#threadsafe |
| SQLITE_DEFAULT_MEMSTATUS=0 (recommended) | "causes the sqlite3_status() interfaces that track memory usage to be disabled" [V]. Sem no | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#default_memstatus |
| SQLITE_DEFAULT_WAL_SYNCHRONOUS=1 (recommended) | "it is recommended that the synchronous setting be changed from FULL to NORMAL when switching to WAL mode. This compile-time option will accomplish that." [V]. Sem no | - | 3.12.0 (2016-03-29) [V changes] | - | WAL | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#default_wal_synchronous |
| SQLITE_LIKE_DOESNT_MATCH_BLOBS (recommended) | "Historically, SQLite has allowed BLOB operands to the LIKE and GLOB operators ... When this option is set, it means that the LIKE and GLOB operators always return FALSE if either operand is a BLOB." Sem YES [V] | "Historically" | 3.10.0 (2016-01-06) [V changes] | - | LIKE, GLOB | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#like_doesnt_match_blobs |
| SQLITE_MAX_EXPR_DEPTH=0 (recommended) | "disables all checking of the expression parse-tree depth" [V]. Sem: removes an error | - | - | - | - | removes the depth error | https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_expr_depth |
| SQLITE_OMIT_DECLTYPE (recommended) | "By omitting the (seldom-needed) ability to return the declared type of columns from the result set of query, prepared statements can be made to consume less memory." [V]. Sem no (API) | - | - | - | sqlite3_column_decltype | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_decltype |
| SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED (recommended) | "Omitting deprecated interfaces and features will not help SQLite to run any faster. It will reduce the library footprint, however. And it is the right thing to do." [V]. Removes deprecated pragmas | "the right thing to do" | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_deprecated |
| SQLITE_OMIT_PROGRESS_CALLBACK (recommended) | removes a conditional from the bytecode inner loop [V] | - | - | - | progress handler | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_progress_callback |
| SQLITE_OMIT_SHARED_CACHE (recommended) | "allows many conditionals in performance-critical sections of the code to be eliminated" [V] | "recommended for most applications" [V #omit_shared_cache] | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_shared_cache |
| SQLITE_OMIT_AUTOINIT (recommended) | the application must call sqlite3_initialize(); otherwise "the likely result will be a segfault" [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_autoinit |
| SQLITE_STRICT_SUBTYPE=1 (recommended) | "causes an error to be raised if an application defined function that does not have the SQLITE_RESULT_SUBTYPE property invokes the sqlite3_result_subtype() interface" [V] | "This recommended option" [V #strict_subtype] | 3.45.0 (SQLITE_RESULT_SUBTYPE) [V changes] | - | app-defined functions | "SQL error" [V] | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#strict_subtype |
| Effect of the full set | "approximately 3% smaller and use about 5% fewer CPU cycles" [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#rcmd |
| SQLITE_CUSTOM_INCLUDE | "Defining SQLITE_CUSTOM_INCLUDE=myconfig.h ... will cause sqlite3.c to include myconfig.h early" [V] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#rcmd |

Sem YES [V] | "breaking backwards compatibility"; "bug" | 3.10.0 [V changes] | - | LIKE | - | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#like_doesnt_match_blobs |

**A.10.4 Size-limit options** (listed in compile.html section 5 and documented on limits.html; see A.9): SQLITE_MAX_ATTACHED, SQLITE_MAX_COLUMN, SQLITE_MAX_COMPOUND_SELECT, SQLITE_MAX_EXPR_DEPTH, SQLITE_MAX_FUNCTION_ARG, SQLITE_MAX_LENGTH, SQLITE_MAX_LIKE_PATTERN_LENGTH, SQLITE_MAX_PAGE_COUNT, SQLITE_MAX_SQL_LENGTH, SQLITE_MAX_VARIABLE_NUMBER [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#controlfeatures section 5]. Not changeable by sqlite3_limit(): SQLITE_FTS3_MAX_EXPR_DEPTH, SQLITE_JSON_MAX_DEPTH, SQLITE_MAX_ALLOCATION_SIZE, SQLITE_MAX_MEMORY, SQLITE_MAX_MMAP_SIZE, SQLITE_PRINTF_PRECISION_LIMIT, SQLITE_TRACE_SIZE_LIMIT, YYSTACKDEPTH [V]. compile.html does not list SQLITE_MAX_TRIGGER_DEPTH, SQLITE_MAX_VDBE_OP or SQLITE_MAX_SCHEMA in section 5 [V], but the measured build reports MAX_TRIGGER_DEPTH=1000, MAX_VDBE_OP=250000000 and MAX_SCHEMA=10000000 [V-measured].

**A.10.8 OMIT options.** General status, quoted exactly [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omitfeatures]:
- "If any of these options are defined, then the same set of SQLITE_OMIT_* options must also be defined when using the Lemon parser generator tool to generate the parse.c file ... these options may only be used when the library is built from canonical source, not from the amalgamation. Some SQLITE_OMIT_* options might work, or appear to work, when used with the amalgamation. But this is not guaranteed."
- "Important Note: The SQLITE_OMIT_* options may not work with the amalgamation. SQLITE_OMIT_* compile-time options usually work correctly only when SQLite is built from canonical source files."
- "The SQLITE_OMIT_* options are unsupported. By this we mean that an SQLITE_OMIT_* option that omits code from the build in the current release might become a no-op in the next release. Or the other way around ... Also, not all SQLITE_OMIT_* options are tested. Some SQLITE_OMIT_* options might cause SQLite to malfunction and/or provide incorrect answers."
- "Important Note: The SQLITE_OMIT_* compile-time options are mostly unsupported."
- "The macros in this section do not require values." `-DSQLITE_OMIT_ALTERTABLE`, `=1` and `=0` "all have the same effect".

Every row below therefore carries the status "unsupported; may not work with the amalgamation" [V].

#### Other C-API pages in scope

| Feature | One-line description | Doc status wording (quoted) | Introduced | STRICT / WITHOUT ROWID differences | Depends on | Failure / result codes | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| sqlite3_libversion / sqlite3_version / sqlite3_sourceid / sqlite3_libversion_number | same values as the SQLITE_VERSION, SQLITE_VERSION_NUMBER and SQLITE_SOURCE_ID macros; "if SQLite is built using an edited copy of the amalgamation, then the last four characters of the hash might be different" [V]. SQL equivalents: sqlite_version(), sqlite_source_id() [V]. Measured: `3.54.1`, `2026-10-16 14:45:20 555e07ef33f69a0957d24d8e555b702464a131d6f1607f4c3c1e4551e8e6ca76` (matches the 3.54.1 SQLITE_SOURCE_ID in changes.html) [V-measured] | - | - | - | - | - | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/libversion.html |
| sqlite3_blob_open | incremental BLOB I/O on `SELECT zColumn FROM zDb.zTable WHERE rowid = iRow`. It fails with SQLITE_ERROR if the db, table or column does not exist, if the "Table zTable is a WITHOUT ROWID table", if the row is missing, if the value is not TEXT or BLOB, if the column is in an index / PK / UNIQUE (read/write), or if "Foreign key constraints are enabled, column zColumn is part of a child key definition and the blob is being opened for read/write access". An expired handle (row changed) gives SQLITE_ABORT on read and write [V] | - | - | **Not usable on WITHOUT ROWID tables** [V], so it is unusable for sql4s3 tables [I] | rowid | SQLITE_ERROR, SQLITE_ABORT, SQLITE_MISUSE | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/blob_open.html |
| sqlite3_create_collation(_v2/16) | register a collation; eTextRep is one of SQLITE_UTF8 / UTF16LE / UTF16BE / UTF16 / UTF16_ALIGNED; "Collation names that compare equal according to sqlite3_strnicmp() are considered to be the same name." Required properties: symmetric equality, transitive, antisymmetric ordering; if they fail, "the behavior of SQLite is undefined." "A collating function must always return the same answer given the same inputs." [V] | "This is different from every other SQLite interface. The inconsistency is unfortunate but cannot be changed without breaking backwards compatibility." (xDestroy is not called when _v2 fails) [V] | - | - | COLLATE | MISSING_COLLSEQ when absent [V rescode] | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/create_collation.html |
| sqlite3_set_authorizer | callback at prepare time; return SQLITE_OK, SQLITE_IGNORE or SQLITE_DENY; "SQLITE_DENY to cause the entire SQL statement to be rejected with an error"; "The authorizer is disabled by default." "invoked only during sqlite3_prepare() or its variants" [V]. Action code SQLITE_PRAGMA = 19 with args (Pragma Name, 1st arg or NULL) [V c_alter_table]. Other codes: CREATE_TABLE 2, INSERT 18, READ 20, SELECT 21, TRANSACTION 22, UPDATE 23, ATTACH 24, ALTER_TABLE 26, FUNCTION 31, RECURSIVE 33, etc. [V] | - | 3.54.0: "The authorizer callback is now invoked for SQL functions found in the DEFAULT clauses of CREATE TABLE statements" [V changes] | - | - | SQLITE_AUTH [V rescode] | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/set_authorizer.html |

---

## 2. Legacy and backward-compatibility flags

This section lists every place where the docs flag legacy, compatibility, quirk, misfeature, deprecated or not-recommended behaviour. Each entry gets one of two verdicts:
- **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX:** sql4s3 refuses some syntax, and the legacy behaviour cannot be reached.
- **UNAVOIDABLE:** the behaviour belongs to ordinary syntax that sql4s3 will accept, so it must be matched exactly.

§2.1 and §2.2 summarise the verdicts. §2.3 to §2.7 give the full lists by area, with the exact wording and URLs.

### 2.1 Summary: legacy behaviour of ordinary syntax that must be matched

None of these can be refused by rejecting syntax. They come with expressions, comparisons, aggregates and constraints that any useful subset contains.

| # | Behaviour | Doc wording / rule | URL |
|---|---|---|---|
| U1 | **Type rules in comparisons.** Storage classes order as NULL < INTEGER/REAL < TEXT < BLOB. Affinity is applied to operands before a comparison, including affinity from STRICT columns. Literals have no affinity, so `SELECT 1='1'` is 0. | "SQLite says that the following query returns false: SELECT 1='1';" [V] | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#compaff, https://sqlite.org/quirks.html |
| U2 | **No hexadecimal in run-time conversions.** `CAST('0x10' AS INTEGER)` is 0. Hex text is "not well-formed" for affinity. | "For backwards compatibility, the "0x" hexadecimal integer notation is only understood by the SQL language parser, not by the type conversions routines." [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#hexint |
| U3 | **Integer overflow.** `+ - *` switch to floating point when integer arithmetic would overflow. `abs(-9223372036854775808)` raises an error. `sum()` raises "integer overflow" only while every input so far is an integer. CAST saturates at the 64-bit limits. | "perform integer arithmetic if both operands are integers and no overflow would result" [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops, https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#sumunc, https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#castexpr |
| U4 | **Division by zero.** The result is NULL, not an error. | "Division by zero gives a result of NULL." [V] | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html (§5) |
| U5 | **Non-numeric text in arithmetic.** It becomes 0 or 0.0, not an error. | "An operand on a mathematical operator that does not look in any way numeric and is not NULL is converted to 0 or 0.0." [V] | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html (§5) |
| U6 | **Boolean contexts.** WHERE, ON, HAVING, CASE WHEN and CHECK cast their value to NUMERIC, so `'english'` is false and `'1english'` is true. A CHECK that evaluates to NULL passes. | [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#booleanexpr, https://sqlite.org/lang_createtable.html#ckconst |
| U7 | **NULL ordering.** NULLs sort first in ASC order and last in DESC order (overridable with NULLS FIRST/LAST). NULLs are equal for DISTINCT, GROUP BY and UNION, but distinct in UNIQUE. | "This seems somewhat arbitrary, but the desire to be compatible with other engines outweighed that objection." [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#nullslast, https://sqlite.org/nulls.html |
| U8 | **ASCII-only case folding.** This applies to LIKE, NOCASE, upper() and lower(). | "SQLite only understands upper/lower case for ASCII characters by default." [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#like, https://sqlite.org/quirks.html, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#collation |
| U9 | **LIKE and GLOB on BLOBs.** The BLOB operands are cast to text. The oracle build lacks `SQLITE_LIKE_DOESNT_MATCH_BLOBS`. | "Historically, SQLite has allowed BLOB operands to the LIKE and GLOB operators" [V] | https://sqlite.org/compile.html#like_doesnt_match_blobs |
| U10 | **NUL characters inside text.** length() and quote() stop at the first NUL, and NOCASE treats NUL as a terminator. | "The use of NUL characters in SQL text strings is not recommended." [V] | https://sqlite.org/nulinstr.html |
| U11 | **sum() and total().** sum() of no rows is NULL; total() of no rows is 0.0. avg() is always REAL. | "The non-standard total() function is provided as a convenient way to work around this design problem in the SQL language." [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#sumunc |
| U12 | **Result column names without AS.** Clients see these names, yet the docs leave them unspecified. | "If there is no AS clause then the name of the column is unspecified and may change from one release of SQLite to the next." [V] | https://sqlite.org/c3ref/column_name.html |
| U13 | **Floating-point to text.** The default is 17-digit fallback rounding; before 3.53.0 it was 15 digits. | §6.4 | https://sqlite.org/floatingpoint.html#*fpdigits |
| U14 | **Empty IN list.** `x IN ()` is allowed and false. | "SQLite allows the parenthesized list ... to be an empty list but most other SQL database engines and the SQL92 standard require the list to contain at least one element." [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#in_op |
| U15 | **Recursive CTE row order** without ORDER BY. | The queue "becomes a FIFO if the ORDER BY clause is omitted, but applications should not depend on that fact since it might change" [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_with.html#recursivecte |
| U16 | **`recursive_triggers` default (off).** It decides whether rows deleted by REPLACE fire delete triggers, which matters once triggers exist. | "initially turned OFF by default, for compatibility" [V] | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_recursive_triggers |
| U17 | **`trusted_schema` default (on in the library).** It decides which functions schema expressions may use. | "In order to maintain backwards compatibility, this setting is ON by default." [V] | https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_trusted_schema |
| U18 | **JSON functions given a BLOB that is not JSONB.** The BLOB is read as text JSON. | "For backwards bug-compatibility, the (formerly incorrect) legacy behavior of interpreting BLOBs as text JSON if no other interpretation works is hereby documented and is officially supported" [V] | https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jblobbug |
| U19 | **CHECK sees the value after affinity is applied.** | 3.32.0: "INSERT and UPDATE statements now always apply column affinity before computing CHECK constraints. This bug fix could, in theory, cause problems for legacy databases" [V] | https://sqlite.org/changes.html |
| U20 | **log(X) is base 10.** It applies only if log() is offered. | "Compatibility note: SQLite works like PostgreSQL in that the log() function computes a base-10 logarithm." [V] | https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#log |

U16 and U17 are oracle defaults, not syntax. sql4s3 adopts one fixed value for each and pins the oracle to it (§4). [I]

### 2.2 Summary: legacy behaviour that rejection removes

These are the rejection rules, gathered from the per-area lists. Items marked "decided" are already project decisions. [I]
- **Table kinds:**
  - rowid tables, which bring PK NULLs, INTEGER PRIMARY KEY aliasing, the `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY DESC` quirk, AUTOINCREMENT, sqlite_sequence, and `rowid`/`oid`/`_rowid_` (decided);
  - non-STRICT tables, which bring flexible typing and type-name affinity rules (decided);
  - the ANY type (decided);
  - CREATE TABLE AS SELECT;
  - duplicate table options.
- **Quoting:**
  - double-quoted string literals (decided; the oracle agrees with `SQLITE_DQS=0`, except in DEFAULT clauses, see §4.2);
  - `[x]` and `` `x` `` identifiers (decided);
  - single-quoted identifiers;
  - unquoted keywords used as identifiers (wholly or partly);
  - `TRUE`/`FALSE` as object names.
- **Parameters:** bare `?` and `$name` parameters (optional).
- **SELECT:**
  - bare columns in aggregate queries;
  - `LIMIT a, b`;
  - non-standard join spellings, ON/USING on comma joins, and outer joins without ON;
  - comma joins mixed with outer joins without parentheses;
  - `INDEXED BY` and `NOT INDEXED`;
  - `MATERIALIZED` hints (or accept and ignore them);
  - a self-referencing CTE without `RECURSIVE`;
  - order-sensitive aggregates without an ORDER BY inside the aggregate.
- **INSERT and UPDATE:**
  - `REPLACE INTO` and the REPLACE conflict algorithm;
  - `INSERT ... SELECT` whose width does not match;
  - repeated columns in an UPDATE SET.
- **Schema:**
  - `MATCH` in foreign keys, and the deferral spellings that are in fact immediate;
  - a conflict clause on a table CHECK;
  - non-deterministic functions in CHECK;
  - `UPDATE OF` naming an unknown column;
  - TEMP triggers;
  - views without a column list.
- **Other statements:**
  - every `PRAGMA` (decided);
  - `EXPLAIN`;
  - `printf()` (use `format()`), `substring()` and `if()` aliases (optional);
  - format() `%n`, `%p`, and the `-0` flag pair.

### 2.3 Types, literals and expressions

- [V] Flexible typing: "Flexible typing is a feature of SQLite, not a bug." The docs also say: "In retrospect, perhaps it would have been less confusing if SQLite had merely implemented an ANY datatype". https://sqlite.org/quirks.html, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html. Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX:** non-STRICT tables (decided). Affinity still applies to STRICT columns and to CAST, so the comparison part stays (U1).
- [V] "For the purposes of this paragraph, hexadecimal integer literals are not considered well-formed and are stored as TEXT. (This is done for historical compatibility with versions of SQLite prior to version 3.8.6 ...)" https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affinity. Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** (U2). In a STRICT INTEGER column, the text '0x10' cannot be converted losslessly, so it should raise `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE`. [I]
- [V] "(Historical note: The "BLOB" type affinity used to be called "NONE". ...)" https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affinity. Verdict: naming only. The CAST table on lang_expr still says "NONE".
- [V] Affinity of compound views is "indeterminate": "The choice might vary across different versions of SQLite." https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affcompoundview. Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX:** compound SELECTs used as a view or FROM subquery whose arms have different column affinities, when the result is compared or stored. [I] Otherwise the behaviour cannot be matched deterministically.
- [V] TRUE and FALSE: "for the sake of backwards compatibility, the TRUE and FALSE identifiers refer to those other objects, not to the boolean values." https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#booleanexpr. Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX:** objects or columns named `true` or `false`.
- [V] `?` parameters: "This parameter format is provided for compatibility with other database engines. ... the use of this parameter format is discouraged." `$AAAA`: "SQLite is really a Tcl extension that has escaped into the wild." https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#varparam. Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX:** `?` without a number, and `$name` (optional).
- [V] Double-quoted string literals: "This misfeature means that a misspelled double-quoted identifier will be interpreted as a string literal ... we continue to support that capability to avoid breaking legacy." https://sqlite.org/quirks.html#dblquote. Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX** (decided). The oracle matches with `SQLITE_DQS=0`, except in DEFAULT clauses (§4.2).
- [V] Keywords as identifiers: "The ability to use keywords as identifiers promotes backwards compatibility. ... sometimes leads to surprising outcomes." https://sqlite.org/quirks.html. Verdict: partly **AVOIDABLE** (see §2.5).
- [V] PK NULLs: "Due to an historical oversight, the column values of a PRIMARY KEY are allowed to be NULL. This is a bug". The docs add: "WITHOUT ROWID and STRICT tables work correctly". https://sqlite.org/quirks.html. Verdict: **AVOIDABLE** (decided).
- [V] FK enforcement off by default: "To avoid breaking those legacy databases, foreign key constraint enforcement is turned off by default". https://sqlite.org/quirks.html. Verdict: **AVOIDABLE** by configuring the oracle (§4.2).
- [V] Comma-join precedence: "SQLite Gets The Precedence Of Comma-Joins Wrong". https://sqlite.org/quirks.html. Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX:** comma joins mixed with RIGHT or FULL joins without parentheses.
- [V] "Dubious SQL Is Allowed Without Any Error Or Warning ... We cannot change SQLite to follow the current preference toward strict and dogmatic behavior without breaking those legacy applications." https://sqlite.org/quirks.html. Verdict: this is the general case. sql4s3 may refuse more than SQLite does, but never less.
- [V] Unicode case folding: "SQL functions like upper() and lower() only work on ASCII characters." https://sqlite.org/quirks.html. Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** (U8).
- [V] NUL in strings: "This seems like a bug. But it is how SQLite works." https://sqlite.org/nulinstr.html. Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** (U10). The only alternative is to refuse, at run time, any text value that contains U+0000. [I]
- [V] NULL handling: "NULLs are still distinct in a UNIQUE column. This seems somewhat arbitrary". https://sqlite.org/nulls.html. Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** (U7).
- [V] CAST of REAL to INTEGER: "Prior to SQLite version 3.8.2 ... resulted in the most negative integer". https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#castexpr. Verdict: historical only; 3.54.1 saturates.
- [V] Compact `IS` and `IS NOT` "are an SQLite extension". https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#isdf. Verdict: not legacy, and well defined. Accept them.
- [V] iif(): "is included in SQLite for compatibility". https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#case. Verdict: optional alias of CASE.

### 2.4 Functions

- **printf() alias.** The doc says "The printf() name is retained as an alias so as not to break legacy code." (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#printf). changes 3.38.0 says "The original printf() name is retained as an alias for backwards compatibility." Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: function name `printf(...)`**. Accept only `format()`. [I] Accepting it costs nothing because the behaviour is identical.
- **substring() alias.** It exists "for compatibility with SQL Server" (changes 3.34.0, https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#substr). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `substring(...)`** (an optional alias).
- **iif() history.** "The iif() function originally required exactly three arguments." "The if() function is just an alternative spelling for iif()." (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#iif). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `if(...)` spelling and non-3-arg iif**. This is optional, because CASE is the canonical form.
- **random() doc error.** "this documentation incorrectly stated that -9223372036854775808 was a possible result until 2025-07-25" (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#random). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE**. The range [-2^63+1, 2^63-1] must be honoured. The exact values cannot be compared with the oracle anyway.
- **sum() vs total().** "NULL is not normally a helpful result for the sum of no rows but the SQL standard requires it ... in order to be compatible. The non-standard total() function is provided as a convenient way to work around this design problem in the SQL language." (https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#sumunc). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE**. These are ordinary aggregates with documented semantics.
- **group_concat vs string_agg.** "String_agg() is compatible with PostgreSQL and SQL-Server and group_concat() is compatible with MySQL." (https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#group_concat). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: one of the two names**. This is optional.
- **Unordered aggregate inputs.** "If no ORDER BY clause is specified, the inputs to the aggregate occur in an arbitrary order that might change from one invocation to the next." (https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#aggorderby). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: order-sensitive aggregates (group_concat, string_agg, json_group_array, json_group_object) without an in-aggregate ORDER BY** [I]. Otherwise the oracle cannot compare results deterministically.
- **log() base 10.** "Compatibility note: SQLite works like PostgreSQL in that the log() function computes a base-10 logarithm. Most other SQL database engines compute a natural logarithm for log()." The 2-arg order is "reversed from SQL Server" (https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html#log). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** if log() is offered. It could be avoided by rejecting 1-arg `log(X)` and requiring `log10`/`ln` [I].
- **Non-deterministic functions in CHECK.** "due to an historical bug and the desire to maintain backwards compatibility, that restriction is not actually enforced. ... no guarantees are made about how that CHECK constraint will function. You ought not do this." (https://sqlite.org/deterministic.html#dtexception region, §2.1). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: non-deterministic functions (random, randomblob, changes, last_insert_rowid, total_changes, sqlite_version, date/time with 'now'/localtime/utc or no args) in CHECK constraints**.
- **Old date/time determinism.** "Prior to SQLite 3.20.0 (2017-08-01) all date/time functions were always considered non-deterministic." Also: "there may be legacy databases in circulation that were created by SQLite version 3.20.0 through 3.35.1 that have non-deterministic date/time functions in their schemas." (https://sqlite.org/deterministic.html). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE**. sql4s3 creates its own databases. Reject no-arg date/time calls in schema contexts the same way as 'now' [I].
- **JSON BLOB input bug.** "This is actually a long-standing bug ... For backwards bug-compatibility, the (formerly incorrect) legacy behavior of interpreting BLOBs as text JSON if no other interpretation works is hereby documented and is officially supported in version 3.45.1" (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jblobbug). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** when a JSON function gets a BLOB that is not JSONB, because the decision depends on the value at run time. The alternative is a runtime refusal: raise an explicit error for non-JSONB BLOBs, which diverges from SQLite only by refusing. Beware the ambiguity: x'33343536' is valid JSONB (456) and also valid text JSON (3456).
- **json_valid default flags.** "For backwards compatibility, the json_valid(X) function without a "flags" argument continues to report false for inputs that are not canonical JSON, even if the input is JSON5" (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#json5). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** for 1-arg json_valid. Alternatively, **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: 1-arg json_valid** (require explicit flags).
- **JSON storage.** "Backwards compatibility constraints mean that SQLite is only able to store values that are NULL, integers, floating-point numbers, text, and BLOBs. It is not possible to add a new "JSON" type." (https://sqlite.org/json1.html). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** (the storage model).
- **JSON quirks.** "SQLite uses U+0000 as a string terminator. If you embed that character in the middle of an object label, then PATH comparisons will only look at the prefix". "SQLite does not strictly enforce JSON string syntax in PATHs ... you can end up with invalid JSON." (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jquirk). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** for ordinary paths. The second quirk can be avoided at run time by refusing PATH strings that contain backslash escapes [I].
- **Duplicate labels.** json(): "it is undefined whether or not the duplicates are preserved ... future enhancements to this routine may choose to silently remove duplicates". json_object(): "currently allows duplicate labels without complaint, though this might change in a future enhancement." (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jmini, #jobj). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE**. Match the current behaviour, which preserves duplicates. Pin the oracle version.
- **Malformed JSONB.** "The way in which SQLite handles invalid JSONB might change from one version of SQLite to the next ... garbage-in/garbage-out" (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jsonbx §3.2.2). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** unless the engine fully validates JSONB inputs and refuses invalid ones [I recommended].
- **json_each id and hidden columns.** "id" "computation of which might change in future releases". For the hidden columns json/root: "you might not get reliable answers" (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jeach). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: selecting `id`, `json`, `root` columns of JSON TVFs** [I].
- **json_extract vs MySQL.** It is a "subtle incompatibility between the json_extract() function in SQLite and the json_extract() function in MySQL" (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jex). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** (SQLite semantics must be matched).
- **-> / ->> operators.** They are "compatible with both MySQL and PostgreSQL". changes 3.46.0: an integer-looking string RHS "is still treated as a string, because that is what PostgreSQL does". Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** if the operators are supported.
- **Zero-pad overrides left-justify in format().** "SQLite ignores the left-justify flag if the zero-padding flag is present. This is the opposite of what ISO-C and POSIX standard say should happen. ... There is no point in changing the behavior ... the only purpose that would serve would be to break legacy systems." (https://sqlite.org/printf.html, §2.5 and §3). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: format strings with both `-` and `0` flags** (detectable at prepare time for literal formats, otherwise at run time). Otherwise it is UNAVOIDABLE and easy to match.
- **%p ambiguity.** "%p format is an alias for %X" (lang_corefunc#format) versus "works like %x" (printf.html). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `%p` in format()**.
- **%n.** It "is silently ignored and does not consume an argument" (lang_corefunc#format). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `%n`**.
- **Width/precision over the limit.** "The size of this smaller width is unspecified and can be 0." "The size of the smaller substitute precision is unspecified" (https://sqlite.org/printf.html). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE**: refuse widths or precisions above a chosen bound [I].
- **strftime history.** "Earlier versions of SQLite might not support all substitutions." (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#strftm). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** (match 3.54.1).
- **unixepoch, localtime, utc misuse.** For 'unixepoch' not directly after a number: "the behavior is undefined". For 'localtime' after a non-UTC time: "the behavior is undefined". For 'utc': "the result of "utc" is undefined". Dates outside 0000-9999: "the results of these functions are undefined" (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#jdmod, #localtime, §5). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX** for 'unixepoch' not directly after the time-value (detectable for literal modifiers). The others need runtime checks or refusal of 'localtime'/'utc' entirely [I]. 'localtime'/'utc' are environment-dependent anyway.
- **auto modifier.** It "should be avoided for applications that might make use of dates in the opening months of 1970" (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#automod). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** if 'auto' is supported. Alternatively, **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: 'auto' modifier**.
- **subsec resolution.** "this might increase to a higher resolution in future releases" (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#subsec). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE** (pin the oracle version).
- **Old Windows DST.** "Non-Vista Windows platforms only support one set of DST rules ... historical DST calculations will be incorrect" (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html §5). Verdict: N/A to sql4s3, but it is evidence that 'localtime' depends on the host.
- **unicode() on non-text.** "If the argument to unicode(X) is not a string then the result is unspecified." (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#unicode). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE** by a runtime refusal for non-text arguments [I].
- **replace() with empty Y changed.** changes 3.4.0 says "Fix the REPLACE() function so that it returns NULL if the second argument is an empty string". The current doc says "If Y is an empty string then return X unchanged." Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE**. Match the oracle (3.54.1) and verify.
- **group_concat empty-string history.** changes 3.8.5 says group_concat(x,'') "returns NULL instead of an empty string when all inputs are empty strings". 3.46.1 and 3.47.0 reverse this ("returns an empty string, not a NULL"). Verdict: **UNAVOIDABLE**. Probe the oracle.
- **Deprecated case_sensitive_like.** changes 3.44.0: "The PRAGMA case_sensitive_like statement is deprecated". Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: PRAGMA case_sensitive_like** (already covered by "no behaviour-changing PRAGMA switches").
- **load_extension.** "For security reasons, extension loading is disabled by default" (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#load_extension). Verdict: **AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `load_extension()`**.
- **Undefined nArg.** "If the third parameter is less than -1 or greater than 127 then the behavior is undefined." (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/create_function.html). Verdict: N/A (C API).

### 2.5 Statements and clauses

- [V] "This is considered a feature, not a bug." / "The ability to include bare columns in a query is an SQLite-specific extension." (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#bareagg). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: a result column, HAVING or ORDER BY term in an aggregate query that refers to a column not inside an aggregate and not in GROUP BY. [I] Rejecting it requires a functional-dependency-free rule (column must be literally in GROUP BY). If bare columns are ever accepted, the min()/max() special case must be matched exactly, and the arbitrary-row case cannot be matched deterministically.
- [V] LIMIT comma form: "This is counter-intuitive... This reversal of the offset and limit is intentional - it maximizes compatibility with other SQL database systems. However, to avoid confusion, programmers are strongly encouraged to use the form of the LIMIT clause that uses the "OFFSET" keyword" (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#limitoffset). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `LIMIT expr , expr`.
- [V] "Whatever the origin of the input quirks, we generally avoid trying to "fix" them... if a syntax quirk is harmless, we leave it alone and document it here" (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#nonstd). This is the umbrella for the next four items.
- [V] Strange JOIN names: "you can use these non-standard join types but you ought not" (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#strange_join_names). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: any join-operator other than `,`, `[NATURAL] [INNER] JOIN`, `[NATURAL] (LEFT|RIGHT|FULL) [OUTER] JOIN`, `CROSS JOIN` in the standard keyword order.
- [V] Flexible join syntax: ON/USING on comma or CROSS joins, and outer joins with no ON, are accepted (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#flexible_join_syntax). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: ON/USING after `,` or CROSS JOIN, and LEFT/RIGHT/FULL JOIN without ON/USING (if the project wants standard rules). [I] An INNER JOIN without ON is just a cross product and is harmless.
- [V] Comma joins and JOIN have equal precedence, left to right (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#precedence_of_comma_joins_and_cross_join). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: mixing comma joins with outer JOIN keywords in one FROM without parentheses. Otherwise UNAVOIDABLE: left-to-right evaluation must be matched.
- [V] CROSS JOIN: "The special handling of CROSS JOIN is an SQLite-specific feature and is not a part of standard SQL" (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#crossjoin). Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE but harmless. The results equal INNER JOIN, and the planner effect is not observable apart from EXPLAIN.
- [V] "for compatibility with SqlServer and Oracle, SQLite does not enforce this rule" (RECURSIVE keyword) (https://sqlite.org/lang_with.html#limitations_and_caveats). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: a self-referencing CTE in a WITH that lacks RECURSIVE.
- [V] MATERIALIZED: "non-standard SQL syntax copied from PostgreSQL"; "Do not use the MATERIALIZED or NOT MATERIALIZED keywords on a common table expression unless you have a compelling reason to do so." (https://sqlite.org/lang_with.html#materialization_hints). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `AS [NOT] MATERIALIZED`. [I] It can be accepted and ignored, since the hints only affect the plan, not the results. One exception: the evaluation count of non-deterministic functions such as random() in a CTE used more than once may differ (chg 3.35.0 says MATERIALIZED is the default for multiply-used CTEs).
- [V] Recursive CTE queue "becomes a FIFO if the ORDER BY clause is omitted, but applications should not depend on that fact since it might change" (https://sqlite.org/lang_with.html#recursivecte). Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE. The row order of a recursive CTE with no ORDER BY, and the LIMIT cut-off of such a CTE, show through in results. Match FIFO.
- [V] "For compatibility with MySQL, the parser allows the use of the single keyword REPLACE as an alias for "INSERT OR REPLACE"." (https://sqlite.org/lang_insert.html); [V] "This alias is provided for compatibility other SQL database engines" (https://sqlite.org/lang_replace.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: the `REPLACE INTO` statement form.
- [V] Insert target alias "is pointless, but also harmless" without UPSERT (https://sqlite.org/lang_insert.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `INSERT INTO t AS a` without an upsert-clause (optional).
- [V] Xfer + generated columns: "Technically, the statement above is incorrect SQL... it was decided not to fix it lest it break existing code. The bug is harmless... So we call this behavior a feature." (https://sqlite.org/lang_insert.html#the_xfer_optimization_and_generated_columns). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `INSERT INTO t SELECT * FROM s` whose column count differs from t's non-generated column count. sql4s3 should always raise the "table X has N columns but M values were supplied" error. [I] Divergence: SQLite accepts it in the xfer case, so the oracle and sql4s3 differ. Treat this shape as "refused" and keep it out of the differential test corpus, or refuse whenever generated columns are present.
- [V] UPSERT parsing ambiguity: "the SELECT statement should always include a WHERE clause, even if that WHERE clause is just "WHERE true"" (https://sqlite.org/lang_upsert.html#parseambig). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `INSERT ... SELECT ... ON CONFLICT` where the SELECT has no WHERE (or ends in a join). UNAVOIDABLE otherwise: the parse must match SQLite's choice.
- [V] UPSERT "is not standard SQL" and follows PostgreSQL "with generalizations" (multiple clauses, a target-less DO UPDATE) (https://sqlite.org/lang_upsert.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: more than one ON CONFLICT clause, or DO UPDATE without a conflict target (optional; the generalizations are well-defined).
- [V] UPSERT DO UPDATE accepts unqualified columns where "PostgreSQL requires the second form, but SQLite accepts either" (https://sqlite.org/lang_upsert.html). Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE (cheap): name resolution must accept both.
- [V] ON CONFLICT clause: "a non-standard extension specific to SQLite" (https://sqlite.org/lang_conflict.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `ON CONFLICT <alg>` on column/table constraints, and `INSERT/UPDATE OR <alg>`. [I] FAIL and REPLACE are the hardest. REPLACE has "exceptional behaviors" that "might change in a future release".
- [V] REPLACE: "The update hook is not invoked for rows that are deleted by the REPLACE conflict resolution strategy. Nor does REPLACE increment the change counter. The exceptional behaviors defined in this paragraph might change in a future release." (https://sqlite.org/lang_conflict.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: OR REPLACE / REPLACE INTO / ON CONFLICT REPLACE. If they are supported, the changes() count rule is UNAVOIDABLE.
- [V] REPLACE delete triggers "fire if and only if recursive triggers are enabled" (https://sqlite.org/lang_conflict.html). Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE if REPLACE and triggers are both supported. It depends on PRAGMA recursive_triggers (default off [I]). sql4s3 has no behaviour-changing pragmas, so fix it at the default: no delete triggers on REPLACE.
- [V] UPDATE FROM: "The output row selected is arbitrary and might change from one release of SQLite to the next, or from one run to the next." (https://sqlite.org/lang_update.html#upfrom). Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE where the join is many-to-one per target row. Results are non-deterministic, so keep such cases out of the differential tests, or refuse them at run time [I].
- [V] SET with a repeated column: "all but the rightmost occurrence is ignored" (https://sqlite.org/lang_update.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: a column named twice in a SET list.
- [V] INDEXED BY: "an SQLite extension and is not portable"; "should not be used as such"; "Application developers are admonished to omit all use of INDEXED BY" (https://sqlite.org/lang_indexedby.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `INDEXED BY` / `NOT INDEXED`.
- [V] RETURNING: "not standard SQL. It is an extension." (https://sqlite.org/lang_returning.html). Verdict: optional feature. Not legacy.
- [V] Square-bracket identifiers: "This is not standard SQL. This quoting mechanism is used by MS Access and SQL Server and is included in SQLite for compatibility." (https://sqlite.org/lang_keywords.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `[ident]` (already decided).
- [V] Backtick identifiers: "This is not standard SQL. This quoting mechanism is used by MySQL and is included in SQLite for compatibility." (https://sqlite.org/lang_keywords.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `` `ident` `` (already decided).
- [V] Quote-bending: "For resilience when confronted with historical SQL statements, SQLite will sometimes bend the quoting rules"; "they exist only so that old and ill-formed SQL statements will run correctly. Future versions of SQLite might raise errors" (https://sqlite.org/lang_keywords.html). Two parts. (a) "x" as a string literal when it cannot resolve: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX (already decided: double quotes mean identifiers only; error "no such column"). (b) 'x' as an identifier where only an identifier is allowed (e.g. `CREATE TABLE 'foo'(...)`, `SELECT * FROM 'tbl'` [I]): AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: a single-quoted string token in an identifier position.
- [V] quirks section 9: "The ability to use keywords as identifiers promotes backwards compatibility... sometimes leads to surprising outcomes." (https://sqlite.org/quirks.html#keywords_can_often_be_used_as_identifiers). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: unquoted keywords (from the 147-element list) used as identifiers. [I] It may be impractical to reject all of them, because SQLite-generated schemas and common column names (e.g. `key`, `action`, `no`, `first`, `last`, `match`, `temp`) are keywords. A per-keyword allowlist that matches SQLite's fallback is UNAVOIDABLE for whatever is accepted.
- [V] VACUUM schema-name: "Compatibility Warning... Prior to that, a schema-name added to the VACUUM statement would be silently ignored" (https://sqlite.org/lang_vacuum.html). Verdict: historical only. Not relevant to 3.54.1.
- [V] ANALYZE: "The default value for the analysis limit is 0 for backwards compatibility." (https://sqlite.org/lang_analyze.html#approx). Verdict: AVOIDABLE (pragma not offered). The statistics are not visible in query results, apart from sqlite_stat1 content.
- [V] ANALYZE: "all recent versions of SQLite ignore the sqlite_stat2 and sqlite_stat3 tables" (https://sqlite.org/lang_analyze.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE (do not implement).
- [V] EXPLAIN: "The details of the output format are subject to change from one release of SQLite to the next. Applications should not use EXPLAIN or EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN since their exact behavior is variable and only partially documented." (https://sqlite.org/lang_explain.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `EXPLAIN` and `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` (or support them with output that is explicitly exempt from oracle comparison).
- [V] Result column names: "If there is no AS clause then the name of the column is unspecified and may change from one release of SQLite to the next." (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/column_name.html). Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE in practice: every `SELECT expr` without AS has a name that clients see. [I] Either match 3.54.1's names exactly, or declare names without AS outside the compatibility contract (and refuse nothing). See E.
- [V] Transactions: "In very old versions of SQLite (before version 3.7.11 - 2012-03-20) the ROLLBACK will fail with an error code SQLITE_BUSY if there are any pending queries." (https://sqlite.org/lang_transaction.html#implicit_versus_explicit_transactions). Verdict: historical. Not relevant.
- [V] Transactions: "Future versions of SQLite may extend the list of errors which might cause automatic transaction rollback. Future versions of SQLite might change the error response." (https://sqlite.org/lang_transaction.html#response_to_errors_within_a_transaction). Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE (I/O/NOMEM paths). Test only through fault injection, and not against the oracle [I].
- [V] DELETE truncate optimization: changes() was wrong before 3.6.5 (https://sqlite.org/lang_delete.html#truncateopt). Verdict: historical. In 3.54.1, `DELETE FROM t` with no WHERE must report the true count [I].
- [V] END TRANSACTION alias for COMMIT (https://sqlite.org/lang_transaction.html). It is not flagged as legacy. Verdict: optional alias. AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `END [TRANSACTION]` if wanted.
