- [V] ATTACH: URI filenames are disabled by default, "however that might change in a future release of SQLite" (https://sqlite.org/lang_attach.html). Verdict: AVOIDABLE (sql4s3 storage is not a file path) [I].

### 2.6 Schema objects and constraints

- "According to the SQL standard, PRIMARY KEY should always imply NOT NULL. Unfortunately, due to a bug in some early versions, this is not the case in SQLite. Unless the column is an INTEGER PRIMARY KEY or the table is a WITHOUT ROWID table or a STRICT table or the column is declared NOT NULL, SQLite allows NULL values in a PRIMARY KEY column. SQLite could be fixed to conform to the standard, but doing so might break legacy applications." CT#primkeyconst. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: any CREATE TABLE without STRICT and WITHOUT ROWID table-options (either one alone removes the quirk).
- Same fact restated: "NOT NULL was not enforced on PRIMARY KEY columns by early versions of SQLite due to a bug. By the time that this bug was discovered, so many SQLite databases were already in circulation that the decision was made not to fix this bug for fear of breaking compatibility. So, ordinary rowid tables in SQLite violate the SQL standard and allow NULL values in PRIMARY KEY fields." WR#differences_from_ordinary_rowid_tables. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: rowid tables.
- rowidtable: "Because it is not a true primary key, columns of the PRIMARY KEY are allowed to be NULL, in violation of all SQL standards." and "All of the complications above ... arise from the need to preserve backwards compatibility ... The designer of SQLite offers his sincere apology for the current mess." https://sqlite.org/rowidtable.html. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: rowid tables (no WITHOUT ROWID).
- INTEGER PRIMARY KEY DESC: "This quirk is not by design. It is due to a bug in early versions of SQLite. But fixing the bug could result in backwards incompatibilities. Hence, the original behavior has been retained (and documented) because odd behavior in a corner case is far better than a compatibility break." CT#rowid. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: rowid tables. In WITHOUT ROWID no column is a rowid alias, so `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY DESC` is just a descending PK [I].
- Only the exact type name "INTEGER" makes a rowid alias ("INT", "BIGINT" ... do not) CT#rowid. Also CHG 3.12.2/3.13.0: `"INTEGER" PRIMARY KEY` (quoted type) must still count as INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, a "backwards compatibility problem". Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: rowid tables. In WITHOUT ROWID STRICT tables the alias rule never applies.
- "If a table contains a user defined column named "rowid", "oid" or "_rowid_", then that name always refers the explicitly declared column" CT#rowid. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: column names rowid/oid/_rowid_ (case-insensitive). Recommended, since 3.54.0 ALTER refuses these names anyway (CHG 3.54.0 item 3). With WITHOUT ROWID only, the bare names (no such column) give "no such column" [I].
- "(For historical compatibility only, table CHECK constraints are allowed to have a conflict resolution clause, but that has no effect.)" CT (section 4.1). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: conflict-clause after a table CHECK.
- Foreign keys "are disabled by default (for backwards compatibility) ... future releases of SQLite might change so that foreign key constraints enabled by default." FK#fk_enable. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `PRAGMA foreign_keys` (and any OFF setting). The oracle must run with foreign_keys=ON on every connection (see D).
- "It is not possible to enable or disable foreign key constraints in the middle of a multi-statement transaction ... Attempting to do so does not return an error; it simply has no effect." FK#fk_enable. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: PRAGMA foreign_keys.
- MATCH clause: "SQLite parses MATCH clauses (i.e. does not report a syntax error if you specify one), but does not enforce them. All foreign key constraints in SQLite are handled as if MATCH SIMPLE were specified." FK#fk_unsupported. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `MATCH name` in foreign-key-clause (SQLite even accepts `MATCH FULL`/`MATCH PARTIAL` and then ignores them).
- Deferral forms: `DEFERRABLE`, `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY IMMEDIATE`, `NOT DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` all give immediate constraints FK#fk_deferred. Not flagged as legacy, but `NOT DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` reads as deferred and is not. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: accept only `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` and no deferral clause at all [I recommendation].
- FK exception: a parent unique only through CREATE UNIQUE INDEX; dropping the index or the parent leaves orphans with no error. FK#fk_schemacommands. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: FKs whose parent key is not a PRIMARY KEY or UNIQUE constraint in the parent's CREATE TABLE [I], or refuse DROP INDEX on such indexes.
- "Any "foreign key mismatch" errors encountered as part of an implicit DELETE are ignored." FK#fk_schemacommands. Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE for DROP TABLE of a parent whose children are misconfigured. It can be avoided by validating FK parents at CREATE time (rejecting DDL SQLite would accept) [I].
- Misconfigured FKs are DML errors, not DDL: "misconfigured foreign key constraints that require looking at both the child and parent are DML errors" FK#fk_indexes. Verdict: AVOIDABLE by refusing such CREATE TABLE (stricter than SQLite, which is allowed). But SQLite permits a child created before its parent, so refusing forward references gives up some schemas [I].
- legacy_alter_table: "Applications that depend on the older (and arguably buggy) behavior can use the PRAGMA legacy_alter_table=ON statement or the SQLITE_DBCONFIG_LEGACY_ALTER_TABLE configuration parameter" AT#altertabrename. CHG 3.25.2: "older, wonky behavior". pragma page: "New applications should leave this flag turned off." Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: PRAGMA legacy_alter_table. The oracle must keep the default OFF.
- Pre-3.26.0 FK rename behaviour depending on foreign_keys AT#altertabrename. Verdict: AVOIDABLE (the oracle is 3.54.1 and FKs are always on).
- ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN: "that database will not be readable by SQLite version 3.1.3 (2005-02-20) and earlier." AT#altertabaddcol. Verdict: not behavioural for sql4s3 [I].
- "Due to an historical oversight, columns named in the "UPDATE OF" clause do not actually have to exist in the table being updated. Unrecognized column names are silently ignored. ... we have resisted fixing the problem for fear of breaking legacy applications." https://sqlite.org/lang_createtrigger.html#description. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: refuse CREATE TRIGGER ... UPDATE OF <unknown column>.
- "The value of NEW.rowid is undefined in a BEFORE INSERT trigger in which the rowid is not explicitly set to an integer." lang_createtrigger#undef_before. Verdict: AVOIDABLE (no rowid in WITHOUT ROWID tables; refuse `rowid` references).
- BEFORE triggers that modify or delete the row being updated or deleted: "the result of the subsequent update or delete operation is undefined ... it is undefined whether or not AFTER triggers ... will in fact run." lang_createtrigger#cautions_on_the_use_of_before_triggers. Verdict: hard to reject syntactically. UNAVOIDABLE if BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE triggers with DML bodies are allowed. Consider supporting only AFTER triggers, or BEFORE triggers whose bodies are SELECT/RAISE only [I].
- TEMP triggers on non-TEMP tables: "Failure to specify the schema name on the target table might result in the TEMP trigger being reattached to a table with the same name in another database whenever any schema change occurs." and DETACH/re-ATTACH: "no guarantees are made about what happens." lang_createtrigger#temptrig. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: TEMP TRIGGER (or TEMP triggers on non-temp tables, or ATTACH entirely).
- "count_changes pragma does count INSTEAD OF trigger firing" (count_changes is a deprecated pragma) lang_createtrigger#instead_of_triggers. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: PRAGMA count_changes.
- View column names: "SQLite allows you to create views that depend on automatically generated column names, but you should avoid using them since the rules used to generate column names are not a defined part of the interface and might change in future releases of SQLite." lang_createview#description. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: CREATE VIEW without a column-name list whose SELECT has result columns with no AS alias and that are not bare column references [I suggestion]. Simplest: require the view column-name list.
- Descending index "legacy" schema format: "The "legacy" schema format (1) ignores index sort order. ... For compatibility, version of SQLite between 3.3.0 and 3.7.9 use the legacy schema format by default." lang_createindex#descidx. The legacy_file_format pragma "is deactivated. It is now a no-op" (CHG 3.31.0). Verdict: AVOIDABLE: sql4s3 writes its own format. The oracle uses format 4 by default [V].
- CTAS types: CHG 3.41.1 "continues to be INT and is not NUM, for historical compatibility". Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: CREATE TABLE ... AS SELECT. It cannot produce a STRICT WITHOUT ROWID table anyway.
- Generated columns quirk: `INSERT INTO dest SELECT * FROM src` accepted although column counts differ ("Technically, this INSERT statement is incorrect") GC#quirks. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: INSERT ... SELECT whose SELECT width does not match the non-generated target column count. Rejecting is stricter than SQLite and allowed.
- STRICT duplicates: "the current parser accepts duplicate options without complaining, but that might change in future releases" ST#other_table_options. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: duplicate table options (`STRICT, STRICT`).
- "The STRICT keyword may still be used as an identifier. (It is only treated as a keyword in a certain part of the syntax ...)" ST#backwards_compatibility. Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE if identifiers named strict are accepted (it is a non-reserved word). The parser must treat STRICT as a contextual keyword, or refuse `strict` as an identifier (allowed).
- "Because of a quirk in the SQL language parser, versions of SQLite prior to 3.37.0 can still read and write STRICT tables if they set "PRAGMA writable_schema=ON"" ST#accessing_strict_tables_in_earlier_versions_of_sqlite. Verdict: AVOIDABLE (refuse writable_schema).
- ANY in a non-STRICT table converts numeric-looking text ('000123' → integer 123) ST#the_any_datatype. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: the ANY type, already decided.
- Deterministic-function rule for CHECK (cross-page): "due to an historical bug and the desire to maintain backwards compatibility, that restriction is not actually enforced. You can put a non-deterministic function inside of a CHECK constraints and SQLite will not complain." https://sqlite.org/deterministic.html#historical_exception_for_check_constraints. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: non-deterministic functions (random(), date/time with 'now'/localtime/utc or with no arguments, sqlite_version(), changes() etc.) inside CHECK.
- CHG 3.32.0: "INSERT and UPDATE statements now always apply column affinity before computing CHECK constraints. This bug fix could, in theory, cause problems for legacy databases". Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE: the CHECK sees the coerced value. In STRICT, coercion happens before the CHECK [I: assumed same ordering].
- AUTOINCREMENT: "should be avoided if not strictly needed" autoinc#summary. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: AUTOINCREMENT (SQLite itself refuses it on WITHOUT ROWID).
- WITHOUT ROWID: "WITHOUT ROWID is found only in SQLite and is not compatible with any other SQL database engine" WR#quirks. Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE by design decision (sql4s3 requires it). Informational.
- "Only "rowid" works as the keyword in the CREATE TABLE statement" (not `WITHOUT oid`) WR#syntax. Verdict: matched by accepting only `WITHOUT ROWID`.
- Shadow tables read/write by default "in order to maintain backwards compatibility" vtab.html#xshadowname. Verdict: AVOIDABLE (no virtual tables with shadow tables).
- generate_series first parameter now required; "The legacy behavior is available using the -DZERO_ARGUMENT_GENERATE_SERIES compile-time option." CHG 3.37.0. Verdict: AVOIDABLE (generate_series is an extension). Note the oracle build options [I].
- Rowid of VIEW: CHG 3.36.0 "-DSQLITE_ALLOW_ROWID_IN_VIEW compile-time option is available to restore the legacy behavior". Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: rowid references on views (SQLite also errors by default).
- PRAGMA writable_schema simple procedure: "Caution: Making a change to the sqlite_schema table like this will render the database corrupt" AT#otheralter. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: PRAGMA writable_schema and DML on sqlite_schema.
- CHECK and NOT NULL not verified on read: "a query might produce results that violate the CHECK constraints" (with ignore_check_constraints) CT#ckconst. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: PRAGMA ignore_check_constraints.

### 2.7 PRAGMAs, compile-time options and result codes

- "Pragmas whose names are struck through are deprecated. Do not use them. They exist for historical compatibility." https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#toc. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: every PRAGMA statement (sql4s3 refuses PRAGMA switches).
- case_sensitive_like: "This pragma is deprecated and exists for backwards compatibility only ... the use of the case_sensitive_like pragma is discouraged." https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_case_sensitive_like. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `PRAGMA case_sensitive_like`. The default LIKE (ASCII case-insensitive) is UNAVOIDABLE: it is ordinary LIKE behaviour that must be matched exactly.
- count_changes, data_store_directory, default_cache_size, empty_result_callbacks, full_column_names, short_column_names, temp_store_directory: "This pragma is deprecated and exists for backwards compatibility only. New applications should avoid using this pragma. Older applications should discontinue use of this pragma at the earliest opportunity." https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_count_changes (same wording at each anchor). Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: these PRAGMAs. The default column-naming result (short_column_names=1, full_column_names=0, measured) is UNAVOIDABLE: column names of ordinary SELECTs must match the oracle.
- legacy_alter_table: "provided as a work-around for older programs that contain code that expect the incomplete behavior of ALTER TABLE RENAME found in older versions of SQLite. New applications should leave this flag turned off." https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_legacy_alter_table. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `PRAGMA legacy_alter_table` (and never set DBCONFIG_LEGACY_ALTER_TABLE in the oracle). Note that the flag is "turned on temporarily while the sqlite3_module.xRename method is being run" (virtual tables only).
- legacy_file_format: "This pragma no longer functions. It has become a no-op." https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_legacy_file_format. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX.
- cache_size: "Backwards compatibility note: The behavior of cache_size with a negative N was different prior to version 3.7.10" https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_cache_size. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX.
- recursive_triggers: "initially turned OFF by default, for compatibility. Recursive triggers may be turned on by default in future versions of SQLite." https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_recursive_triggers. Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE once triggers exist: the default (OFF) changes the behaviour of ordinary trigger syntax. Pin it in the oracle (G.j).
- foreign_keys default: "the default setting for foreign key enforcement is OFF. However, that might change in a future release of SQLite." https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_foreign_keys. Verdict: AVOIDABLE by configuring the oracle (FK on); the PRAGMA itself is rejected syntax.
- automatic_index: "enabled by default as of version 3.7.17 (2013-05-20), but this might change in future releases" https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_automatic_index. Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE but invisible (plans only); [I] it may affect row order without ORDER BY.
- trusted_schema: "In order to maintain backwards compatibility, this setting is ON by default." https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_trusted_schema. Also "defaults to ON (a value of 1) for legacy compatibility" https://sqlite.org/compile.html#trusted_schema and "This setting defaults to "on" for legacy compatibility" https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigtrustedschema. Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE for which functions are allowed in schema expressions; the oracle setting must be pinned (G.i).
- Double-quoted strings: "the legacy double-quoted string literal misfeature" https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigdqsdml ; "SQLITE_DQS=0. This setting disables the double-quoted string literal misfeature." https://sqlite.org/compile.html#rcmd ; "the default setting is 3 for maximum compatibility with legacy applications" https://sqlite.org/compile.html#dqs. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: a double-quoted token that does not resolve to an identifier is an error (oracle DQS=0 does the same). Caveat: DEFAULT clauses accept identifiers as strings even with DQS=0 (G.a).
- LIKE with BLOBs: "Historically, SQLite has allowed BLOB operands to the LIKE and GLOB operators"; SQLITE_LIKE_DOESNT_MATCH_BLOBS is "at the expense of breaking backwards compatibility ... There was a long-standing bug in the LIKE processing logic" https://sqlite.org/compile.html#like_doesnt_match_blobs. Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE: ordinary `x LIKE y` with a BLOB operand. The oracle build does not set the option (measured `x'61' like 'a'` = 1), so blobs are "cast to TEXT before the comparison" [V]. An alternative is to reject BLOB operands to LIKE/GLOB at run time [I] (that is a semantic refusal, not a syntax refusal).
- Covering index scan: "will cause results to appear in a different order from legacy, which could cause some (incorrectly-coded) legacy applications to break" https://sqlite.org/compile.html#allow_covering_index_scan. Verdict: UNAVOIDABLE only if sql4s3 tries to match the order of unordered results; [I] it should not promise any order without ORDER BY, and the test harness should compare such results as multisets.
- SQLITE_OMIT_AUTOINIT: "For backwards compatibility with older versions of SQLite that lack the sqlite3_initialize() interface" https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_autoinit. Verdict: not SQL (API only).
- SQLITE_OMIT_AUTORESET history: "Rather than fix the many defective smartphone applications, the behavior of SQLite was changed in 3.6.23.2 to automatically reset the prepared statement." https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_autoreset. Verdict: not SQL (API only).
- Result codes: "For historic compatibility, the C-language interfaces return primary result codes by default." https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#pve. Verdict: not SQL; the harness must request extended codes.
- SQLITE_SCHEMA with "the legacy sqlite3_prepare() interface" https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_foreign_keys. Verdict: not SQL (API).
- create_collation_v2 xDestroy: "The inconsistency is unfortunate but cannot be changed without breaking backwards compatibility." https://sqlite.org/c3ref/create_collation.html. Verdict: not SQL (API).
- DBCONFIG_LEGACY_FILE_FORMAT: VACUUM "will fail with an obscure error ... This is not considered a bug" https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfiglegacyfileformat. Verdict: AVOIDABLE (keep the oracle off; measured off).
- DBCONFIG_ENABLE_TRIGGER / ENABLE_VIEW: "Originally this option disabled all triggers. However, since SQLite version 3.35.0, TEMP triggers are still allowed" https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigenabletrigger. Verdict: AVOIDABLE (leave on in the oracle).
- SQLITE_ENABLE_ORDERED_SET_AGGREGATES: the standard WITHIN GROUP syntax is off by default "because ... it makes your SQL code more difficult to read, write, and understand without adding any value" https://sqlite.org/compile.html#enable_ordered_set_aggregates. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: `WITHIN GROUP` (the oracle rejects it too, since the option is absent in the measured build; WITHIN is not a keyword there [I]).
- Unknown pragmas: "No error messages are generated if an unknown pragma is issued. Unknown pragmas are simply ignored." https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX: sql4s3 should reject all PRAGMA statements. This is a documented divergence from the oracle, which accepts them silently [I].
- PRAGMA backward compatibility: "There is no guarantee of backwards compatibility." https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax. Verdict: AVOIDABLE BY REJECTING SYNTAX.
- Obsolete or no-op compile options: SQLITE_ENABLE_JSON1 "no-op"; SQLITE_ENABLE_STAT2 / STAT3 "no-op"; SQLITE_ENABLE_TREE_EXPLAIN "no longer used"; SQLITE_INTROSPECTION_PRAGMAS "obsolete"; SQLITE_OMIT_BTREECOUNT "no-op"; SQLITE_EXTRA_DURABLE "no longer supported"; SQLITE_RTREE_INT_ONLY "deprecated and untested"; SQLITE_STDCALL "deprecated"; SQLITE_OMIT_DISKIO "has not been maintained and probably does not work". https://sqlite.org/compile.html. Verdict: not relevant to SQL semantics.
- Unused result codes: SQLITE_EMPTY "not currently used"; SQLITE_FORMAT "not currently used by SQLite"; CANTOPEN_NOTEMPDIR, IOERR_BLOCKED, IOERR_DIR_CLOSE, IOERR_SHMLOCK "no longer used"; CANTOPEN_DIRTYWAL "not used at this time". https://sqlite.org/rescode.html. Verdict: never needs to be produced.

---

## 3. STRICT and WITHOUT ROWID

§3.1 covers the combination that sql4s3 requires. §3.2 and §3.3 cover STRICT and WITHOUT ROWID separately. §3.4 collects related facts from the statement, function and configuration pages.

### 3.1 The combination STRICT + WITHOUT ROWID

| Topic | What the docs say | Consequence for sql4s3 |
|---|---|---|
| **Syntax** | Table options are a comma-separated list after the closing parenthesis: "If there are multiple options, they can be specified in any order." Duplicates are accepted "but that might change in future releases". [V https://sqlite.org/stricttables.html (§5)] | `CREATE TABLE t(...) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID` and `... WITHOUT ROWID, STRICT` are both valid. [V] The oracle accepts the combination. [V-measured] sql4s3 should refuse duplicate options. [I] |
| **Allowed type names** | "INT", "INTEGER", "REAL", "TEXT", "BLOB", "ANY". "No other datatype names are allowed, though new types might be added in future releases". [V https://sqlite.org/stricttables.html] | sql4s3 allows INT, INTEGER, REAL, TEXT and BLOB. INT and INTEGER behave identically, because no column is a rowid alias without a rowid. [I] |
| **Type coercion** | STRICT still coerces: "SQLite attempts to coerce the data into the appropriate type using the usual affinity rules ... If the value cannot be losslessly converted in the specified datatype, then an SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE error is raised." [V] | Affinity conversion on INSERT and UPDATE is still required. Examples: '123' into INTEGER gives 123; 5 into REAL gives 5.0; 5 into TEXT gives '5'; 1.0 into INTEGER gives 1. [I, from the datatype3 affinity rules; probe each] The measured error message is "cannot store TEXT value in INT column t.a". [V-measured] |
| **PRIMARY KEY is required** | "Every WITHOUT ROWID table must have a PRIMARY KEY. An error is raised if a CREATE TABLE statement with the WITHOUT ROWID clause lacks a PRIMARY KEY." [V https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html] | Every table has an explicit PK. [V] |
| **PK columns are NOT NULL** | Both kinds of table enforce it. STRICT: "Columns that are part of the PRIMARY KEY are implicitly NOT NULL." WITHOUT ROWID: "NOT NULL is enforced on every column of the PRIMARY KEY". [V] | A NULL into a PK column raises `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL`. [I code; measured message format "NOT NULL constraint failed: t.a"] |
| **INTEGER PRIMARY KEY** | STRICT says a NULL inserted into an INTEGER PRIMARY KEY "is automatically converted into a unique integer". WITHOUT ROWID says the special behaviours "do not apply"; the column "works like an "INT PRIMARY KEY" column in an ordinary table". [V] | **No key is ever generated.** In the combination, the WITHOUT ROWID rule wins: there is no rowid to alias. A NULL into an `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY` therefore fails NOT NULL and is not auto-assigned. [I, follows from both pages; probe] Applications must supply every key value. |
| **AUTOINCREMENT** | "An error is raised if the "AUTOINCREMENT" keyword is used in the CREATE TABLE statement for a WITHOUT ROWID table." [V https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html] | Not available; sqlite_sequence never appears. [V] |
| **rowid, oid, _rowid_** | They "are not available on WITHOUT ROWID tables." [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html (§9)] Since 3.54.0, "The ALTER TABLE command raises an error on any attempt to add, drop, or rename columns with the special names "ROWID", "_ROWID_", or "OID"." [V https://sqlite.org/changes.html] | A reference gives "no such column" unless a real column has that name. [I] sql4s3 could refuse those column names outright. [I] |
| **last_insert_rowid()** | "Inserts into WITHOUT ROWID tables are not recorded. If no successful INSERTs into rowid tables have ever occurred on the database connection D, then sqlite3_last_insert_rowid(D) returns zero." [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/last_insert_rowid.html] | On the oracle it always returns 0. [I] sql4s3 should refuse it or define it as constant 0. |
| **Incremental BLOB I/O** | "does not work for WITHOUT ROWID tables". sqlite3_blob_open fails if "Table zTable is a WITHOUT ROWID table". [V https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html, https://sqlite.org/c3ref/blob_open.html] | Not available. zeroblob() only constructs a value. [I] |
| **Update hook** | "The sqlite3_update_hook() interface does not fire callbacks for changes to a WITHOUT ROWID table." [V https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html] The pre-update hook gives "callbacks prior to any change to a rowid table". [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#enable_preupdate_hook] | If the local SQLite cache uses the same WITHOUT ROWID schema, change capture cannot rely on either hook. [I] This is relevant to [sqlite-cache-and-oracle.md](sqlite-cache-and-oracle.md). |
| **changes()** | WITHOUT ROWID is counted normally. A "long-standing problem in sqlite3_changes() for WITHOUT ROWID tables" was fixed in 3.8.11.1. [V https://sqlite.org/changes.html] | No difference. [I] |
| **Duplicate PK columns** | "If the PRIMARY KEY of a WITHOUT ROWID tables uses the same columns with the same collating sequence more than once, then the second and subsequent occurrences of that column in the PRIMARY KEY definition are ignored." [V https://sqlite.org/fileformat2.html] | `PRIMARY KEY(a, a)` is legal and means `PRIMARY KEY(a)`. sql4s3 could refuse it. [I] |
| **Internal index names** | "In a WITHOUT ROWID table, there is no sqlite_schema entry for the PRIMARY KEY, but the "sqlite_autoindex_TABLE_N" name is set aside for the PRIMARY KEY". [V https://sqlite.org/fileformat2.html] | Matters only if sqlite_schema or index lists are exposed. [I] |
| **Detection** | PRAGMA index_info on a WITHOUT ROWID table returns the PK columns. `PRAGMA table_list` has `strict` and `wr` columns. [V https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html, https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_table_list] | The test harness can assert both flags on the oracle's tables. [I] |
| **Performance guidance** | "it is good design to avoid creating WITHOUT ROWID tables with single-column PRIMARY KEYs of type INTEGER". The docs also advise that "the average size of a single row in a WITHOUT ROWID table should be less than about 1/20th the size of a database page". [V https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html#wtu] | The SQL semantics are unaffected: "They both generate the same answers given the same SQL statements." [V] The local SQLite cache would carry this cost if it uses the same table kind. [I] |
| **CREATE TABLE ... AS SELECT** | It creates a table with "no PRIMARY KEY and no constraints of any kind". The syntax diagram gives this form no table options. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_createtable.html] | It cannot produce a STRICT WITHOUT ROWID table, so sql4s3 must refuse it. [I] |
| **Generated columns** | A generated column cannot be part of the PRIMARY KEY. [V https://sqlite.org/gencol.html] Since 3.51.0: "Enforce STRICT typing on computed columns." [V https://sqlite.org/changes.html] | Generated values must satisfy the STRICT type. When and with what code this is checked is not documented. [I] |
| **Other kinds of table** | sqlite_schema is an ordinary rowid table with untyped-style columns. ANALYZE creates `sqlite_stat1`. The JSON table-valued functions declare `ANY` columns. [V https://sqlite.org/fileformat2.html, https://sqlite.org/lang_analyze.html, https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jeach] | These are not application tables. If sql4s3 ever exposes them, they are exceptions to "every table is STRICT and WITHOUT ROWID". [I] |

### 3.2 STRICT tables in detail

- Introduced 3.37.0 (2021-11-27) [V ST#introduction, CHG 3.37.0 item 1 "STRICT tables provide a prescriptive style of data type management, for developers who prefer that kind of thing."].
- Syntax: "if the "STRICT" table-option keyword is added to the end, after the closing ")"" [V ST#strict_tables]. The table-options diagram is `WITHOUT ROWID | STRICT`, comma-separated, any order [V CT diagram, ST#other_table_options]. So `CREATE TABLE t(a INT PRIMARY KEY) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID` and `... WITHOUT ROWID, STRICT` are both legal.
- "Every column definition must specify a datatype for that column. The freedom to specify a column without a datatype is removed." [V]
- Allowed type list, exactly: "INT", "INTEGER", "REAL", "TEXT", "BLOB", "ANY". "No other datatype names are allowed, though new types might be added in future releases of SQLite." [V ST#strict_tables]. [I] So VARCHAR(10), INT(11), NUMERIC, BOOLEAN, DATETIME are all refused. The docs on the pages read give no error message for a disallowed type. [I] The real message is likely "unknown datatype for t.c: \"VARCHAR\"" and must be checked against the oracle. [I] Whether `INTEGER(5)` (type with size arguments) is accepted in STRICT must also be checked against the oracle.
- Coercion: "Content inserted into the column with a datatype other than ANY must be either a NULL (assuming there is no NOT NULL constraint on the column) or the type specified. SQLite attempts to coerce the data into the appropriate type using the usual affinity rules, as PostgreSQL, MySQL, SQL Server, and Oracle all do. If the value cannot be losslessly converted in the specified datatype, then an SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE error is raised." [V]. So conversions still happen: the intro example "an attempt to insert the string '123' results in an integer 123" (stated for INTEGER columns in general) [V ST#introduction]. [I] Expected per affinity: '123' → INTEGER col becomes 123; 'xyz' → INTEGER col gives SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE; 1.0 → INTEGER col becomes 1; 1.5 → INTEGER col gives an error; 5 → REAL col becomes 5.0; 5 → TEXT col becomes '5'; X'00' → INTEGER col gives an error; any non-NULL into BLOB col must already be a blob [I]. The exact matrix is not on these pages. Get it from the oracle and datatype3.html.
- ANY: "Columns with datatype ANY can accept any kind of data ... No type coercion occurs for a column of type ANY in a STRICT table." In STRICT, `'000123'` stays text '000123'; non-strict ANY gives integer 123 [V ST#the_any_datatype]. (sql4s3 rejects ANY.)
- "Columns that are part of the PRIMARY KEY are implicitly NOT NULL. However, even though the PRIMARY KEY has an implicit NOT NULL constraint, when a NULL value is inserted into an INTEGER PRIMARY KEY column, the NULL is automatically converted into a unique integer, using the same rules for INTEGER PRIMARY KEY on ordinary, non-strict tables." [V]. [I] This auto-rowid applies only to rowid STRICT tables. In STRICT + WITHOUT ROWID, INTEGER PRIMARY KEY is not a rowid alias, so NULL gives a NOT NULL error (WR: "will throw an error on any attempt to insert a NULL into a PRIMARY KEY column").
- PRAGMA integrity_check / quick_check check column types in STRICT tables [V].
- Unchanged in STRICT: CHECK, NOT NULL, FOREIGN KEY, UNIQUE, DEFAULT, COLLATE, generated columns, ON CONFLICT, indexes, AUTOINCREMENT. "An INTEGER PRIMARY KEY column is an alias for the rowid, but an INT PRIMARY KEY column is not." Same on-disk format [V ST#strict_tables].
- Generated columns in STRICT: ST says "Generated columns work the same", but CHG 3.51.0 item 14 says "Enforce STRICT typing on computed columns." [V]. So from 3.51.0 the generated value must satisfy the STRICT type [I: timing and code presumably SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE].
- DEFAULT in STRICT: "DEFAULT clauses work the same" [V]. [I] A default whose value cannot convert (e.g. `x INTEGER DEFAULT 'abc'`) probably fails only when used at INSERT, not at CREATE. Check against the oracle.
- CREATE TABLE AS SELECT cannot be STRICT (the diagram gives table-options only to the column-list form; CTAS types "NUM" and "" are not STRICT types) [I from V facts].
- "The STRICT keyword may still be used as an identifier" and "sqlite3_keyword_check(..) does not recognize it as a regular keyword" [V ST#backwards_compatibility].
- Old versions with writable_schema=ON ignore STRICT. PRAGMA quick_check detects the resulting corruption [V].

### 3.3 WITHOUT ROWID tables in detail

- Introduced 3.8.2 (2013-12-06) [V WR#compatibility, CT#rowid, CHG]. Older versions give "malformed database schema" [V].
- Keyword case-insensitive. Only `ROWID` (not oid/_rowid_) is valid in `WITHOUT ROWID` [V WR#syntax].
- "Every WITHOUT ROWID table must have a PRIMARY KEY. An error is raised if a CREATE TABLE statement with the WITHOUT ROWID clause lacks a PRIMARY KEY." [V WR#syntax]. Also CT#primkeyconst: "The PRIMARY KEY is optional for ordinary tables but is required for WITHOUT ROWID tables." [V]
- "The special behaviors associated with "INTEGER PRIMARY KEY" do not apply on WITHOUT ROWID tables. ... An "INTEGER PRIMARY KEY" column in a WITHOUT ROWID table works like an "INT PRIMARY KEY" column in an ordinary table: It is a PRIMARY KEY that has integer affinity." [V]. CT#primkeyconst: INTEGER PRIMARY KEY is defined only when "the table is not a WITHOUT ROWID table" [V]. autoinc: "a column with type INTEGER PRIMARY KEY is an alias for the ROWID (except in WITHOUT ROWID tables)" [V].
- "AUTOINCREMENT does not work on WITHOUT ROWID tables. ... An error is raised if the "AUTOINCREMENT" keyword is used in the CREATE TABLE statement for a WITHOUT ROWID table." [V WR; also autoinc.html#the_autoincrement_keyword].
- "NOT NULL is enforced on every column of the PRIMARY KEY in a WITHOUT ROWID table. ... WITHOUT ROWID tables do follow the standard and will throw an error on any attempt to insert a NULL into a PRIMARY KEY column." [V]. [I] code SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL.
- "The sqlite3_last_insert_rowid() function does not work for WITHOUT ROWID tables. Inserts into a WITHOUT ROWID do not change the value returned by the sqlite3_last_insert_rowid() function. The last_insert_rowid() SQL function is also unaffected since it is just a wrapper" [V]. [I] So in an all-WITHOUT-ROWID engine, last_insert_rowid() always returns 0 on a fresh connection. Either support it as constant 0 matching the oracle, or reject it.
- "The incremental blob I/O mechanism does not work for WITHOUT ROWID tables." [V]
- "The sqlite3_update_hook() interface does not fire callbacks for changes to a WITHOUT ROWID table." [V]
- changes(): CHG 3.8.11.1 "Fix a long-standing problem in sqlite3_changes() for WITHOUT ROWID tables" [V]. So changes() does count WITHOUT ROWID rows [I]. INSTEAD OF firings are not counted (lang_createtrigger) [V].
- PK is a true clustered key. No separate unique index for the PK: "The exceptions are INTEGER PRIMARY KEY and PRIMARY KEYs on WITHOUT ROWID tables." [V CT#uniqueconst]
- No rowid/oid/_rowid_ pseudo-columns: "The rowid (and "oid" and "_rowid_") is omitted in WITHOUT ROWID tables." [V CT#rowid]. [I] Referencing them gives "no such column".
- Detection: PRAGMA index_info(table) returns rows only for WITHOUT ROWID tables [V WR#determining_if_an_existing_table_is_without_rowid]. table_info/table_xinfo are identical to rowid tables [V].
- Generated columns may not be in the PK (GC#limitations) [V], so a WITHOUT ROWID PK must use ordinary columns [I].
- FK parent key "must use named columns only" / not the rowid [V CT, FK]. Nothing special for WITHOUT ROWID parents except CHG 3.19.0 (foreign_key_check fix) and CHG 3.16.2 (REPLACE fix with triggers/FKs on WITHOUT ROWID) [V].
- Performance guidance (not semantics): avoid WITHOUT ROWID for single INTEGER PK and for rows larger than ~1/20 page [V WR#wtu]. [I] This matters only for the sql4s3 storage design, not for oracle parity.
- WITHOUT ROWID virtual tables: PK not enforced by SQLite, rowid not accessible [V vtab.html#worid].

#### Rowid-table-only facts that disappear under the restrictions

- INTEGER PRIMARY KEY rowid aliasing, "datatype mismatch" error on non-integer values (CT#rowid: "a "datatype mismatch" error occurs and the statement is aborted"), NULL→auto rowid, max-rowid random selection, SQLITE_FULL, AUTOINCREMENT/sqlite_sequence, VACUUM changing un-aliased rowids (rowidtable), CTAS "contiguously ascending rowid values". All are [V] on the cited pages and do not apply when every table is WITHOUT ROWID [I].

### 3.4 Related facts from the function, statement and configuration pages

#### Functions

- [V] **last_insert_rowid() ignores WITHOUT ROWID.** "Inserts into WITHOUT ROWID tables are not recorded. If no successful INSERTs into rowid tables have ever occurred on the database connection D, then sqlite3_last_insert_rowid(D) returns zero." (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/last_insert_rowid.html). Also: "The last_insert_rowid() SQL function is also unaffected since it is just a wrapper" (https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html). [I] Every sql4s3 table is WITHOUT ROWID and there are no FTS or virtual tables. So last_insert_rowid() would always return 0 on an sql4s3 connection. One exception might be the JSON TVFs, but they do not insert. Recommendation: **reject last_insert_rowid()** with an explicit error, or implement it as the constant 0. It is also listed as non-deterministic [V deterministic.html].
- [V] **What changes()/total_changes() count.** They count "rows modified, inserted or deleted by the most recently completed INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE". "auxiliary changes caused by triggers, foreign key actions or REPLACE constraint resolution are not counted". "a CREATE TABLE AS SELECT statement does not count". Statements on a view via INSTEAD OF triggers always give zero. Within triggers the value is saved and restored (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/changes.html). changes 3.8.11.1: "Fix a long-standing problem in sqlite3_changes() for WITHOUT ROWID tables". [I] Foreign keys are always on in sql4s3, so FK-action changes must be left out of changes(). Whether total_changes() counts them is not stated on the pages read here (c3ref/total_changes was not read), so probe the oracle.
- [V] **zeroblob() and incremental BLOB I/O.** zeroblob() exists to "reserve space for a BLOB that is later written using incremental BLOB I/O" (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#zeroblob). But "The incremental blob I/O mechanism does not work for WITHOUT ROWID tables" (https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html). [I] In sql4s3 zeroblob() is only a value constructor.
- [V] **sqlite_offset(X)** returns NULL "If X is not a column in an ordinary table" (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#sqlite_offset). It depends on the file layout and is compile-gated, so [I] reject it.
- [V] **Non-deterministic functions are banned in schema contexts**: "In the WHERE clause of a partial index", "an expression index", "the expression of a generated column", and nominally CHECK, which is not enforced (https://sqlite.org/deterministic.html). Date/time functions "will throw an exception if they encounter any of the non-deterministic features in a context where only deterministic functions are allowed". [V changes 3.51.0] "Enforce STRICT typing on computed columns", so in STRICT tables a function result in a generated column must fit the declared column type.
- [I] **JSON in STRICT tables.** STRICT column types cannot be `JSON`. Store text JSON in TEXT columns and JSONB in BLOB columns. A BLOB column holding text JSON triggers the "JSON BLOB input bug" path (B).
- [I] **Doc examples need rewriting.** windowfunctions uses `CREATE TABLE t0(x INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, y TEXT)` and untyped `CREATE TABLE t2(a, b)`. json1 uses `CREATE TABLE big(json JSON)` and `big.rowid`. These must be rewritten as STRICT WITHOUT ROWID with an explicit PRIMARY KEY before they can serve as oracle tests. In WITHOUT ROWID tables "INTEGER PRIMARY KEY" has no rowid-alias meaning [V withoutrowid.html].
- [V] **The JSON TVF schema declares `ANY` columns** (key, value, atom) (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jeach). This is a virtual-table schema and does not violate "no ANY column type" for user tables. [I] Values coming from json_each are dynamically typed.
- [V] **typeof()** returns one of 5 type names. [I] In STRICT tables a stored value's typeof is fixed by the column type. Expression results remain dynamically typed.

#### Statements

- [V] last_insert_rowid: "Inserts into WITHOUT ROWID tables are not recorded. If no successful INSERTs into rowid tables have ever occurred on the database connection D, then sqlite3_last_insert_rowid(D) returns zero." (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/last_insert_rowid.html). [I] So in sql4s3 `SELECT last_insert_rowid()` must always return integer 0. All the OR REPLACE / trigger / rollback rules on that page do not apply.
- [V] "Each entry in most SQLite tables (except for WITHOUT ROWID tables) has a unique 64-bit signed integer key called the "rowid". The rowid is always available as an undeclared column named ROWID, OID, or _ROWID_ as long as those names are not also used by explicitly declared columns." (same page). [I] On WITHOUT ROWID tables, `rowid`/`oid`/`_rowid_` are ordinary unresolvable names ("no such column"), unless a column has that name.
- [V] changes(): "Fix a long-standing problem in sqlite3_changes() for WITHOUT ROWID tables" (chg 3.8.11.1, 3.8.11.1). [I] The counting rules are otherwise identical for WITHOUT ROWID.
- [V] changes() excludes "auxiliary changes caused by triggers, foreign key actions or REPLACE constraint resolution" (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/changes.html). Relevant because FKs are always on: ON DELETE CASCADE rows are not counted.
- [V] "Disallow the use of "rowid" in common table expressions." (chg 3.8.11, 3.8.11).
- [V] VACUUM "may change the ROWIDs of entries in any tables that do not have an explicit INTEGER PRIMARY KEY" (https://sqlite.org/lang_vacuum.html#howvacuumworks). [I] This does not apply to WITHOUT ROWID tables.
- [V] NOT INDEXED: "the rowid can still be used to look up entries even when "NOT INDEXED" is specified" (https://sqlite.org/lang_indexedby.html). [I] For WITHOUT ROWID the PK index is the table, and the docs are silent on it.
- [V] "Enhance the SQLITE_ENABLE_UPDATE_DELETE_LIMIT compile-time option so that it works for WITHOUT ROWID tables." (chg 3.22.0, 3.22.0).
- [V] "Fix the REPLACE statement for WITHOUT ROWID tables that lack secondary indexes so that it works correctly with triggers and foreign keys." (chg 3.16.2, 3.16.2). This shows that REPLACE on WITHOUT ROWID with triggers and FKs is a historically fragile area.
- [V] "Fix the ANALYZE command so that it adds correct samples for WITHOUT ROWID tables in the sqlite_stat4 table." (chg 3.8.6, 3.8.6).
- [V] RETURNING `*` "expands into a list of all non-hidden columns" (https://sqlite.org/lang_returning.html#details). It never includes a rowid. Same for SELECT `*` [I].
- [V] The INSERT/VALUES rule that unlisted columns get "the default column value ... or with NULL if no default value is specified" (https://sqlite.org/lang_insert.html). [I] For WITHOUT ROWID the PK is implicitly NOT NULL, so a missing PK value without a default fails with a NOT NULL constraint. Check the exact error with the oracle. For STRICT, the default value must also satisfy the column type.
- [V] ON CONFLICT applies to "UNIQUE, NOT NULL, and PRIMARY KEY constraints only" (https://sqlite.org/lang_conflict.html). [I] The STRICT type check is a separate constraint kind (SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE, per the STRICT page), so OR IGNORE / OR REPLACE presumably do not suppress it. Verify with the oracle.
- [V] The UPSERT conflict target must name a uniqueness constraint: "an explicit UNIQUE or PRIMARY KEY constraint within the CREATE TABLE statement, or a unique index" (https://sqlite.org/lang_upsert.html). The WITHOUT ROWID PRIMARY KEY qualifies.
- [V] lang_with example uses a WITHOUT ROWID table (`CREATE TABLE org(name TEXT PRIMARY KEY, boss TEXT REFERENCES org) WITHOUT ROWID;`) (https://sqlite.org/lang_with.html#withorderby). [I] It is a usable test fixture if STRICT is added and the column types match.
- [V] isolation.html's example uses `SELECT rowid, * FROM table` and `DELETE FROM table WHERE rowid=?` (https://sqlite.org/isolation.html). [I] This does not apply. The same "undefined" visibility rules apply to PK-keyed WITHOUT ROWID tables.
- [V] stricttables page: "The STRICT keyword may still be used as an identifier." (https://sqlite.org/stricttables.html). It is not in the 147-keyword list.

#### Configuration and result codes

- SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE (3091): "an insert or update attempted to store a value inconsistent with the column's declared type in a table defined as STRICT." [V https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#constraint_datatype]. Measured message: `cannot store TEXT value in INT column t.a` [V-measured].
- SQLITE_MISMATCH: "The rowid of a table must be an integer. Attempt to set the rowid to anything other than an integer (or a NULL ...) results in an SQLITE_MISMATCH error." [V https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#mismatch]. [I] Never applies to WITHOUT ROWID tables.
- SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_ROWID: "a rowid is not unique" [V]. [I] Not reachable for WITHOUT ROWID tables. PK violations there are SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_PRIMARYKEY [I].
- PRAGMA table_list returns `wr` ("1 if the table is a WITHOUT ROWID table") and `strict` ("1 if the table is a STRICT table") [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_table_list]. These are useful to check from the oracle side that test schemas are STRICT and WITHOUT ROWID [I].
- PRAGMA foreign_key_check: the second column is "the rowid of the row that contains the invalid REFERENCES clause, or NULL if the child table is a WITHOUT ROWID table" [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_foreign_key_check].
- PRAGMA index_info / index_xinfo given the name of a WITHOUT ROWID table (as of 3.30.0): they return the PK columns "with duplicate columns removed" / "de-duplicated PRIMARY KEY columns first followed by data columns" [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_index_info, #pragma_index_xinfo]. index_info column 2: "-1 means rowid" [V].
- index_list origin "pk" = "the index was created by a PRIMARY KEY constraint" [V].
- sqlite3_blob_open fails with SQLITE_ERROR if "Table zTable is a WITHOUT ROWID table" [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/blob_open.html].
- SQLITE_ENABLE_PREUPDATE_HOOK: callbacks "prior to any change to a rowid table" [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#enable_preupdate_hook].
- SQLITE_OMIT_AUTOINCREMENT / SQLITE_CORRUPT_SEQUENCE: concern AUTOINCREMENT and sqlite_sequence [V]. [I] AUTOINCREMENT only applies to INTEGER PRIMARY KEY of rowid tables, so it is irrelevant under WITHOUT ROWID.
- SQLITE_ENABLE_SORTER_REFERENCES: "the records passed to the sorter often contain only a ROWID value" [V]. Performance only.
- count_changes pragma: the change count excludes trigger, FK-action and upsert changes [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_count_changes]. This is relevant to changes() semantics, which is documented elsewhere.
- Limits: SQLITE_MAX_LENGTH "also determines the maximum number of bytes in a row" [V https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_length]; rows per table 2^64 theoretical [V].
- No compile-time option, PRAGMA or db_config in these pages forces STRICT or WITHOUT ROWID on all tables [V: grep of https://sqlite.org/compile.html and https://sqlite.org/pragma.html for "STRICT" finds only SQLITE_STRICT_SUBTYPE and the table_list `strict` column].

---

## 4. Oracle configuration

### 4.1 Summary per restriction

For each decided restriction, the table names the switch that makes a stock SQLite reject the same input or behave the same way.

| Restriction | Switch on a stock SQLite | Status in the measured 3.54.1 build | Residual divergence that sql4s3 must enforce alone |
|---|---|---|---|
| Double quotes mean identifiers only | Compile time: `SQLITE_DQS=0`, "The recommended setting is 0 ... However, the default setting is 3 for maximum compatibility with legacy applications" [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#dqs]. Run time: `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DQS_DML` and `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DQS_DDL` set to 0 [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigdqsdml]. | `DQS=0` is compiled in. `select "abc"` fails with `no such column: "abc" - should this be a string literal in single-quotes?` [V-measured] | `DEFAULT "abc"`, and even a bare `DEFAULT abc`, are still accepted and stored as the text 'abc'. [V-measured] Single-quoted identifiers are also accepted, for example `CREATE TABLE 't1'('a' INT)`; the docs call this a fallback "For resilience when confronted with historical SQL statements". [V https://sqlite.org/lang_keywords.html, V-measured] No switch disables either. |
| No `[x]` or `` `x` `` identifiers | **None.** compile.html and pragma.html have no such option. [V] | Both are accepted. [V-measured] | sql4s3 must reject all of them. The harness treats such inputs as expected divergences. [I] |
| Foreign keys always enforced | `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`, `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_FKEY`, or compile time `SQLITE_DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS=1`. "This pragma is a no-op within a transaction". The docs advise: "applications should set the foreign key enforcement flag as required by the application and not depend on the default setting." [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_foreign_keys, https://sqlite.org/compile.html#default_foreign_keys] | Off by default. [V-measured] | The oracle must enable it on every connection before any BEGIN and read the value back. [I] Never set `defer_foreign_keys`: "Setting "defer_foreign_keys=0" resets the internal foreign-key tracking state ... possible to commit the current transaction even if there are foreign key violations." [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_defer_foreign_keys] |
| UTF-8 only | The encoding of a new database is "determined by the API used to open the connection". It cannot be changed afterwards ("silently ignored"). ATTACH of a database with another encoding fails. [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_encoding] `SQLITE_OMIT_UTF16` removes the UTF-16 APIs and is an unsupported OMIT option. [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_utf16] | `pragma encoding` reports `UTF-8`. [V-measured] | Open the oracle with the UTF-8 API (`sqlite3_open_v2`) and assert `pragma_encoding`. [I] CAST between BLOB and TEXT depends on the encoding, so this matters. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#castexpr] |
| All tables STRICT | **None.** [V: no option in compile.html or pragma.html] | A non-STRICT CREATE TABLE is accepted. [I] | The harness asserts `pragma_table_list.strict = 1` for every user table. [I, V for the column] |
| All tables WITHOUT ROWID | **None.** [V] | Accepted, including combined with STRICT. [V-measured] | The harness asserts `pragma_table_list.wr = 1`. [I] |
| No ANY type | **None.** The authorizer does not see column types. [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_alter_table.html] | `CREATE TABLE t(a ANY) STRICT` is accepted. [V-measured] | Expected divergence. The harness can detect ANY columns with `pragma_table_xinfo`. [I] |
| No PRAGMA switches | `SQLITE_OMIT_PRAGMA` is a compile-time OMIT option: "unsupported" and "may not work with the amalgamation". Unknown pragmas are "simply ignored", so narrower OMITs do not cause rejection. At run time, an authorizer that returns `SQLITE_DENY` for action code `SQLITE_PRAGMA` (19) rejects the statement with `SQLITE_AUTH`. [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_pragma, https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax, https://sqlite.org/c3ref/set_authorizer.html] | The authorizer is "disabled by default". [V] | Install the authorizer after the harness has configured the connection. Compare only "rejected vs accepted", because the error codes differ. [I] Whether `SELECT * FROM pragma_xxx()` triggers the PRAGMA action is not documented. [I] |
| (Pinned defaults) | `recursive_triggers` (default off), `trusted_schema` (library default on), `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE`, `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_FP_DIGITS` (default 17), `legacy_alter_table` (off), `reverse_unordered_selects` (off), `case_sensitive_like` (off, deprecated), `ignore_check_constraints` (off), limits through `sqlite3_limit()`. [V, see §4.3] | The plain shell turns defensive on and trusted_schema off; the library defaults are the opposite. The build compiles `MAX_VARIABLE_NUMBER=250000`, while the documented default is 32766. [V-measured] | Set every setting explicitly on each oracle connection, and do not rely on defaults. [I] |

Three restrictions can be shared through configuration: DQS, foreign keys and encoding. The DQS switch leaves the gaps listed above. Rejecting PRAGMA statements needs an authorizer callback rather than a setting. Rejecting STRICT-less tables, rowid tables, ANY, and bracket or backtick quoting has no switch at all; sql4s3 must enforce these alone, and the harness must class such inputs as expected divergences. [I]

### 4.2 Detailed oracle configuration (from the configuration extract)

#### Measured oracle build [V-measured]

`sqlite3 :memory: "select sqlite_version()"` → `3.54.1`; source id `2026-10-16 14:45:20 555e07ef33f69a0957d24d8e555b702464a131d6f1607f4c3c1e4551e8e6ca76`.

`sqlite3 :memory: "select * from pragma_compile_options"` → (full list):

Notable absences: LIKE_DOESNT_MATCH_BLOBS, CASE_SENSITIVE_LIKE, DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS, TRUSTED_SCHEMA, ENABLE_QPSG, ENABLE_ORDERED_SET_AGGREGATES, ENABLE_UPDATE_DELETE_LIMIT, SOUNDEX, OMIT_*, REVERSE_UNORDERED_SELECTS, ENABLE_ICU, ENABLE_NULL_TRIM, ENABLE_SNAPSHOT, ENABLE_SETLK_TIMEOUT.

**CLI vs library caveat [V-measured]:** the plain CLI changes some connection settings. `.dbconfig` shows `defensive on`, `trusted_schema off`, `load_extension on`. With `sqlite3 -unsafe-testing` the same build shows `defensive off` and `trusted_schema on`, which match the documented library defaults ("defaults to "on" for legacy compatibility" [V]). [I] A Go harness that links the library or a driver will start with the library defaults, not the CLI's. Set every setting below explicitly on each connection; do not rely on defaults.

Full measured `.dbconfig` (plain CLI): attach_create on, attach_write on, comments on, defensive on, dqs_ddl off, dqs_dml off, enable_fkey off, enable_qpsg off, enable_trigger on, enable_view on, fts3_tokenizer off, fp_digits 17, legacy_alter_table off, legacy_file_format off, load_extension on, no_ckpt_on_close off, reset_database off, reverse_scanorder off, stmt_scanstatus off, trigger_eqp off, trusted_schema off, writable_schema off.

Measured `.limit`: length 1000000000, sql_length 1000000000, column 2000, expr_depth 1000, parser_depth 2500, compound_select 500, vdbe_op 250000000, function_arg 1000, attached 10, like_pattern_length 50000, variable_number 250000, trigger_depth 1000, worker_threads 0, schema 10000000, trigger_steps 65000.

#### Double-quoted strings are identifiers only

- Compile-time: SQLITE_DQS=N. "The recommended setting is 0, meaning that double-quoted strings are disallowed in all contexts. However, the default setting is 3 for maximum compatibility with legacy applications." [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#dqs]. It is also the first item in the recommended options: "SQLITE_DQS=0. This setting disables the double-quoted string literal misfeature." [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#rcmd]
- Run-time: SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DQS_DML ("for DML statements only, that is DELETE, INSERT, SELECT, and UPDATE statements") and SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DQS_DDL ("for DDL statements, such as CREATE TABLE and CREATE INDEX"); "The default value of this setting is determined by the -DSQLITE_DQS compile-time option." [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigdqsdml]. Added in 3.29.0, where "Both default to "on" for legacy compatibility" [V changes].
- Oracle status: the build has DQS=0 and both dbconfigs are off [V-measured]. `select "abc"` → error `no such column: "abc" - should this be a string literal in single-quotes?` [V-measured]. **Matches the restriction.** [I] Still set both dbconfigs to 0 explicitly in the harness, as protection against a different build.
- Gaps (oracle accepts, sql4s3 must refuse) [V-measured]: (1) `CREATE TABLE t(x TEXT DEFAULT "abc")` and even `DEFAULT abc` (bare identifier) succeed, and the default value is the text 'abc', despite DQS=0. (2) Single-quoted identifiers are accepted: `create table 't1'('a' int)`, `t1.'a'`. No switch on these pages disables either. [I] The harness must list these as expected divergences.

#### No [bracket] or `backtick` identifiers

- **No switch exists.** compile.html and pragma.html contain no option for bracket or backtick quoting [V: grep for "bracket" and "backtick" in https://sqlite.org/compile.html and https://sqlite.org/pragma.html returned nothing]. lang_keywords.html: "A keyword enclosed in square brackets is an identifier. This is not standard SQL. This quoting mechanism is used by MS Access and SQL Server and is included in SQLite for compatibility." and "A keyword enclosed in grave accents (ASCII code 96) is an identifier. This is not standard SQL. This quoting mechanism is used by MySQL and is included in SQLite for compatibility." [V https://sqlite.org/lang_keywords.html].
- Oracle status: `select [a], `a` from (select 1 as a)` → `1|1` [V-measured]. [I] This is an expected divergence. The harness should treat any input containing `[` or a backtick outside string literals as "sql4s3 must reject; oracle result ignored".

#### Foreign keys always enforced

- PRAGMA foreign_keys: "As of SQLite version 3.6.19, the default setting for foreign key enforcement is OFF. However, that might change in a future release of SQLite. The default setting for foreign key enforcement can be specified at compile-time using the SQLITE_DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS preprocessor macro. To minimize future problems, applications should set the foreign key enforcement flag as required by the application and not depend on the default setting." "This pragma is a no-op within a transaction" [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_foreign_keys].
- SQLITE_DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS=1: "enforcement of foreign key constraints will be on by default", but "Each database connection can always turn enforcement of foreign key constraints on and off at run-time" [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#default_foreign_keys]. Added in 3.7.5 [V changes].
- SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_FKEY: "This is the same setting that is enabled or disabled by the PRAGMA foreign_keys statement." Pass 1 to enable [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigenablefkey].
- Oracle status: the build lacks DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS, so FK is **off** (measured pragma 0, dbconfig enable_fkey off) [V-measured]. **Action:** on every oracle connection, before any transaction, call `sqlite3_db_config(db, SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_FKEY, 1, &out)` or run `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`, and assert that the result reads back 1 [I]. Measured with FK on: FK violation → `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` [V-measured].
- Do NOT use PRAGMA defer_foreign_keys: "Setting "defer_foreign_keys=0" resets the internal foreign-key tracking state ... possible to commit the current transaction even if there are foreign key violations" [V]. Note SQLITE_OMIT_TRIGGER "also disables enforcement of foreign key constraints" [V], so never use it.

#### UTF-8 only

- PRAGMA encoding: the default encoding of a new database "defaults to one determined by the API used to open the connection." It cannot be changed after creation, and "any attempt to do so will be silently ignored." "An attempt to ATTACH a database with a different text encoding from the "main" database will fail." [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_encoding].
- SQLITE_OMIT_UTF16: "omit support for UTF16 text encoding. When this is defined all API functions that return or accept UTF16 encoded text are unavailable. These functions can be identified by the fact that they end with '16'" [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_utf16]. It is an OMIT option, so it is "unsupported" and "may not work with the amalgamation" [V]. compile.html does not say whether it prevents UTF-16 database files [V].
- No SQLITE_DEFAULT_ENCODING entry exists in compile.html [V: grep].
- Oracle status: `pragma encoding` → `UTF-8` [V-measured] (the CLI opens with sqlite3_open, which is UTF-8 [I]). **Action:** open oracle databases with sqlite3_open / sqlite3_open_v2 (UTF-8 API), optionally run `PRAGMA encoding='UTF-8'` before creating the db, and assert `pragma_encoding` = 'UTF-8' [I]. sql4s3 should reject `PRAGMA encoding` in any case.

#### All tables STRICT

- **No switch exists** on these pages to force STRICT: https://sqlite.org/compile.html and https://sqlite.org/pragma.html mention STRICT only as SQLITE_STRICT_SUBTYPE (unrelated: application-defined function subtypes) and the `strict` column of PRAGMA table_list [V: grep]. 
- Oracle status: a non-STRICT `CREATE TABLE` is accepted by the oracle [I, ordinary behaviour]. **Action [I]:** the harness rejects any CREATE TABLE without STRICT before it reaches the oracle, or simply expects sql4s3 to error. After schema setup, assert `SELECT count(*) FROM pragma_table_list WHERE schema='main' AND type='table' AND name NOT LIKE 'sqlite_%' AND strict=0` = 0 [V: table_list `strict` column, https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_table_list].

#### All tables WITHOUT ROWID

- **No switch exists** on these pages [V: no compile option or pragma mentions forcing WITHOUT ROWID]. 
- **Action [I]:** as for STRICT, check `pragma_table_list.wr = 1` for every user table [V: "wr: 1 if the table is a WITHOUT ROWID table"]. Measured: `create table p(id integer primary key) strict, without rowid;` is accepted [V-measured].
- Consequence: never use sqlite3_blob_open in the harness; it fails on WITHOUT ROWID tables [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/blob_open.html].

#### No ANY column type

- **No switch exists** on these pages. Measured: `create table t(a any) strict; insert into t values(1); select typeof(a)` → `integer` (accepted) [V-measured]. [I] The authorizer cannot see column types: SQLITE_CREATE_TABLE only passes the table name [V c_alter_table: "SQLITE_CREATE_TABLE 2 /* Table Name NULL */"]. **Action [I]:** expected divergence; the harness rejects or flags any schema with an ANY column (detectable after the fact via `pragma_table_xinfo(t).type = 'ANY'`).

#### No PRAGMA switches

What exists:
- SQLITE_OMIT_PRAGMA: "used to omit the PRAGMA command from the library ... This macro removes the PRAGMA command only." [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_pragma]. Caveats: OMIT options are "unsupported" and "may not work with the amalgamation"; they need a build from canonical source [V]. Removing PRAGMA would also stop the harness from configuring the oracle with PRAGMA (it would have to use sqlite3_db_config) and from using pragma_xxx introspection [I]. Not recommended for the oracle [I].
- Narrower OMITs: SQLITE_OMIT_FLAG_PRAGMAS ("a subset of PRAGMA commands that query and set boolean properties"), SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED (removes count_changes, data_store_directory, default_cache_size, empty_result_callbacks, full_column_names, short_column_names, temp_store_directory), SQLITE_OMIT_CASE_SENSITIVE_LIKE_PRAGMA, SQLITE_OMIT_SCHEMA_PRAGMAS, SQLITE_OMIT_SCHEMA_VERSION_PRAGMAS, SQLITE_OMIT_PAGER_PRAGMAS, SQLITE_OMIT_INTROSPECTION_PRAGMAS, SQLITE_OMIT_INTEGRITY_CHECK, SQLITE_OMIT_COMPILEOPTION_DIAGS [V compile.html]. Note: an omitted pragma becomes an unknown pragma, which is "simply ignored", not an error [V pragma.html; I for the combination]. So these OMITs do not make the oracle *reject* PRAGMA input.
- Run-time: sqlite3_set_authorizer with action code SQLITE_PRAGMA (19), arguments "Pragma Name" and "1st arg or NULL" [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_alter_table.html]. Returning SQLITE_DENY makes "the entire SQL statement to be rejected with an error" (SQLITE_AUTH) [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/set_authorizer.html, https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#auth]. "The authorizer is disabled by default." It runs only at prepare time [V]. **This is the only documented way to make a stock SQLite reject PRAGMA statements** [I]. [I] Install it after the harness has configured the connection (FK, DQS, etc.), so that configuration pragmas still work. The code differs (SQLITE_AUTH vs sql4s3's error), so compare "rejected vs accepted", not codes. [I] It is not documented here whether `SELECT * FROM pragma_xxx(...)` triggers SQLITE_PRAGMA, SQLITE_READ or SQLITE_FUNCTION; verify against the oracle.
- DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE blocks only the dangerous pragmas: "The PRAGMA writable_schema=ON statement. The PRAGMA journal_mode=OFF statement. The PRAGMA schema_version=N statement." [V]. Measured: under defensive, `pragma writable_schema=1` is silently ignored (stays 0), and `pragma journal_mode=off` on :memory: returns `memory` [V-measured]. These are no-ops, not errors.

#### Defensive mode and trusted_schema

- DEFENSIVE: "When the defensive flag is enabled, language features that allow ordinary SQL to deliberately corrupt the database file are disabled." The list is in A.8 [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigdefensive]. Library default: not stated on the page; measured off (`-unsafe-testing`), on in the plain CLI [V-measured]. **Recommendation [I]:** enable it on oracle connections (`sqlite3_db_config(db, SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE, 1, &out)`). It only affects features sql4s3 refuses (writable_schema, journal_mode=OFF, schema_version writes, sqlite_dbpage, shadow tables).
- trusted_schema: "In order to maintain backwards compatibility, this setting is ON by default. There are advantages to turning it off, and most applications will be unaffected if it is turned off. For that reason, all applications are encouraged to switch this setting off on every database connection as soon as that connection is opened." [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_trusted_schema]. With it OFF, functions in triggers, views, CHECK, DEFAULT, expression indexes, partial indexes and generated columns must be SQLITE_INNOCUOUS [V dbconfig]. Measured: ON in the library defaults (`-unsafe-testing`), OFF in the plain CLI. **Decision needed [I]:** it changes which schema expressions the oracle accepts, so sql4s3 must choose one value and pin the oracle to it. All built-in deterministic functions are presumably innocuous [I, not stated on these pages]; verify with `pragma_function_list` flags (3.31.0 (2020-01-22): "Enhance the function_list pragma to show ... the function property flags SQLITE_DETERMINISTIC, SQLITE_DIRECTONLY, SQLITE_INNOCUOUS, and/or SQLITE_SUBTYPE" [V changes]).

#### Other legacy toggles to pin

- **recursive_triggers**: default OFF, "initially turned OFF by default, for compatibility. Recursive triggers may be turned on by default in future versions" [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_recursive_triggers]. Measured 0, both in the plain CLI and with `-unsafe-testing`, **even though compile_options lists `DEFAULT_RECURSIVE_TRIGGERS`** [V-measured]. compile.html has no entry for SQLITE_DEFAULT_RECURSIVE_TRIGGERS [V]. [I] The macro is probably defined to 0. Do not trust the listing; set the value explicitly. There is no db_config equivalent on these pages, so pin it with `PRAGMA recursive_triggers=<chosen>` at connection open (before the authorizer from G.h is installed).
- **case_sensitive_like**: "deprecated and exists for backwards compatibility only" [V]. The build lacks SQLITE_CASE_SENSITIVE_LIKE, so it is off. Measured `pragma case_sensitive_like=1; select 'a' like 'A'` → 0 (the pragma works when issued) [V-measured]. No action other than never issuing it. It is write-only, so its state cannot be read back [V syntax].
- **legacy_alter_table**: default OFF; "New applications should leave this flag turned off." [V]. Measured off. Optionally pin it with DBCONFIG_LEGACY_ALTER_TABLE = 0.
- **reverse_unordered_selects**: default off [V]. Measured off. [I] The harness could run unordered queries twice, with it off and on (DBCONFIG_REVERSE_SCANORDER), to detect tests that depend on order; results should then be compared as multisets.
- **SQLITE_LIKE_DOESNT_MATCH_BLOBS**: absent in the build, so BLOBs are "cast to TEXT before the comparison" [V]. Measured `x'61' like 'a'` = 1. sql4s3 must match this (or reject BLOB LIKE operands at run time [I]).
- **SQLITE_STRICT_SUBTYPE**: present (=1 implied) [V-measured]. Affects only application-defined functions that call sqlite3_result_subtype() [V]. Irrelevant unless sql4s3 exposes custom functions [I].
- **ignore_check_constraints**: default off [V]; measured 0. Never set it.
- **count_changes / empty_result_callbacks / full_column_names / short_column_names**: deprecated [V]. Measured defaults count_changes 0, empty_result_callbacks 0, full_column_names 0, short_column_names 1. Keep the defaults; column-name tests depend on them.
- **automatic_index**: on (1) [V-measured]. It affects plans only.
- **ENABLE_QPSG**: off [V-measured]. With ENABLE_STAT4 on, plans may depend on ANALYZE data [V], but results should not [I].
- **FP_DIGITS**: 17 [V-measured]. Pin it explicitly (`SQLITE_DBCONFIG_FP_DIGITS`, 17), because REAL-to-text output depends on it [V].
- **ENABLE_COMMENTS**: on [V-measured]. Keep it on if sql4s3 accepts comments; set it to 0 if sql4s3 refuses comments [I].
- **Limits**: the oracle allows VARIABLE_NUMBER up to 250000 [V-measured], while the docs give 32766 [V]. Pin with sqlite3_limit() to sql4s3's chosen values so that limit errors match [I].
- **trusted_schema / DEFENSIVE**: see G.i; pin both explicitly.
- **load_extension**: the CLI shows it on [V-measured]; keep it off on harness connections [I].
- **Extended result codes**: call sqlite3_extended_result_codes(db, 1) or read sqlite3_extended_errcode(), since "the C-language interfaces return primary result codes by default" [V https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#pve].

Suggested per-connection oracle setup [I]:
1. Open with sqlite3_open_v2 (UTF-8).
2. Set DBCONFIG DQS_DML=0, DQS_DDL=0, ENABLE_FKEY=1, DEFENSIVE=1, TRUSTED_SCHEMA=<chosen>, LEGACY_ALTER_TABLE=0, REVERSE_SCANORDER=0, FP_DIGITS=17, ENABLE_COMMENTS=<chosen>.
3. Run `PRAGMA recursive_triggers=<chosen>`.
4. Call sqlite3_limit() for each limit sql4s3 implements.
5. Turn on extended result codes.
6. Assert the settings: pragma_foreign_keys=1, pragma_encoding='UTF-8', pragma_recursive_triggers.
7. Install an authorizer that returns SQLITE_DENY for SQLITE_PRAGMA (19).
8. Run tests. Check pragma_table_list (strict=1, wr=1) after DDL, or do this before step 7 / with the authorizer allowing it.

### 4.3 Other configuration facts by area

#### Configuration, limits and result codes

Settings that change semantics, with documented default → measured value in the oracle (CLI, see G.0):

- SQLITE_DQS (default 3, recommended 0) → measured DQS=0; DBCONFIG_DQS_DML/DDL off [V, V-measured].
- PRAGMA foreign_keys / DBCONFIG_ENABLE_FKEY / SQLITE_DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS (default OFF) → measured off. **The oracle must turn it ON per connection.**
- PRAGMA recursive_triggers (default OFF) → measured 0.
- PRAGMA trusted_schema / DBCONFIG_TRUSTED_SCHEMA / SQLITE_TRUSTED_SCHEMA (library default ON) → measured ON with `-unsafe-testing`, OFF in the plain CLI.
- DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE (library default not stated) → measured off with `-unsafe-testing`, on in the plain CLI.
- PRAGMA case_sensitive_like / SQLITE_CASE_SENSITIVE_LIKE (default off) → option absent from the build.
- SQLITE_LIKE_DOESNT_MATCH_BLOBS (default absent) → absent; measured `x'61' like 'a'` = 1.
- PRAGMA reverse_unordered_selects / DBCONFIG_REVERSE_SCANORDER (default off) → off.
- PRAGMA legacy_alter_table / DBCONFIG_LEGACY_ALTER_TABLE (default off) → off.
- PRAGMA ignore_check_constraints (default off) → 0.
- PRAGMA short_column_names (default not stated; measured 1) and full_column_names (measured 0).
- PRAGMA encoding (default "determined by the API used to open the connection") → measured UTF-8.
- PRAGMA automatic_index (default on) → 1.
- DBCONFIG_ENABLE_QPSG (default off) → off. SQLITE_ENABLE_STAT4 is ON in the build, which "violates the query planner stability guarantee" [V] (plans only).
- DBCONFIG_FP_DIGITS (default 17) → 17. This affects REAL-to-TEXT output.
- DBCONFIG_ENABLE_COMMENTS (default on) → on.
- SQLITE_ENABLE_MATH_FUNCTIONS → ON (math functions exist). SQLITE_ENABLE_PERCENTILE → ON (percentile functions exist). SQLITE_ENABLE_OFFSET_SQL_FUNC → ON (sqlite_offset() exists). JSON is built in (no OMIT_JSON). SQLITE_SOUNDEX → absent. ENABLE_ORDERED_SET_AGGREGATES → absent. ENABLE_UPDATE_DELETE_LIMIT → absent. SQLITE_ENABLE_UNKNOWN_SQL_FUNCTION → ON (EXPLAIN only).
- SQLITE_STRICT_SUBTYPE → ON (app-defined functions only).
- Limits: VARIABLE_NUMBER = 250000 in the build vs documented default 32766. MAX_FUNCTION_ARG 1000; COLUMN 2000; EXPR_DEPTH 1000; COMPOUND_SELECT 500; LENGTH and SQL_LENGTH 1e9; LIKE_PATTERN 50000; TRIGGER_DEPTH 1000; ATTACHED 10; PARSER_DEPTH 2500; TRIGGER_STEPS 65000; SCHEMA 1e7; VDBE_OP 2.5e8.

#### Functions

- **SQLITE_ENABLE_MATH_FUNCTIONS.** The math functions are "only active if the amalgamation is compiled using" it (https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html). The docs give no default other than this wording. [I] Check with `SELECT sqlite_compileoption_used('ENABLE_MATH_FUNCTIONS')` on the oracle.
- **SQLITE_OMIT_JSON.** JSON is "built into SQLite by default, as of SQLite version 3.38.0" and can be omitted with -DSQLITE_OMIT_JSON. The legacy option -DSQLITE_ENABLE_JSON1 applied before 3.38.0 (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#howtocompile).
- **SQLITE_ENABLE_PERCENTILE** (default off). It gates median(), percentile(), percentile_cont() and percentile_disc(). Built into the amalgamation since 3.51.0 (https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#median; changes 3.51.0).
- **SQLITE_ENABLE_ORDERED_SET_AGGREGATES** (default off) gates the WITHIN GROUP ordered-set syntax (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqliteselforder1).
- **SQLITE_SOUNDEX** (default off): "This function is omitted from SQLite by default." (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#soundex)
- **SQLITE_ENABLE_OFFSET_SQL_FUNC** (default off) gates sqlite_offset() (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#sqlite_offset).
- **SQLITE_PRINTF_PRECISION_LIMIT**, default 2147483647. It caps format() width and precision. **SQLITE_FP_PRECISION_LIMIT** is internal, "not modifiable at compile-time", and smaller (https://sqlite.org/printf.html).
- **SQLITE_DBCONFIG_FP_DIGITS.** It sets the significant digits for float→text. Default 17. The c3ref page says "as of SQLite version 3.52.0. The value was 15 in all prior versions" (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html). https://sqlite.org/changes.html places it in 3.53.0 (item 9b: "Rounding is now done by default to 17 significant digits, instead of 15"), and notes that 3.52.0 was "Withdrawn". [I] This affects every REAL→TEXT conversion in concat(), ||, quote(), hex(), length(), octet_length(), json output, and so on. The oracle must use the default (17). Do not let the test harness change it. The format() %f/%g limit is separate: 16 digits, or 26 with "!".
- **SQLITE_STRICT_SUBTYPE=1** is "a recommended compile-time option for every application that makes use of subtypes". With it, an SQL error is raised if a function not flagged SQLITE_RESULT_SUBTYPE sets a subtype (changes 3.45.0).
- **Extension loading.** sqlite3_enable_load_extension() is "disabled by default". SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_LOAD_EXTENSION enables the C API only (changes 3.13.0) (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#load_extension).
- **SQLITE_DBCONFIG_TRUSTED_SCHEMA / PRAGMA trusted_schema=OFF.** These "disable the use of SQL functions inside views and triggers and in schema structures ... unless the function is tagged with SQLITE_INNOCUOUS". "Most built-in functions are innocuous." (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqliteinnocuous)
- **PRAGMA case_sensitive_like** is deprecated (changes 3.44.0). The **SQLITE_CASE_SENSITIVE_LIKE** compile-time option sets its default to "on" (changes 3.2.3). [I] The oracle must be built without it, so that LIKE is case-insensitive for ASCII. Verify with sqlite_compileoption_used.
- **ICU extension.** It changes lower()/upper() and LIKE so they fold non-ASCII case (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#lower; lang_expr). [I] The oracle must NOT load ICU.
- **SQLITE_LIMIT_FUNCTION_ARG** limits variadic argument counts (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/create_function.html). The default value was not given on these pages.
- **Clock and timezone.** 'now' comes from the VFS xCurrentTime. 'localtime'/'utc' use the C library localtime_r(), so results depend on the host TZ environment (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval, §5). [I] The oracle harness must fix TZ, or the engine must refuse 'localtime'/'utc'. sql4s3 must record 'now' as a non-deterministic input.
- **PRNG.** ChaCha20 since 3.40.0 (changes 3.40.0). random() and randomblob() can never be compared with the oracle. Record them as non-deterministic inputs.
- **JSON nesting limit** is 1000, a fixed constant (changes 3.42.0, https://sqlite.org/json1.html).
- **PRAGMA function_list** (3.31.0) lists arg counts, type (scalar, aggregate, window) and flags. Use it to dump the oracle's real determinism and innocuous flags for every built-in (changes 3.31.0).
- **PRAGMA compile_options / sqlite_compileoption_get(N)** return the build options (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#sqlite_compileoption_get).

#### Statements

- [V] SQLITE_ENABLE_UPDATE_DELETE_LIMIT (compile-time) enables ORDER BY/LIMIT/OFFSET on UPDATE and DELETE (https://sqlite.org/lang_update.html#optional_limit_and_order_by_clauses, https://sqlite.org/lang_delete.html#optional_limit_and_order_by_clauses). [I] Default off. The oracle build should stay without it, or the tests must know which way it was built. sql-stmt lists `delete-stmt-limited` and `update-stmt-limited` [V].
- [V] SQLITE_LIMIT_COMPOUND_SELECT caps the number of SELECTs in a compound but not VALUES rows (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#values; chg 3.8.8, L398). [I] Default 500.
- [V] SQLITE_LIMIT_ATTACHED (sqlite3_limit) caps attached databases (https://sqlite.org/lang_attach.html). [V] SQLITE_MAX_ATTACHED: max raised to 125 (chg 3.8.6, 3.8.6). [V] "the default value remains at 10" (chg 3.7.6, 3.7.6).
- [V] PRAGMA recursive_triggers decides whether REPLACE-deleted rows fire delete triggers (https://sqlite.org/lang_conflict.html; chg 3.6.18). [I] Default OFF.
- [V] PRAGMA read_uncommitted plus shared cache is the only way to see uncommitted data. "The read_uncommitted pragma is off by default" (https://sqlite.org/isolation.html).
