- [V] PRAGMA journal_mode: DELETE is "the current default behavior". WAL is enabled with "PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL" (https://sqlite.org/isolation.html). EXCLUSIVE equals IMMEDIATE in WAL (https://sqlite.org/lang_transaction.html#immediate). journal_mode=OFF makes ROLLBACK undefined [V].
- [V] URI filenames for ATTACH / VACUUM INTO: off by default. Enabled by -DSQLITE_USE_URI=1, sqlite3_config(SQLITE_CONFIG_URI,1), or SQLITE_OPEN_URI (https://sqlite.org/lang_vacuum.html#vacuuminto, https://sqlite.org/lang_attach.html).
- [V] PRAGMA synchronous NORMAL/FULL makes VACUUM INTO fsync its output (https://sqlite.org/lang_vacuum.html#vacuuminto).
- [V] PRAGMA auto_vacuum / page_size are changed by VACUUM. secure_delete is an alternative (https://sqlite.org/lang_vacuum.html).
- [V] SQLITE_ENABLE_STAT4 (sqlite_stat4). SQLITE_ENABLE_STAT2/3 tables are ignored now (https://sqlite.org/lang_analyze.html). [V] PRAGMA analysis_limit default 0. PRAGMA optimize (bitmask, e.g. 0x10002) (https://sqlite.org/lang_analyze.html#approx, #pragopt).
- [V] SQLITE_OMIT_TRUNCATE_OPTIMIZATION. The authorizer returning SQLITE_IGNORE for SQLITE_DELETE bypasses truncation (https://sqlite.org/lang_delete.html#truncateopt). [I] No visible result difference in 3.54.1.
- [V] PRAGMA count_changes is mentioned as related to the change count (https://sqlite.org/lang_delete.html#truncateopt, https://sqlite.org/c3ref/changes.html). [I] It is deprecated (other page) and must not be enabled on the oracle.
- [V] SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_COMMENTS: "Comments are enabled by default." (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html). Added 3.49.0 together with SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_ATTACH_CREATE and SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_ATTACH_WRITE; "All default to "on"" (chg 3.49.0).
- [V] SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_TRIGGER / ENABLE_VIEW control only main/attached schemas, not TEMP (chg 3.35.0, 3.35.0).
- [V] Shared cache mode: attaching the same file twice is an error (https://sqlite.org/lang_detach.html).
- [V] SQLITE_STMTSTATUS_FULLSCAN_STEP / SQLITE_STMTSTATUS_SORT through sqlite3_stmt_status() are suggested over INDEXED BY (https://sqlite.org/lang_indexedby.html).
- [V] sqlite3_keyword_count(), sqlite3_keyword_name(), sqlite3_keyword_check() report the build's keyword set (https://sqlite.org/lang_keywords.html). [I] Use them to confirm that the oracle build has all 147.

#### Schema

| Setting | Effect | Default (per docs) | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| PRAGMA foreign_keys | FK enforcement per connection. No-op inside a transaction | OFF ("As of SQLite version 3.6.19, the default setting for foreign key enforcement is OFF. However, that might change in a future release") [V cross-page pragma.html] | FK#fk_enable |
| SQLITE_DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS | Compile-time default for foreign_keys | 0 [I]. Added 3.7.5 [V CHG] | pragma.html, CHG 3.7.5 |
| SQLITE_OMIT_FOREIGN_KEY | FK definitions become a syntax error | not defined [I] | FK#fk_enable |
| SQLITE_OMIT_TRIGGER | FKs parsed but not enforced. PRAGMA foreign_keys becomes a no-op. Triggers gone | not defined [I] | FK#fk_enable |
| PRAGMA defer_foreign_keys | All FKs deferred until the outermost COMMIT. Also disables RESTRICT (3.12.0). Auto-reset at COMMIT/ROLLBACK. "Setting "defer_foreign_keys=0" resets the internal foreign-key tracking state ... possible to commit the current transaction even if there are foreign key violations." | OFF [V cross-page pragma.html] | FK#fk_deferred, CHG 3.8.0, 3.12.0 |
| PRAGMA legacy_alter_table / SQLITE_DBCONFIG_LEGACY_ALTER_TABLE | Pre-3.25 RENAME behaviour (rewrites only the initial occurrence; triggers, views, CHECKs and partial-index WHERE unchanged). Per connection, not persistent. Temporarily ON during xRename | OFF [V cross-page pragma.html] | AT#altertabrename |
| PRAGMA writable_schema | ALTER ignores unparsable schema rows (3.38.0). Allows editing sqlite_schema | OFF [I] | AT (section 7, #disable_error_checking_using_pragma_writable_schema_on) |
| PRAGMA ignore_check_constraints | Disables CHECK enforcement | OFF ("CHECK constraints are enforced by default") [V cross-page pragma.html] | CT#ckconst |
| PRAGMA recursive_triggers | Does not affect FK actions [V]. 3.6.18: delete triggers fire on REPLACE only when recursive triggers are on [V CHG] | OFF [I] | FK#fk_unsupported, CHG 3.6.18 |
| PRAGMA count_changes | Counts INSTEAD OF firings | OFF [I], deprecated [I] | lang_createtrigger#instead_of_triggers |
| PRAGMA schema_version | Used by the writable_schema procedure | - | AT#otheralter |
| PRAGMA legacy_file_format / SQLITE_DBCONFIG_LEGACY_FILE_FORMAT | Index DESC honoured or not. The pragma is a no-op since 3.31.0 | new format (4) by default since 3.7.10 [V] | lang_createindex#descidx, CHG 3.31.0 |
| SQLITE_ENABLE_UPDATE_DELETE_LIMIT | ORDER BY/LIMIT on top-level UPDATE/DELETE (never inside triggers) | off [I] | lang_createtrigger syntax restrictions |
| SQLITE_MAX_TRIGGER_DEPTH / SQLITE_LIMIT_TRIGGER_DEPTH | Trigger and FK-action recursion depth | - (not given) | FK#fk_unsupported |
| SQLITE_LIMIT_TRIGGER_STEPS, SQLITE_LIMIT_SCHEMA | New limits in 3.54.0 | - | CHG 3.54.0 |
| SQLITE_MAX_COLUMN / SQLITE_LIMIT_COLUMN | Columns per table and per index | - (not given here) | CT#tablecoldef, lang_createindex |
| SQLITE_MAX_LENGTH | Row byte limit | - | CT#tablecoldef |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE | Shadow tables read-only. Disallows triggers writing shadow tables (3.39.4) | off [V "Shadow tables are read/write by default"] | vtab.html#xshadowname, CHG 3.39.4 |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_TRIGGER / ENABLE_VIEW | Enable triggers/views in main and attached schemas. TEMP ones always allowed (3.35.0) | on [I] | CHG 3.35.0 item 7 |
| SQLITE_DBCONFIG_TRUSTED_SCHEMA, SQLITE_VTAB_DIRECTONLY/INNOCUOUS | Function/vtab use in triggers and views | - | vtab.html#security_considerations |
| SQLITE_ALLOW_ROWID_IN_VIEW | Restores the legacy view rowid | off [V "available to restore"] | CHG 3.36.0 |
| SQLITE_DETERMINISTIC (function flag) | Required for app functions in indexes and partial-index WHERE | app functions non-deterministic by default [V] | expridx#restrictions |
| SQLITE_ENABLE_DBSTAT_VTAB | Needed for the dbstat eponymous vtab | off [V implied "must be compiled with"] | vtab.html#epovtab |
| -DZERO_ARGUMENT_GENERATE_SERIES | Legacy generate_series | off | CHG 3.37.0 |

Oracle recommendation [I]: open every oracle connection with `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` and leave legacy_alter_table, defer_foreign_keys, ignore_check_constraints, writable_schema and recursive_triggers at their defaults. sql4s3 rejects all of these pragmas.

---

## 5. Proposed slice ladder

This is a suggestion to react to, not a plan. [I throughout, except where a rule is cited] Slices are ordered by dependency. Each slice adds the syntax listed and nothing more; everything else stays an explicit error.

Two observations shape the order:
- **Text formatting can come late.** The Go API returns typed values (int64, float64, string, []byte), and §6.4 shows that REAL-to-text conversion is hard. Every construct that turns a number into text can therefore be deferred: CAST to TEXT, `||` with numbers, format(), quote(), and a number inserted into a TEXT column.
- **Prepare-time type checks are a legitimate rejection.** When all operand types are known at prepare time (literals, or STRICT columns that are NOT NULL), sql4s3 may refuse type mixes it has not implemented yet. An example is text in arithmetic. Bound parameters make types dynamic. So the slice that adds parameters must either implement the full conversions, or refuse at run time with an explicit error.

Every slice also forces some general behaviour:
- **Errors.** Every rejection needs a code and a message. Parse errors are `SQLITE_ERROR`; the oracle's message for them is `near "X": syntax error` [I, probe]. Compare extended codes, and compare messages only where pinned. [V https://sqlite.org/rescode.html]
- **Keywords** are case-insensitive. Identifiers are compared case-insensitively for ASCII. [I, probe]
- **Result rows are a multiset.** Without ORDER BY the order is not defined, so the harness must not compare it. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#orderby]

| # | Slice | Exact syntax added | SQLite behaviour this slice forces us to match |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `SELECT 1` | `SELECT <decimal-integer-literal>`, optional `;`, whitespace. Reject literals above 9223372036854775807, because those are REAL. | The value is INTEGER [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#litvalue]. The result column name is "a copy of the expression text", so `SELECT 1` has a column named `1` [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_full_column_names; I that the exact source text is kept]. The keyword is case-insensitive. Error code and message for any other input. |
| 2 | Comments | `-- ...` and `/* ... */` anywhere whitespace may appear. | "Comments do not nest." An unterminated `/*` runs to the end of the input and is accepted. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_comment.html] What happens to the column name of `SELECT 1 /*c*/` must be probed. [I] |
| 3 | Several columns and aliases | `SELECT e1, e2, ...`, `AS name`, `"quoted name"` (doubled `""` inside). Bare keywords stay refused as aliases. | Column names come from AS [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/column_name.html]. Identifier quoting [V https://sqlite.org/lang_keywords.html]. The column limit is 2000, giving the error "too many columns in result set" [V-measured]. |
| 4 | NULL, string and BLOB literals | `NULL`, `'text'` (doubled `''` inside), `X'hex'`. | typeof would report null, text and blob. No backslash escapes. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#litvalue] Column-name text for `SELECT 'a'` must be probed. [I] Probe how SQL text that is not valid UTF-8 is handled. [I] |
| 5 | REAL and hexadecimal literals, digit separators | `1.5`, `.5`, `1e10`, `0x1F`, `1_000`. | The integer/real boundary. `0x8000000000000000` is -9223372036854775808. A literal outside the 64-bit range is REAL. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#hexint] Text-to-double parsing must be bit-exact with the oracle, because the 3.53.0 rewrite of the conversion routines may matter (§6.2). |
| 6 | Unary operators | `-e`, `+e`, `~e`, parentheses. | `-9223372036854775808` is an INTEGER literal [V https://sqlite.org/changes.html]. Negating the minimum integer overflows to REAL [I, per the overflow rule]. NULL propagates. Precedence [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops]. |
| 7 | Arithmetic on numbers and NULL | `+ - * / %` with INTEGER, REAL and NULL operands. Text and BLOB operands are refused at prepare time. | Integer arithmetic unless it would overflow, then IEEE 754. "Integer divide yields an integer result, truncated toward zero." Division by zero gives NULL. The result type of `%`. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html (§5)] |
| 8 | Comparisons and logic | `= == != <> < <= > >=`, `IS [NOT]`, `IS [NOT] DISTINCT FROM`, `ISNULL`, `NOTNULL`, `NOT NULL`, `AND`, `OR`, `NOT`, `TRUE`, `FALSE`. | Cross-class ordering NULL < numeric < TEXT < BLOB. Literals have no affinity, so `1='1'` is 0. Text uses the BINARY collation (memcmp). Three-valued logic. IS never returns NULL. `x IS TRUE` treats x as a boolean. [V https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#comparisons, https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#isisnot] Operands of AND, OR and NOT are cast to NUMERIC [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#booleanexpr]. |
| 9 | CASE, BETWEEN, IN lists | `CASE [base] WHEN ... THEN ... [ELSE ...] END`, `[NOT] BETWEEN`, `[NOT] IN (list)`, including the empty list. | Lazy evaluation. The base form compares with `=`. The IN NULL matrix. IN with an empty list is false even when the left side is NULL. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#case, #in_op] |
| 10 | First functions | `typeof`, `coalesce`, `ifnull`, `nullif`, `iif`; `abs` and `sign` on numbers. An unknown-function error and an arity error. | typeof names [V https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#typeof]. `abs(-9223372036854775808)` "throws an integer overflow error" [V https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#abs]. Short-circuit evaluation [V https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#iif]. A determinism flag per function, needed later. |
| 11 | Text operands and conversions | Text and BLOB in arithmetic and comparisons. `CAST(e AS INTEGER, REAL, BLOB or NUMERIC)`. CAST to TEXT is still refused for REAL. | Four different text-to-number rule sets (§6.3). Non-numeric text becomes 0. Hexadecimal text is not recognised. CAST saturates at the 64-bit limits. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#castexpr, https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html] |
| 12 | Number-to-text conversion | `CAST(real AS TEXT)`, `\|\|`, `length`, `upper`, `lower`, `substr`, and so on. | The FP_DIGITS=17 algorithm: round to 15 digits, check the round trip, and fall back to 17 digits. [V https://sqlite.org/floatingpoint.html#*fpdigits] ASCII-only case folding [V]. substr sign rules [V https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#substr]. |
| 13 | Bound parameters | `?NNN`, `:name`, perhaps `@name`. Bare `?` and `$name` are optional. | Parameter numbering. An unbound parameter is NULL. The limit is 32766 in the docs but 250000 in the build, so pin it with `sqlite3_limit()`. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#varparam, V-measured] Types are now dynamic, so slices 7, 8 and 11 must work at run time. |
| 14 | Create and drop tables | `CREATE TABLE [IF NOT EXISTS] t(c TYPE [NOT NULL] [PRIMARY KEY], ..., [PRIMARY KEY(c, ...)]) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID` (either option order). `DROP TABLE [IF EXISTS] t`. TYPE is one of INT, INTEGER, REAL, TEXT, BLOB. | A PK is mandatory, and PK columns are implicitly NOT NULL [V §3.1]. Errors for an unknown type, a missing PK, a duplicate column and an existing table, with oracle messages [I]. The `sqlite_` prefix is reserved [V https://sqlite.org/fileformat2.html#intschema]. Refuse duplicate options and rowid-named columns [I]. |
| 15 | INSERT ... VALUES | `INSERT INTO t [(cols)] VALUES (...), (...)`. | STRICT coercion, or `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE` (3091) with the message "cannot store TEXT value in INT column t.a" [V https://sqlite.org/rescode.html, V-measured]. `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL` (1299) "NOT NULL constraint failed: t.a" [V-measured]. A duplicate key gives `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_PRIMARYKEY` (1555) [V code; I message]. A failing statement changes nothing (ABORT) [V https://sqlite.org/lang_conflict.html]. Unlisted columns get NULL [V https://sqlite.org/lang_insert.html]. Values-count errors of the form "table dest has 2 columns but 4 values were supplied" [V https://sqlite.org/lang_insert.html]. |
| 16 | SELECT from one table | `SELECT cols \| * \| t.* FROM t [WHERE e]`. | Column affinity in comparisons: an INTEGER column compared with `'5'` converts the text to a number, while `+col` does not [V https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#compaff]. The column name is the bare column name [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_full_column_names]. `*` without FROM is an error [V https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#resultset]. A WHERE that is NULL excludes the row. |
| 17 | ORDER BY and LIMIT | `ORDER BY e [ASC\|DESC] [NULLS FIRST\|LAST]`, ordinal terms and alias terms. `LIMIT n [OFFSET m]`; the comma form is refused. | NULLs first in ASC and last in DESC. Cross-class order. The collation of the column [V https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html (§6, §7)]. A LIMIT must convert to an integer losslessly; a negative LIMIT means no limit [V https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#limitoffset]. Ties are undefined. |
| 18 | UPDATE and DELETE | `UPDATE t SET c = e, ... [WHERE e]`, `DELETE FROM t [WHERE e]`. | "all scalar expressions are evaluated before any assignments are made". Refuse a column named twice in SET. Updating a PK can conflict. STRICT coercion on UPDATE. [V https://sqlite.org/lang_update.html] |
| 19 | changes() | `changes()`, `total_changes()`. | Only direct changes count: no trigger, FK-action or REPLACE effects. The value is kept across statements that are not DML. [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/changes.html] These functions are non-deterministic, so record them as inputs. |
| 20 | Transactions | `BEGIN [DEFERRED\|IMMEDIATE\|EXCLUSIVE]`, `COMMIT`, `END`, `ROLLBACK`; then `SAVEPOINT`, `RELEASE`, `ROLLBACK TO`. | Errors for a nested BEGIN and for COMMIT or ROLLBACK with no transaction (messages from the oracle [I]). Savepoint stack rules [V https://sqlite.org/lang_savepoint.html]. Statement-level undo inside a transaction. |
| 21 | Column constraints, part 1 | `DEFAULT literal \| signed-number \| (expr)`, `UNIQUE`, table `UNIQUE(...)`. | A parenthesised DEFAULT is evaluated per row [V https://sqlite.org/lang_createtable.html#dfltval]. Refuse bare or double-quoted identifiers as DEFAULT (an oracle divergence, §4.1). UNIQUE treats NULLs as distinct. `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE` (2067). |
| 22 | Column constraints, part 2 | `CHECK(expr)` on columns and tables, without a conflict clause. | A violation is a result of zero after a cast to NUMERIC; NULL passes [V https://sqlite.org/lang_createtable.html#ckconst]. Affinity is applied first (3.32.0). Refuse subqueries and non-deterministic functions. `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_CHECK` (275) with an oracle message. |
| 23 | Collations | `COLLATE BINARY\|NOCASE\|RTRIM` on columns and as an operator. | The precedence rules for choosing a collation [V https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#colrules]. NOCASE folds ASCII only. An unknown collation gives `SQLITE_ERROR_MISSING_COLLSEQ` (257) [V https://sqlite.org/rescode.html]. |
| 24 | Aggregates | `count(*)`, `count(x)`, `sum`, `total`, `avg`, `min`, `max`; then `GROUP BY` and `HAVING`; then `DISTINCT` inside aggregates and `FILTER`. Bare columns are refused. | An aggregate over empty input returns one row [V https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#resultset]. The sum/total/avg typing and overflow rules [V https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html]. GROUP BY treats NULLs as equal and INTEGER and REAL as equal [V https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html (§6)]. |
| 25 | Joins | `,`, `[INNER] JOIN ... ON`, `USING`, `LEFT JOIN`; later `RIGHT` and `FULL`. | Name resolution and ambiguity errors. USING merges columns and uses the left side's affinity and collation [V https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#fromclause]. Outer-join NULL rows are added before WHERE is applied. |
| 26 | Foreign keys | Column `REFERENCES p(c)` and table `FOREIGN KEY`; NO ACTION first, then the actions; `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` last. | Always enforced. `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY` (787) "FOREIGN KEY constraint failed" [V-measured]. The parent must be a PK or UNIQUE. "foreign key mismatch" is a DML-time error [V https://sqlite.org/foreignkeys.html]. A deferred violation found at COMMIT leaves the transaction open. |

Later slices, in rough dependency order:
- subqueries: scalar, `EXISTS`, `IN (SELECT ...)`, and row values;
- `CREATE INDEX` (unique, partial, expression);
- DISTINCT;
- compound SELECT;
- WITH and WITH RECURSIVE;
- views (with a mandatory column list);
- UPSERT;
- RETURNING;
- the other conflict algorithms (REPLACE last or never);
- string, date, math and format() functions;
- window functions;
- JSON;
- generated columns;
- triggers;
- ALTER TABLE.

The function, statement and schema extracts each propose a finer order for their own area (§5.1 to §5.4).

### 5.1 Function slices (from the function extract)

- **Function-call infrastructure (first prerequisite).** Needs a parser for `name(args)`, `name(*)` and `name(DISTINCT x)`, a name+arity lookup table with an explicit "unknown function" error, and a per-function determinism flag. The determinism flag drives refusal in CHECK, generated columns and indexes, and the non-deterministic input recording.
- **Slice 1: deterministic scalars with no type subtleties.** typeof(X), coalesce, ifnull, nullif, iif (3-arg CASE desugar first). Minimal syntax: `SELECT typeof(1)`, `SELECT coalesce(NULL,2)`. Depends on literals and NULL only.
- **Slice 2: text functions.** length, octet_length, lower, upper (ASCII), substr (with all sign cases), instr, replace, trim/ltrim/rtrim, hex, unhex, char, unicode, quote, concat, concat_ws. Depends on text/blob values and the numeric→text rendering. The rendering must exist first and match FP_DIGITS=17.
- **Slice 3: numeric functions.** abs (overflow error), sign, round (probe ties), random/randomblob (record as non-deterministic input). Depends on 64-bit integer and double arithmetic and text→number conversion rules. Note that sign() and abs() use different lossless rules.
- **Slice 4: LIKE/GLOB.** These need expression operators and the ESCAPE clause. ASCII-only case folding. Refuse PRAGMA case_sensitive_like. Minimal: `SELECT 'a' LIKE 'A'`.
- **Slice 5: aggregates.** Needs GROUP BY, aggregate-context machinery and empty-group semantics. Order: count(*) → count(X) → sum/total/avg (integer overflow error, REAL rules, Inf/NULL rule) → min/max → group_concat/string_agg (require an in-aggregate ORDER BY, which needs the 3.44.0 syntax) → DISTINCT → FILTER. Minimal: `SELECT count(*) FROM t`.
- **Slice 6: format().** Needs text rendering of integers and reals. Begin with %d %s %% and NULL/missing args. Then %f %e %g with the 16-digit cap, %x %o %c %q %Q %w, then the flags. Refuse %n, %p, the `-0` flag combination, and huge widths. Minimal: `SELECT format('%d', 1)`.
- **Slice 7: date/time.** Needs a time-value parser (12 formats + TZ suffix) and a modifier pipeline. Start with deterministic inputs only: `SELECT date('2024-01-01')`. 'now', no-arg forms and 'subsec'-as-first-arg need the "non-deterministic input recording" mechanism and refusal in schema contexts. Consider refusing 'localtime'/'utc' outright: they depend on the host TZ, the behaviour is undefined on misuse, and they are hard to oracle-test.
- **Slice 8: math functions.** These need the oracle to be built with SQLITE_ENABLE_MATH_FUNCTIONS (check first). Results depend on libm, so bit-for-bit equality with the oracle's libm is a risk [I]. Minimal: `SELECT sqrt(4)`.
- **Slice 9: window functions.** Needs SELECT with ORDER BY, sorting, partitioning, and the aggregate machinery from slice 5. Order: row_number() OVER (ORDER BY ...) → rank/dense_rank → aggregate OVER with the default frame → ROWS frames → RANGE/GROUPS/EXCLUDE → named WINDOW and chaining → lag/lead/first_value/last_value/nth_value/ntile/percent_rank/cume_dist. Minimal: `SELECT row_number() OVER (ORDER BY x) FROM t`.
- **Slice 10: JSON.** Needs text and blob values, a JSON parser (RFC-8259 then JSON5), a 1000-depth limit, path parsing, and value **subtypes** in the expression evaluator. Subtypes are a new value attribute that must not survive storage. Order: json_valid(X) and json_error_position → json(X) → json_extract / `->` / `->>` → json_array / json_object / json_quote (need subtypes) → json_type / json_array_length → json_insert / replace / set / remove / patch → aggregates → JSONB (a binary format to reproduce byte-exactly, because jsonb() output is observable as a BLOB) → json_each / json_tree (needs table-valued-function support). Minimal: `SELECT json_valid('{}')`.
- **Not planned / refuse.** last_insert_rowid() (always 0 under WITHOUT ROWID), load_extension(), sqlite_offset(), soundex(), the percentile family (compile-gated), ordered-set syntax (compile-gated), and likely the printf() and substring() aliases. sqlite_version()/sqlite_source_id()/sqlite_compileoption_*() would report sql4s3's own values, so they can never match the oracle. Refuse them or define them as recorded non-deterministic inputs [I].
- **changes()/total_changes().** These need INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE first, plus the counting rules that exclude FK actions and REPLACE deletions from changes(). They are non-deterministic per the docs, so they need the recording mechanism.

### 5.2 Statement slices (from the statement extract)

Order of dependencies [I]:

1. `SELECT expr[, expr]` with no FROM. It needs the tokenizer (comments as whitespace, quoting rules), the expression evaluator, and the result-column naming rule. Slice: `SELECT 1`, then `SELECT 1 AS x`. Naming without AS must match the oracle, so start with literals and AS aliases.
2. `SELECT ... FROM t` (a single STRICT WITHOUT ROWID table) needs CREATE TABLE, INSERT ... VALUES, and catalog lookup with temp/main resolution. Minimal: `SELECT a FROM t`, then `*` and `t.*`, with the error for `*` without FROM.
3. WHERE needs three-valued boolean evaluation. Minimal: `SELECT a FROM t WHERE a = 1`.
4. ORDER BY needs comparison and collation (BINARY first), then integer-K and alias resolution, then ASC/DESC, then NULLS FIRST/LAST. Minimal: `ORDER BY 1`, `ORDER BY a DESC`.
5. LIMIT / OFFSET needs integer-conversion rules and their errors. Minimal: `LIMIT 1`, `LIMIT 1 OFFSET 1`. Refuse `LIMIT 1, 2`.
6. DISTINCT needs the IS DISTINCT FROM equality (int/real equal, BLOB affinity).
7. Aggregates: GROUP BY and HAVING need aggregate functions, the GROUP BY alias / K resolution, and the empty-input one-row rule. Refuse bare columns at first. Later, perhaps allow only the single min()/max() case.
8. Joins need multi-table name resolution. Order: comma/INNER JOIN + ON → USING/NATURAL (column merging and coalescing) → LEFT JOIN → RIGHT/FULL (3.39.0+ semantics) → CROSS JOIN (just INNER). Refuse the strange join names, ON/USING on comma joins, and outer joins without ON. Possibly require parentheses when comma joins and JOIN are mixed.
9. Subqueries in FROM need affinity/collation propagation of subquery columns.
10. Compound SELECT (UNION ALL → UNION / INTERSECT / EXCEPT) needs duplicate rules without affinity, compound ORDER BY matching, and LIMIT on the whole compound. Then VALUES as a compound of simple SELECTs (and its column names, from the oracle).
11. WITH (ordinary CTE) needs subqueries and views-as-scope. Then WITH RECURSIVE needs the queue algorithm and the LIMIT/OFFSET/ORDER BY-in-recursion rules. Require the RECURSIVE keyword. Refuse or ignore MATERIALIZED hints.
12. INSERT ... VALUES / SELECT / DEFAULT VALUES needs defaults, NOT NULL, the STRICT type check, and the WITHOUT ROWID PK uniqueness check (ABORT only at first). Refuse `REPLACE INTO`, and refuse the xfer-generated-column mismatch shape.
13. DELETE [WHERE] and UPDATE ... SET [WHERE]: SET evaluates all expressions before assigning. Refuse duplicate SET columns. Refuse INDEXED BY/NOT INDEXED and LIMIT/ORDER BY (they are compile-option-dependent).
14. changes() needs DML counting (direct changes only). last_insert_rowid() is a constant 0 (all tables WITHOUT ROWID).
15. BEGIN [DEFERRED|IMMEDIATE|EXCLUSIVE] / COMMIT / END / ROLLBACK need the statement-level undo (ABORT) and the nested-BEGIN error. Then SAVEPOINT / RELEASE / ROLLBACK TO need a savepoint stack with name matching.
16. Conflict algorithms OR ROLLBACK/ABORT/FAIL/IGNORE (FK always ABORT) need the transaction machinery. REPLACE last, or never: it needs delete side effects, the NOT NULL-default substitution, and the changes() exclusion, and it has "might change" behaviour.
17. UPSERT needs unique-constraint matching on the conflict target, the `excluded.` pseudo-table, and DO UPDATE with forced ABORT. Refuse INSERT ... SELECT without WHERE when ON CONFLICT follows.
18. RETURNING needs DML and buffering of all output until the changes are done. Refuse aggregates/window functions at the top level and references to tables other than the target.
19. UPDATE ... FROM needs joins. Consider a run-time refusal when one target row matches several FROM rows, because SQLite's choice is arbitrary [I].
20. ATTACH / DETACH / schema-qualified names, VACUUM, ANALYZE, REINDEX, EXPLAIN: they depend on the storage design. EXPLAIN output is "subject to change", so refuse it or exempt it from oracle comparison.

### 5.3 Schema slices (from the schema extract)

Suggested dependency order (all [I] unless quoted):

1. **CREATE TABLE minimal**: `CREATE TABLE name (col TYPE, ..., PRIMARY KEY(...)) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID` (accept either option order; reject duplicates and a missing option). Needs: identifier parsing (double-quote identifiers only), the type whitelist INT/INTEGER/REAL/TEXT/BLOB (no ANY), a mandatory PRIMARY KEY (column or table form), implicit NOT NULL on PK columns, rejection of the "sqlite_" prefix, rejection of rowid/oid/_rowid_ column names, and the error "table X already exists". Smallest syntax: `CREATE TABLE t(a INTEGER PRIMARY KEY) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;`. Depends on: a schema catalog and a store for the original SQL text (if sqlite_schema is ever exposed).
2. **INSERT/SELECT on such tables**: STRICT coercion (lossless conversions per affinity, SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_DATATYPE), PK uniqueness (SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_PRIMARYKEY), PK NOT NULL (SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL).
3. **DROP TABLE [IF EXISTS]**: smallest `DROP TABLE t;`. Later needs FK implicit-DELETE semantics.
4. **IF NOT EXISTS on CREATE TABLE**: trivial, but note the index-name clash rule.
5. **NOT NULL column constraint** (with no conflict-clause first). Then **DEFAULT literal / signed-number**. Then **DEFAULT (constant expr)** (needs constant-expression checking: no subquery, column ref, parameter or double-quoted string). Then CURRENT_* (needs a clock and statement-stable time).
6. **UNIQUE** (column, then table form). Needs secondary unique indexes and NULL-distinct semantics.
7. **CHECK** (column and table; refuse the conflict-clause on table CHECK; refuse non-deterministic functions and subqueries). Needs CAST-to-NUMERIC truth semantics and the error message format.
8. **COLLATE** on columns (BINARY, NOCASE, RTRIM only). Affects comparisons, indexes and FK matching.
9. **CREATE INDEX / CREATE UNIQUE INDEX** on columns, then ASC/DESC, then COLLATE. Then **partial indexes** (WHERE with deterministic expressions, no parameters or subqueries). Then **expression indexes** (deterministic only; the planner match is not needed for correctness). DROP INDEX [IF EXISTS].
10. **Conflict clauses** on PK/UNIQUE/NOT NULL (ROLLBACK/ABORT/FAIL/IGNORE/REPLACE): depends on statement-level ON CONFLICT and transaction machinery. REPLACE interacts with NOT NULL defaults and triggers. Postpone.
11. **Foreign keys** (always on): column REFERENCES, then table FOREIGN KEY. Parent must be PK or UNIQUE in the parent's CREATE TABLE (suggest refusing unique-index-only parents and unresolvable parents at CREATE time; stricter than SQLite but safe). Then NO ACTION immediate checks, then RESTRICT, CASCADE, SET NULL, SET DEFAULT, then `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` (needs explicit transactions and COMMIT-time checks with the transaction staying open on failure). Refuse MATCH, the other deferral spellings, and PRAGMA foreign_keys/defer_foreign_keys. Depends on: indexes for efficiency, transactions, DEFAULT (for SET DEFAULT).
12. **Generated columns**: VIRTUAL first (`c INTEGER AS (a+b)`), then STORED. Restrictions: no DEFAULT, not in PK, deterministic scalar functions only, no self/cyclic references, at least one non-generated column, STRICT typing (3.51.0). Depends on: the expression evaluator, STRICT coercion. Refuse `INSERT INTO t SELECT *` when widths mismatch.
13. **Views**: `CREATE VIEW v(c1,...) AS SELECT ...` (suggest requiring the column list). DROP VIEW [IF EXISTS]. Views are read-only (error on DML). Refuse TEMP initially.
14. **Triggers**: AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE ON table FOR EACH ROW [WHEN] BEGIN ... END first. Then BEFORE (consider limiting bodies), UPDATE OF (refuse unknown columns), RAISE(), INSTEAD OF on views (needs views). Body restrictions: unqualified names, no DEFAULT VALUES, no INDEXED BY, no ORDER BY/LIMIT, no top-level WITH. Refuse TEMP triggers and FOR EACH STATEMENT. Depends on: DML, NEW/OLD scoping, trigger recursion limits (FK actions count too).
15. **ALTER TABLE** (last; heavy dependence on schema-text rewriting if sqlite_schema.sql is observable): RENAME TO, then RENAME COLUMN, ADD COLUMN (with its restriction list), DROP COLUMN (with its failure list), ALTER COLUMN SET/DROP NOT NULL (3.53.0), ADD CHECK/DROP CONSTRAINT (3.53.0, diagram only, needs oracle exploration). Refuse legacy_alter_table and writable_schema.
16. **Never / refuse**: rowid tables, AUTOINCREMENT, sqlite_sequence, INTEGER PRIMARY KEY aliasing, ANY, CREATE TABLE AS SELECT (cannot be STRICT or WITHOUT ROWID), CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE, MATCH, PRAGMA foreign_keys / defer_foreign_keys / legacy_alter_table / ignore_check_constraints / writable_schema / recursive_triggers / count_changes, rowid/oid/_rowid_ references, last_insert_rowid() (or treat it as returning the oracle's value: unchanged by WITHOUT ROWID inserts), duplicate table options, NULLS FIRST/LAST in indexes (SQLite also refuses).
17. **Table-valued functions / eponymous vtabs** (json_each, json_tree, generate_series, pragma_* functions): need FROM-clause function syntax and HIDDEN-column argument binding ("more arguments than there are hidden columns" → error). Separate slice after JSON support. Not part of the schema-object work.

### 5.4 Result codes, limits and PRAGMA (from the configuration extract)

- Result codes: needed from the first slice (`SELECT 1` → ROW/DONE; malformed input → SQLITE_ERROR). [I] Model errors as (primary, extended, message, offset) to mirror sqlite3_extended_errcode / sqlite3_errmsg / sqlite3_error_offset. Have the harness compare extended codes. Compare messages only optionally.
- Constraint codes arrive with their features: NOTNULL and DATATYPE with STRICT CREATE TABLE plus INSERT; PRIMARYKEY with WITHOUT ROWID PKs; UNIQUE with UNIQUE constraints and indexes; CHECK with CHECK; FOREIGNKEY with REFERENCES (enforced from the start, oracle FK=on); TRIGGER and PINNED with triggers.
- TOOBIG: needs a length limit check in every string or blob producing operator (||, printf/format, zeroblob, randomblob, replace, group_concat...) [I]. Also an SQL text length check before parsing.
- RANGE: needed when parameter binding is added.
- MISSING_COLLSEQ: needed when COLLATE is added (minimum: BINARY, NOCASE, RTRIM built-ins [I]).
- Limits: each limit belongs with its feature: COLUMN (result set width: needed from `SELECT 1, 2, ...`), EXPR_DEPTH (expressions), COMPOUND_SELECT (UNION), FUNCTION_ARG (functions), VARIABLE_NUMBER (parameters), LIKE_PATTERN_LENGTH (LIKE), TRIGGER_DEPTH (triggers), ATTACHED (ATTACH). Decision: use the oracle build's actual values (VARIABLE_NUMBER = 250000), or configure the oracle with sqlite3_limit() to match sql4s3's values [I].
- PRAGMA: minimal slice = reject every `PRAGMA ...` statement with an explicit error. If introspection is ever needed, a later slice could offer read-only table-valued functions (pragma_table_info etc.); these need table-valued function support in FROM first [I].
- Column naming rules (AS / expression text / short column name) are needed from the first SELECT with a column reference or expression. `SELECT 1` gives column name "1" [I: rule 2 "copy of the expression text"].
- REAL-to-text output (FP_DIGITS=17) is needed as soon as REAL literals are returned as text.

---

## 6. Semantic areas where exact compatibility is hard

§6.1 to §6.10 cover the areas named in the brief. §6.11 onward hold the detailed rule lists from the four extracts.

### 6.1 Expression typing and affinity in comparisons, even with STRICT columns

- [V] STRICT only changes what may be *stored*. Columns of a STRICT table still have an affinity. Comparisons apply affinity by these rules: "If one operand has INTEGER, REAL or NUMERIC affinity and the other operand has TEXT or BLOB or no affinity then NUMERIC affinity is applied to the other operand. If one operand has TEXT affinity and the other has no affinity, then TEXT affinity is applied to the other operand. Otherwise, no affinity is applied and both operands are compared as is." https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#compaff
- [I] Consequences for STRICT columns:
  - `int_col = '5'` converts '5' to 5 and can be true.
  - `text_col = 5` converts 5 to '5'.
  - `+int_col = '5'` applies no affinity and is always false, because "Any operators applied to column names, including the no-op unary "+" operator, convert the column name into an expression which always has no affinity" [V https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#expraff].
  - `blob_col = 'x'` applies no conversion.
- [V] The right side of `IN (list)` has no affinity, even for column values or CASTs: "a IN (x, y, z, ...)" is equivalent to "a = +x OR a = +y OR ...". `x IN (SELECT y ...)` behaves like `x=y`. BETWEEN is two separate comparisons, "even if that means different affinities are applied to 'a' in each of the comparisons". https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#compaff
- [V] Columns of a view or subquery take the affinity of their expression. Literals and computed expressions have none. For compound SELECTs the choice "is indeterminate". https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affview
- [V] A CASE with a base expression compares "as if the base expression and WHEN expression are respectively the left- and right-hand operands of an = operator". https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#case
- [V] Foreign keys compare child and parent using the parent's affinity and collation. https://sqlite.org/foreignkeys.html#parentchild
- [V] GROUP BY, DISTINCT and compound operators apply no affinity: "No affinities are applied to any values as the result of a GROUP by clause", and UNION, INTERSECT and EXCEPT compare values "as is". https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html (§6), https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#distinct

### 6.2 Numeric literal parsing

- [V] A literal is REAL if it has a decimal point or an exponent, or if it lies outside the 64-bit range. Hexadecimal literals are two's complement, so `0x8000000000000000` is the minimum integer. Underscores between digits are allowed since 3.46.0. https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#litvalue, #hexint
- [V] `-9223372036854775808` is accepted as an integer literal: "Allow the value -9223372036854775808 as an integer literal". https://sqlite.org/changes.html
- [V] The text-to-binary64 routines were "rewritten to use 64-bit integer arithmetic instead of floating-point computations" in the release that introduced FP_DIGITS (dated 3.52.0 on the page, shipped in 3.53.0). https://sqlite.org/floatingpoint.html#*fpdigits
- [I] Whether SQLite's decimal-to-double conversion is correctly rounded in every case is not documented. If it is not, sql4s3 must port SQLite's algorithm rather than use Go's `strconv.ParseFloat`. Probe with hard cases: halfway values, subnormals, numbers with 20 or more digits, and `1e309`, which should give Inf.

### 6.3 Text-to-number conversion

There are at least five rule sets, and each must be reproduced separately. [V]

| Context | Rule | URL |
|---|---|---|
| Affinity on store and in comparisons | The conversion applies only if lossless and only for a "well-formed integer or real literal". Hexadecimal is not well-formed. '3.0e+5' becomes the integer 300000. Text for an integer that is too big becomes REAL. About 15.95 significant digits are kept. | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affinity |
| CAST AS INTEGER / REAL | The longest numeric prefix is used and the rest ignored. Leading spaces are skipped. No prefix gives 0. Results saturate at ±2^63. `CAST('123e+5' AS INTEGER)` is 123. | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#castexpr |
| CAST AS NUMERIC | INTEGER if the text looks like an integer that fits. Text that looks like a float becomes INTEGER if it converts losslessly "back and forth between IEEE 754 64-bit float and a 51-bit signed integer". | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#castexpr |
| Arithmetic operands | The conversion is "performed even if it is lossy and irreversible". Text that looks real becomes REAL (no conversion of 3.0 to INTEGER). "does not look in any way numeric" becomes 0. | https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html (§5) |
| Boolean context | Cast to NUMERIC, then zero is false. | https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#booleanexpr |
| Function-specific | sign() returns NULL for text that "cannot be losslessly converted", while abs() returns 0.0 for unconvertible text. Math functions return NULL for a string that is "not readily converted". LIMIT needs a lossless integer. | https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#sign, https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html, https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#limitoffset |

- [I] Leading and trailing whitespace, a leading `+`, `.5`, `5.`, `1e`, `inf`, `nan` and `0x10` should each be probed in every context.

### 6.4 Floating-point formatting and rounding

- [V] The default REAL-to-text algorithm since 3.53.0: "Round to 15 digits. Convert the text result back to binary-64. If the binary-64 value from the previous step is different, then: Redo step (a) with 17-digit rounding." Before that, rounding was always to 15 digits. `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_FP_DIGITS` selects the digit count. https://sqlite.org/floatingpoint.html#*fpdigits, https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html
- [V] format() and printf() are capped at 16 significant digits for `%f`, `%e` and `%g`, or 26 with the `!` flag. https://sqlite.org/printf.html
- [V] NaN is converted to NULL ("Always convert IEEE floating point NaN values into NULL", 3.5.9). Infinity renders as "Inf", or as "9.0e+999" with some flags. https://sqlite.org/changes.html, https://sqlite.org/printf.html
- [I] Still to determine by probing: the exponent format (`1e+20` vs `1.0e+20`), the cutover between plain and exponent notation, whether `.0` is appended to integral REALs (for example whether CAST(1.0 AS TEXT) gives '1.0'), the rendering of -0.0, and the output of `quote()`. None of these is specified on the pages read.
- [V] For TEXT/REAL conversions, "about 15.95 significant decimal digits of the number are preserved". https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affinity
- [V] sum() of REAL values is not compensated: the docs give an example of "an imprecise result". https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#sumunc. [I] The summation order and algorithm must match bit for bit.

### 6.5 Collation and sort order across types

- [V] Sort order across classes: NULL first, then INTEGER and REAL "interspersed in numeric order", then TEXT by collation, then BLOB by memcmp. "No storage class conversions occur before the sort." https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html (§6), https://sqlite.org/fileformat2.html (§2.2)
- [I] How an INTEGER compares with a REAL beyond 2^53 (for example 9007199254740993 vs 9007199254740992.0) must be exact. SQLite does not compare by converting to double alone. Probe this.
- [V] Collation selection in a comparison: an explicit COLLATE wins, leftmost first. Then a column operand's collation, leftmost first; a column with unary + or CAST still counts as a column here, unlike for affinity. Otherwise BINARY. https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#colrules
- [V] The ORDER BY collation follows a four-step rule, and a compound's ORDER BY has its own matching rules. https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#orderby
- [V] NOCASE folds only ASCII A–Z and treats U+0000 as a terminator. RTRIM ignores trailing spaces only. https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#collation
- [V] The order of ties is undefined. https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#orderby

### 6.6 Result column names

- [V] "If there is no AS clause then the name of the column is unspecified and may change from one release of SQLite to the next." https://sqlite.org/c3ref/column_name.html
- [V] The deprecated `full_column_names` page documents the current default rules: "If there is an AS clause on the result, then the name of the column is the right-hand side of the AS clause." "If the result is a general expression, not just the name of a source table column, then the name of the result is a copy of the expression text." With `short_column_names` on (the default), a column reference is named after the column alone. https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_full_column_names
- [V] Compound SELECTs take their names from the left-most SELECT. https://sqlite.org/changes.html
- [V] View column names without a column list "are not a defined part of the interface". https://sqlite.org/lang_createview.html
- [I] "Copy of the expression text" means the parser must keep the exact source span of each result expression, with its whitespace, case and comments. Names for `*`, for subqueries, for `t.*` after joins, for USING columns, for VALUES (`column1`, ...?) and for CTE columns must all come from oracle probes.

### 6.7 typeof() results

- [V] typeof() returns one of "null", "integer", "real", "text" or "blob". https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#typeof
- [V] A REAL stored in a REAL column may be kept on disk as an integer, but this is "completely invisible at the SQL level". https://sqlite.org/datatype3.html#affinity
- [I] Expression results stay dynamically typed even in a STRICT-only database:
  - `1 + 1.0` is real;
  - `5 / 2` is integer 2;
  - `'5' + 0` is integer, and `'5.0' + 0` is real;
  - `sum()` of integers is integer;
  - `avg()` is always real;
  - `round()` returns real;
  - `iif()` and `coalesce()` return whatever their branch returns;
  - `max(1, 1.0)` must be probed.

### 6.8 Integer overflow to REAL

- [V] `+ - *`: "floating point arithmetic ... if ... integer arithmetic would produce an overflow". https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#binaryops
- [V] Other overflow behaviour:
  - abs() of the minimum integer is an error;
  - sum() overflow is an error, but total() never overflows;
  - an integer literal out of range is REAL;
  - integer-like text that is too large becomes REAL under affinity;
  - CAST to INTEGER saturates.

  https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#abs, https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#sumunc, https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#castexpr
- [V] sum() raises its error only "if all inputs so far are integers or NULL". The row order therefore decides whether an error occurs. https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#sumunc
- [I] Integer `/` and `%` with -9223372036854775808 and -1 must be probed: the C operation overflows, and SQLite handles it specially.

### 6.9 Date and time functions

- [V] Only the enumerated time-value formats are accepted, and anything else gives NULL rather than an error. Modifiers are case sensitive. Behaviour outside the years 0000–9999 is "undefined". 'localtime' and 'utc' depend on the host time zone. 'now' is stable within one `sqlite3_step()`. See §6.11 (date/time) for the full list. https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html
- [I] Recommendation: start with deterministic inputs only. Refuse 'localtime' and 'utc', or fix the time zone on both sides. Record 'now' as a non-deterministic input, as the spec requires.

### 6.10 Deterministic versus non-deterministic functions

- [V] The documented non-deterministic built-ins are random(), randomblob(), changes(), last_insert_rowid(), sqlite_version(), and date/time functions that use 'now', 'localtime' or 'utc', or that have no arguments. https://sqlite.org/deterministic.html
- [V] Non-deterministic functions are prohibited in partial-index WHERE clauses, expression indexes and generated columns. In those contexts the date/time functions "will throw an exception". CHECK constraints should also be restricted, but "due to an historical bug ... that restriction is not actually enforced". https://sqlite.org/deterministic.html
- [V] "within a single SQL statement" defines the scope of SQLITE_DETERMINISTIC. https://sqlite.org/c3ref/create_function.html
- [I] sql4s3 should refuse non-deterministic functions in CHECK, a refusal that SQLite does not make. It needs a per-function flag table. The oracle's flags can be dumped with `PRAGMA function_list`.

### 6.11 Function rules

**Integer overflow and summation**
- abs(-9223372036854775808) "throws an integer overflow error" (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#abs).
- sum() raises "integer overflow" only while all inputs so far are integers or NULL. "No overflow error is ever raised if any prior input was a floating point value." [I] So the order of rows matters for whether the error occurs. total() never overflows (https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#sumunc).
- sum() of integers returns INTEGER. With any non-integer input it returns REAL "an approximation". total() is always REAL and gives 0.0 for no rows. sum() of no rows gives NULL.
- Infinity in sum/total: "If very large values with differing signs are used such that the SUM() or TOTAL() function is unable to determine if the correct result is +Infinity or -Infinity or some other value in between, then the result is NULL." Example: `WITH t1(x) AS (VALUES(1.0),(-9e+999),(2.0),(+9e+999),(3.0)) SELECT sum(x)` → NULL. Overflow beyond the double range → ±Infinity (https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#sumunc). Related fixes landed in 3.43.1 and 3.45.3, so exact behaviour must be probed.
- sum() of floats is not compensated: the 1.55e+308 example "is an imprecise result" (https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#sumunc). [I] The engine must use the same summation algorithm as SQLite to get bit-identical results. The docs do not say whether SQLite uses plain or Kahan-style summation, so probe it.

**Aggregate typing**
- avg() is "total()/count()". It is always REAL with ≥1 non-NULL input. Non-numeric strings and blobs count as 0 (https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#avg).
- count(X) counts non-NULL X. count(*) counts rows.
- min/max aggregates: "the value that would be returned last in an ORDER BY" or first, so they follow the cross-type sort order and collation [I] (https://sqlite.org/lang_aggfunc.html#max_agg).

**Scalar edge cases**
- Scalar max() returns NULL if ANY arg is NULL [V]. Scalar min() is docs silent [V asymmetry]. Collation comes from the leftmost arg that defines one, else BINARY. The same rule applies to nullif() (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#max_scalar).
- round(X,Y) returns REAL. A negative or omitted Y is taken as 0. The half-way rule is not stated. changes 3.29.0 changed rounding toward "what people who are used to thinking in decimal actually expect" (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#round). [I] Probe many .5 cases and large magnitudes, and check whether round(int) returns REAL.
- substr: 1-based. A negative Y counts from the right. A negative Z returns the abs(Z) characters *preceding* Y. Text counts codepoints and blobs count bytes. Y=0 and out-of-range behaviour is docs silent, so probe `substr('abc',0,2)`, `substr('abc',-5,2)` and `substr(x,1,-1)` (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#substr).
- length(): counts codepoints of text "prior to the first U+0000". It counts bytes for blobs. Numbers are measured as the length of their text form, which depends on FP_DIGITS for REAL. octet_length() counts bytes of the text encoding (UTF-8 here) (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#length, #octet_length).
- hex() of a number renders it as UTF-8 text first: hex(12345678) = '3132333435363738' (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#hex).
- instr(): BLOB positions apply only when *both* args are BLOBs. Otherwise both are read as strings and positions count characters (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#instr).
- Case folding is ASCII only: lower(), upper(), and LIKE ('a' LIKE 'A' true, 'æ' LIKE 'Æ' false). GLOB is case sensitive (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#lower; https://sqlite.org/lang_expr.html#like).
- like()/glob() take the pattern FIRST: like(X,Y) = "Y LIKE X" (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#like).
- replace() with an empty Y returns X unchanged. Z is cast to text. Comparison is BINARY (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#replace).
- quote(): strings with an embedded NUL are truncated before the NUL. BLOBs → X'..'. [I] For REAL the output text must round-trip and match the oracle digit for digit.
- unhex(): any non-hex char not in Y → NULL. Hex digits must come in adjacent pairs. Hex digits inside Y are ignored for the skip-set (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#unhex).
- sign(): returns NULL for a string "that cannot be losslessly converted". abs() returns 0.0 for an unconvertible string. **Different conversion rules apply to different functions** (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#sign, #abs).
- Math functions return NULL for NULL, blob, or "a string that is not readily converted", and for domain errors, never an error (https://sqlite.org/lang_mathfunc.html).
- concat() of all NULLs → ''. concat_ws(NULL,...) → NULL. concat_ws includes empty strings (3.50.2) (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#concat_ws).
- randomblob(N) with N<1 → a 1-byte blob (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#randomblob).
- char() with zero args → ''. Invalid codepoints are not an error (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#char).
- iif()/coalesce()/ifnull() short-circuit, so unused args are never evaluated. This matters for errors in unused branches, such as abs(-9223372036854775808) inside an unchosen branch (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#iif).

**format()/printf()**
- FORMAT NULL → NULL. Missing args behave as NULL, giving 0, 0.0 or ''. %s of NULL → ''. %Q of NULL → NULL. %J of NULL → null (https://sqlite.org/lang_corefunc.html#format; https://sqlite.org/printf.html).
- Width and precision count BYTES by default and characters with "!". For multi-byte UTF-8 `%5s` pads by bytes (https://sqlite.org/printf.html#alt2).
- Float output is limited to 16 significant digits (26 with "!") (https://sqlite.org/printf.html §1.2).
- "0" flag: Inf/NaN render as "9.0e+999"/"null", otherwise "Inf"/"NaN". "-" is ignored when "0" is present. "#" with %f suppresses "-0.00" (3.51.0) (https://sqlite.org/printf.html#alt1).
- %d of a REAL argument: docs silent. %c uses the first character of a string. Length modifiers are ignored and values are always 64-bit (https://sqlite.org/printf.html).
- `,` flag works for integers and (since 3.42.0) floats, and only left of the decimal point (https://sqlite.org/printf.html#comma).

**Date/time**
- Only the 12 enumerated time-value formats are accepted. Everything else (and every bad modifier) → NULL, not an error. "Modifiers are case sensitive". Extra spaces break them ("end  of day") (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval, §3.1).
- Exception: in deterministic-only contexts, 'now', 'localtime', 'utc' or no args "throw an exception" instead (https://sqlite.org/deterministic.html#dtexception).
- Time-only formats assume 2000-01-01. Timezone suffix "[+-]HH:MM" is subtracted. "Z" is a no-op (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval).
- Fractional seconds: only 3 digits are significant, but any number of digits is accepted (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval).
- A numeric time-value is a Julian day by default. 'unixepoch' or 'auto' must follow immediately. The 'auto' ranges are 0.0–5373484.499999 for JD and -210866760000..253402300799 for unix (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#automod).
- Modifier NNN may be "any floating point number" with a "+"/"-" prefix. The trailing "s" is optional. Time shifts 7–13 are applied year→month→day→time. The default month/year overflow resolution is "ceiling", and "floor" goes to the last day of the previous month (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmshf, #dtambg).
- weekday N: the N argument must be an integer -6..+6. "weekday -0" means the previous Sunday or the same day (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#wdmod).
- 'julianday' modifier on non-numeric input → NULL (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#jdmod).
- subsec/subsecond can be the first argument (it implies 'now'). It only affects datetime(), time(), unixepoch() and strftime %s (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#subsec).
- julianday() returns REAL, unixepoch() INTEGER (REAL with subsec), and date()/time()/datetime() text. The strftime equivalents %J and %s give text with "inexactitude" (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#strftm).
- Every day is 86400 s with no leap seconds. Gregorian calendar only. Results outside 0000-01-01..9999-12-31 (JD 1721059.5–5373484.5) are "undefined" (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html §5).
- 'now' is the same for all invocations within one sqlite3_step() (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmval). [I] sql4s3 must capture 'now' once per step and record it.
- timediff(A,B) gives "(+|-)YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS.SSS". It is human-oriented, so the same output can cover different day counts (https://sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html#tmdif).

**JSON**
- Malformed JSON text → error. Exceptions: json_valid, json_quote and json_error_position. The error message text is not quoted on these pages. The only quoted message is "malformed JSON" (JSONB case). [I] Get the exact message and code from the oracle; most likely SQLITE_ERROR (https://sqlite.org/json1.html §3.1, §3.2.2).
- Subtype propagation: a value arg is JSON only when it comes "directly" from a JSON function or `->`, never from `->>` or a column read. [I] A subquery, CTE, view or table column may drop the subtype, so probe each. The 3.47.0 "indexed-subtype-expr" optimization and SQLITE_RESULT_SUBTYPE notes show that subtype loss is optimizer-sensitive (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#varg; changes 3.47.0; https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqliteresultsubtype).
- json_extract with one path returns SQL values (true→1, false→0, null→NULL, strings dequoted, containers as JSON text). With several paths it returns a JSON array text. `->` always returns JSON text and `->>` always an SQL value (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jex, #jptr).
- The `->`/`->>` RHS: a label X → '$.X'. An integer N → '$[N]' or '$[#-K]'. An integer-looking *string* stays a label (3.46.0) (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jptr).
- json_array/json_object/json_insert/json_replace/json_set raise an error on any BLOB argument. Yet "Any SQL function parameter that accepts text JSON as an input will also accept a BLOB in the JSONB format", so the first ("json") argument of json_set and similar accepts JSONB. [I] The wording conflicts for the first argument of the json_insert family, so probe it (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jins, #jsonbx).
- JSON5 is accepted on input. Inf/NaN extensions: "Inf"/"Infinity" in any case, and NaN/QNaN/SNaN "as just an alternative spellings for "null"". "+NaN"/"-NaN" are rejected. Unquoted keys may contain any char >U+007f that is not whitespace (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#json5).
- json() preserves duplicate labels. json_remove(x,'$') → NULL. json_array_length of a non-array → 0, of a missing path → NULL. json_type of a missing path → NULL. json_valid with NULL X or Y → NULL, with flags outside 1..15 → error. json_quote(NULL) → 'null' (https://sqlite.org/json1.html).
- json_group_array includes NULLs. json_group_object skips NULL labels. Empty input gives [] or {} (not NULL) (https://sqlite.org/json1.html#jgrouparray).
- Number formatting in JSON output: [I] REAL→text follows the FP_DIGITS default of 17 and must match exactly.

**Window functions**
- The default frame is RANGE UNBOUNDED PRECEDING..CURRENT ROW, which includes peers, so peers share a value (https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#framespec).
- Built-ins other than first_value, last_value and nth_value ignore the frame. FILTER on a built-in window function is a syntax error. DISTINCT is not allowed. Window functions are allowed only in the result set and ORDER BY (https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html).
- Without ORDER BY, row_number()/ntile() order is "arbitrary". [I] Reject these without ORDER BY, or the oracle cannot compare results.
- ntile puts larger groups first. percent_rank and cume_dist return REAL (https://sqlite.org/windowfunctions.html#builtins).

**Determinism**
- Documented non-deterministic: random(), randomblob(), changes(), last_insert_rowid(), sqlite_version(), and date/time with 'now', 'localtime', 'utc' or no args (https://sqlite.org/deterministic.html; https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_deterministic.html#sqlitedeterministic). [I] Also total_changes(), sqlite_source_id(), sqlite_compileoption_*() and load_extension(). All JSON functions are DETERMINISTIC+INNOCUOUS [V].
- SQLITE_DETERMINISTIC means "within a single SQL statement" (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/create_function.html).

### 6.12 Statement rules

- Result column names. [V] "The name of a result column is the value of the "AS" clause for that column, if there is an AS clause. If there is no AS clause then the name of the column is unspecified and may change from one release of SQLite to the next." (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/column_name.html). [V] Compound names come "from the left-most SELECT" (chg 3.3.5) and show "only the name of the column of the original table and omit the table name" (chg 3.6.1). [I] The engine must match 3.54.1's names without AS. In practice that is the original expression text as typed for expressions, and the bare column name for column references. Test this exhaustively against the oracle, or put names without AS outside the contract.
- `*` / `alias.*` expansion. [V] "It is an error to use a "*" or "alias.*" expression in any context other than a result expression list. It is also an error to use a "*" or "alias.*" expression in a simple SELECT query that does not have a FROM clause." (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#resultset). [V] With USING/NATURAL, the shared column appears once: the left column for INNER/LEFT, the right column for RIGHT, and coalesce(lhs.X,rhs.X) for FULL (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#fromclause).
- USING comparison. [V] "the normal rules for handling affinities, collation sequences and NULL values in comparisons apply. The column from the dataset on the left-hand side of the join-operator is considered to be on the left-hand side of the comparison operator (=) for the purposes of collation sequence and affinity precedence." (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#fromclause).
- WHERE / HAVING / ON truthiness. [V] Rows are excluded "if the WHERE clause evaluates to either false or NULL" (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#whereclause). [V] DELETE: "Rows for which the expression is false or NULL are retained." (https://sqlite.org/lang_delete.html).
- Outer join ON vs WHERE. [V] "the extra NULL rows for non-matched rows on the other operand are added after ON clause processing but before WHERE clause processing." (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#whereclause).
- Outer join without ON. [V] It "behave[s] like an inner join" ("as if the ON clause where "ON true"") (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#flexible_join_syntax).
- Join associativity. [V] "((A join-op-1 B) join-op-2 C)"; comma and JOIN have equal precedence (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#precedence_of_comma_joins_and_cross_join).
- Aggregate without GROUP BY on an empty input. [V] It "always returns exactly one row"; non-aggregate expressions are "evaluated against a row consisting entirely of NULL values" (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#resultset).
- GROUP BY equality. [V] "NULL values are considered equal". Text uses the usual collation selection. GROUP BY terms follow the ORDER BY term-resolution rules (integer K, then alias) (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#resultset). [V] "Identifiers in GROUP BY clauses always prefer output column names" (chg 3.8.0). [I] So `SELECT a AS b, ... GROUP BY b`, where b is also a table column, groups by the alias. This differs from ORDER BY expressions and WHERE.
- Name resolution precedence between aliases and columns. [V] "Bare identifiers in ORDER BY clauses bind more tightly to output column names, but identifiers in expressions bind more tightly to input column names." (chg 3.8.0). [V] WHERE uses AS names "if there are no other matches" (chg 3.7.17). [V] ORDER BY prefers the result alias over the table column (chg 3.5.4).
- DISTINCT equality. [V] Values are compared "using the IS DISTINCT FROM operator... An integer is equal to a floating point number if they represent the same quantity... BLOB affinity is used for DISTINCT comparisons, hence no type coercions occur." (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#distinct). [I] So 1 and 1.0 are duplicates, and which representative is kept (the first seen?) shows in the output. Test with the oracle.
- Compound duplicate rules. [V] NULLs are equal to each other. The collation is chosen as for `=` "except that greater precedence is not assigned to a collation sequence specified with the postfix COLLATE operator". "No affinity transformations are applied" (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#compound).
- Compound column count. [V] "all the constituent SELECTs must return the same number of result columns" (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#compound).
- Compound left-associativity with no operator precedence. [V] "(A op B op C) is processed as ((A op B) op C)" (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#compound).
- Compound ORDER BY matching. [V] A non-integer term is matched against the left-most SELECT's result columns first (alias or exact expression), then the next one. "If no matching expression can be found in the result columns of any constituent SELECT, it is an error. Each term of the ORDER BY clause is processed separately and may be matched against result columns from different SELECT statements in the compound." (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#orderby).
- ORDER BY collation selection, 4-step rule (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#orderby). It ends with: "Otherwise, the BINARY collation sequence is used."
- NULL ordering. [V] NULLs sort first in ASC and last in DESC by default. NULLS FIRST/LAST override this (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#nullslast).
- Unstable ties. [V] The order of rows that tie on all ORDER BY keys "is undefined" (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#orderby). [I] Differential tests must order by unique keys, or compare as multisets.
- LIMIT/OFFSET evaluation. [V] The value must be an integer "or a value that can be losslessly converted to an integer"; NULL or a lossy value gives "an error"; a negative LIMIT means no limit; a negative OFFSET means 0 (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#limitoffset). [I] So '5' (text) and 5.0 are accepted, while 5.5, 'abc' and NULL are errors. Error text and code are not documented here; take them from the oracle.
- LIMIT comma order. [V] `LIMIT a, b` means OFFSET a, LIMIT b (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#limitoffset).
- VALUES restrictions. [V] VALUES cannot be followed by ORDER BY or LIMIT. A compound whose last element is VALUES cannot have ORDER BY/LIMIT. A bare VALUES cannot be preceded by WITH, but a compound starting with VALUES can (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#values, section 1). [I] The result column names of VALUES (column1, column2, ...) are not on these pages. Get them from the oracle.
- Recursive CTE semantics: the queue algorithm, UNION dedup against everything ever queued, LIMIT counting rows added to the recursive table, OFFSET skipping rows but still processing them, ORDER BY as a priority queue, FIFO otherwise (https://sqlite.org/lang_with.html#recursivecte).
- UPDATE evaluation. [V] "all scalar expressions are evaluated before any assignments are made"; for a duplicated column, "all but the rightmost occurrence is ignored" (https://sqlite.org/lang_update.html).
- UPSERT. [V] Only uniqueness constraints are caught. Clauses are tried in order, and the first matching target wins. Unqualified column names in DO UPDATE are the old values, and `excluded.` gives the new ones. DO UPDATE always uses ABORT, "even if the DO UPDATE clause is contained within an INSERT statement or a trigger that specifies some other conflict resolution algorithm" (https://sqlite.org/lang_upsert.html).
- Conflict algorithms. [V] ABORT undoes the statement's changes and keeps the transaction. ROLLBACK undoes the transaction (ABORT if in autocommit). FAIL keeps prior row changes of the statement, but "A foreign key constraint violation causes an ABORT". IGNORE skips the row but is "like ABORT for foreign key constraint errors". REPLACE deletes conflicting rows; for NOT NULL it uses the default value or else ABORT; CHECK/FK give ABORT. All raise SQLITE_CONSTRAINT (https://sqlite.org/lang_conflict.html). [I] The extended codes (SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE / _PRIMARYKEY / _NOTNULL / _CHECK / _FOREIGNKEY / _DATATYPE) and messages come from other pages or the oracle.
- [V] "The algorithm specified in the OR clause of an INSERT or UPDATE overrides any algorithm specified in a CREATE TABLE." (https://sqlite.org/lang_conflict.html).
- RETURNING. [V] All changes happen in the first sqlite3_step(). Output order is arbitrary. Values are post-change for INSERT/UPDATE and pre-change for DELETE. No trigger effects. No top-level aggregates or window functions. Only the target table may be referenced (https://sqlite.org/lang_returning.html). [I] Since row order is arbitrary, compare as multisets.
- changes() rules. [V] Only direct changes count; trigger, FK-action and REPLACE deletions are excluded; non-DML statements leave the value unchanged; CREATE TABLE AS is not counted; the value is saved and restored around triggers (https://sqlite.org/c3ref/changes.html). [I] Consequences: `SELECT changes()` after a SELECT or DDL returns the count of the previous DML. An `INSERT OR IGNORE` that skips rows counts only the inserted rows. An UPSERT DO UPDATE counts the updated row (not documented here; check with the oracle).
- Transaction errors. [V] BEGIN inside a transaction (including one started by SAVEPOINT) "will fail with an error". RELEASE / ROLLBACK TO with an unknown name fails "and leaves the state of the database unchanged". A ROLLBACK after an automatic rollback "will fail with an error". COMMIT with pending writes gives SQLITE_BUSY (https://sqlite.org/lang_transaction.html, https://sqlite.org/lang_savepoint.html). [I] The exact message strings are not on these pages and need the oracle. Likely texts, not verified: "cannot start a transaction within a transaction", "no such savepoint: NAME", "cannot commit - no transaction is active", "cannot rollback - no transaction is active".
- Savepoint stack semantics. [V] RELEASE removes back to and including the most recent match. ROLLBACK TO keeps the matched savepoint on the stack and cancels the inner ones. When the outermost SAVEPOINT is outside BEGIN, it acts as BEGIN DEFERRED. RELEASE of the outermost savepoint commits. Names need not be unique, and the most recent match wins (https://sqlite.org/lang_savepoint.html#transaction_nesting_rules).
- Same-connection read-during-write. [V] It is "undefined" whether a running SELECT sees changes made after it started on the same connection. It may return a row twice or return a deleted row (https://sqlite.org/isolation.html). [I] sql4s3 can choose any behaviour, but tests must not depend on it.
- Comments. [V] `--` runs to the newline or end of input. `/*` runs to `*/` or end of input. They do not nest. They are whitespace and allowed anywhere whitespace is (https://sqlite.org/lang_comment.html). [I] Unterminated `/* ...` at end of input is legal. `SELECT 1/**/` is legal. A comment between tokens such as `- -` versus `--` matters.
- Quoting. [V] 'x' is a string. "x" is an identifier. [x] and `x` are identifiers. There are two fallbacks (https://sqlite.org/lang_keywords.html). Within the project's decided subset, "x" that fails to resolve must be an error. [I] The oracle accepts it, so such statements must be classed as "refused" in test generation.
- Keyword-as-identifier fallback (https://sqlite.org/quirks.html#keywords_can_often_be_used_as_identifiers). [I] The set of keywords that can be used unquoted, and the contexts, follow SQLite's parser %fallback ID list. That list is not documented on these pages.
- REINDEX name resolution. [V] A collation name takes precedence over index/table names. `REINDEX EXPRESSIONS` also rebuilds things named "expressions" (https://sqlite.org/lang_reindex.html).
- Unqualified object resolution order. [V] temp, then main, then attached in attach order, first match wins. Objects of the wrong type are ignored (https://sqlite.org/lang_naming.html).
- Bare columns. [V] With exactly one built-in min()/max(), bare columns come from the row holding the extreme value. Otherwise the row is arbitrary (https://sqlite.org/lang_select.html#bareagg). [I] Deterministic matching is possible only for the single-min/max, no-ties case.
- UPDATE FROM with many matches. [V] The row used is "arbitrary" (https://sqlite.org/lang_update.html#upfrom). It cannot be matched deterministically.
- INSERT column-count errors. [V] Example message: "Parse error: table dest has 2 columns but 4 values were supplied" (CLI prefix "Parse error:") (https://sqlite.org/lang_insert.html#the_xfer_optimization_and_generated_columns).

### 6.13 Schema rules

- CHECK evaluation: "the expression associated with each CHECK constraint is evaluated and cast to a NUMERIC value in the same way as a CAST expression. If the result is zero (integer value 0 or real value 0.0), then a constraint violation has occurred. If the CHECK expression evaluates to NULL, or any other non-zero value, it is not a constraint violation." CT#ckconst. [I] So `CHECK('abc')` → CAST('abc' AS NUMERIC)=0 → violation. `CHECK('1x')` → 1 → OK. `CHECK(0.0)` fails. `CHECK(NULL)` passes. Affinity is applied before the CHECK (CHG 3.32.0) [V].
- CHECK may not contain a subquery [V CT#ckconst]. Non-deterministic functions are silently allowed (cross-page deterministic.html) [V].
- CHECK failure error names the constraint (3.7.12) and messages were improved in 3.34.0 [V CHG]. [I] The message format "CHECK constraint failed: <name or expr text>" must be taken from the oracle.
- PK/UNIQUE NULL distinctness: "NULL values are considered distinct from all other values, including other NULLs" CT#primkeyconst, #uniqueconst. Under WITHOUT ROWID/STRICT the PK cannot be NULL, but UNIQUE columns still allow many NULLs [V].
- DEFAULT constant-ness: "an expression is considered constant if it contains no sub-queries, column or table references, bound parameters, or string literals enclosed in double-quotes instead of single-quotes." CT#dfltval. Parenthesized expressions are "evaluated once for each row inserted" (so `DEFAULT (random())` differs per row) [V]. DEFAULT `-(-9223372036854775808)` gives a float ≈ +9.223372036854775808e18 (CHG 3.8.4) [V].
- CURRENT_TIME/DATE/TIMESTAMP default formats "HH:MM:SS", "YYYY-MM-DD", "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS", UTC [V CT#dfltval]. [I] Within one statement all rows get the same time (statement-stable 'now'). Verify against the oracle.
- NOT NULL plus REPLACE: CHG 3.27.0 "Fix the REPLACE command so that it is no longer able to sneak a NULL value into a NOT NULL column even if the NOT NULL column has a default value of NULL." [V]. Under REPLACE conflict resolution, a NOT NULL violation uses the column default [I, lang_conflict]. Match exactly if OR REPLACE is supported.
- NaN into NOT NULL → error (3.42.0). NaN is converted to NULL (CHG 3.5.9 "Always convert IEEE floating point NaN values into NULL") [V].
- Conflict-clause precedence: an outer statement's ON CONFLICT overrides the constraint default. Inside triggers the outer statement's policy wins [V lang_createtrigger]. CHECK is always ABORT [V].
- FK child/parent comparison: parent collation, and parent affinity applied to the child value [V FK#parentchild]. FK satisfied if ANY child column is NULL (MATCH SIMPLE) [V].
- FK RESTRICT fires immediately, even when deferred. NO ACTION checks at statement end (immediate) or COMMIT (deferred) [V FK#fk_actions].
- ON UPDATE action only when the new parent key is not equal to the old: `UPDATE parent SET x='key'` where x was already 'key' does not run SET NULL [V FK#fk_actions]. [I] "Not equal" presumably uses the parent column collation and type (e.g. 1 vs 1.0). Verify against the oracle.
- FK action order: BEFORE triggers, local constraints, row change, FK actions, AFTER triggers [V].
- Deferred FK COMMIT failure leaves the transaction open: "The transaction remains open." [V FK#fk_deferred]. Without an explicit transaction, deferred acts like immediate [V].
- FK DML errors appear at prepare time of statements that touch child or parent: "prevent the application from preparing SQL statements that modify the content of the child or parent tables in ways that use the foreign keys" [V FK#fk_indexes]. Error text "foreign key mismatch" (3.7.16 adds both table names) or "no such table" [V].
- DROP TABLE with FKs: an implicit DELETE that runs FK actions but no triggers. Mismatch errors ignored [V].
- ALTER ADD COLUMN NOT NULL needs a non-NULL default. REFERENCES with FKs on needs default NULL. Expression and CURRENT_* defaults refused [V AT#altertabaddcol]. [I] The resulting schema text is the original CREATE TABLE with the column-def text appended. sqlite_schema.sql text is observable via `SELECT sql FROM sqlite_schema`.
- Schema text rewriting is visible: "SQLite stores the schema as plain text in the sqlite_schema table. All ALTER TABLE commands modify that text and then attempt to reparse the entire schema." For DROP COLUMN "the only text modified is that the column definition is removed from the CREATE TABLE statement." [V AT#how_it_works]. RENAME rewrites names inside trigger bodies, views, FK clauses, CHECKs and partial-index WHERE (3.25/3.26). Also names in WITH clauses of views and triggers (CHG 3.27.0) [V]. [I] For sqlite_schema.sql parity sql4s3 must store the original statement text byte-for-byte (whitespace and comments included, as typed) and replicate SQLite's rename-rewrite quoting. 3.5.7: "ALTER TABLE uses double-quotes instead of single-quotes for quoting" [V CHG]. This is a large surface. Consider not exposing sqlite_schema.sql, or postponing ALTER.
- RENAME COLUMN fails "If the column name change would result in a semantic ambiguity in a trigger or view" [V].
- ALTER SET NOT NULL with redundant NOT NULLs: "guaranteed to remove one or more of them, but not necessarily all of them" [V AT#alter_table_alter_column]. Avoidable by refusing CREATE TABLE with duplicate NOT NULL on a column [I].
- Partial index usability is planner-only (no result effect), except that a UNIQUE partial index enforces uniqueness only where WHERE is true; NULL/false rows are excluded [V partialindex].
- Expression and partial indexes: only deterministic functions. Date/time functions with 'now', 'localtime', 'utc', or no arguments are non-deterministic and "will throw an exception" in those contexts (cross-page deterministic.html, CHG 3.20.0 and 3.35.2) [V].
- Generated column value is converted by affinity of its declared type. Its collation comes only from its COLLATE clause [V GC]. STRICT typing enforced on them since 3.51.0 [V].
- Generated columns are left out of table_info and shown in table_xinfo. HIDDEN vtab columns are left out of `*` [V].
- CTAS column names = "the name of the corresponding column in the result set of the SELECT statement" (result naming rules) [V]. CHG 3.22.0/3.21.0 fixed inconsistencies [V].
- View column names without a list come from the result-set naming rules, "not a defined part of the interface" [V].
- UPDATE OF col: fires only if col is on the left side of SET (not if the value is unchanged and not via triggers) [V].
- Triggers: "The trigger fires once for each row" [V]. RAISE(IGNORE) inside a nested trigger resumes the outer trigger "at the beginning of the next step" [V].
- RAISE error code is SQLITE_CONSTRAINT with the given message. Extended code SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_TRIGGER [V lang_createtrigger#raise + rescode]. The message may be any expression since 3.47.0 [V].
- STRICT coercion matrix and the unknown-type error message: not on these pages. Must come from the oracle [I].
- IF NOT EXISTS on a table name that clashes with an index still errors. CREATE TABLE named like a trigger is allowed [V CT].
- "sqlite_" prefix reserved for tables [V CT]. [I] Probably also for indexes, views and triggers. Check against the oracle.
- WITHOUT ROWID PK NULL insert → error [V]. [I] SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL with message "NOT NULL constraint failed: t.c".

### 6.14 Configuration, limits and error rules
