- Unknown PRAGMA: "No error messages are generated if an unknown pragma is issued." [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax]. Measured `pragma no_such_pragma; select 1;` gives `1`. [I] sql4s3 rejects PRAGMA, so the differential harness must treat any PRAGMA input as an expected divergence.
- PRAGMA boolean arguments: "1 yes true on / 0 no false off"; "Keyword arguments can optionally appear in quotes"; queries return numbers [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#syntax]. (Only relevant if a read-only PRAGMA or a pragma_ function is ever supported.)
- pragma_xxx table-valued functions take "the schema as an optional, last argument" [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragfunc].
- table_info `type` is the "data type if given, else ''"; `pk` is "the 1-based index of the column within the primary key"; "The "cid" column should not be taken to mean more than "rank within the current result set"." [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_table_info].
- Result column naming (full_column_names): "If there is an AS clause on the result, then the name of the column is the right-hand side of the AS clause." "If the result is a general expression, not just the name of a source table column, then the name of the result is a copy of the expression text." With short_column_names ON (default, measured), a plain column reference is named COLUMN without the table prefix [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_full_column_names]. [I] "copy of the expression text" means the original source text, including its spacing and case, so sql4s3 must keep token spans.
- LIKE default: "The default behavior of the LIKE operator is to ignore case for ASCII characters. Hence, by default 'a' LIKE 'A' is true." [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_case_sensitive_like]. BLOB operands: "The default behavior of LIKE is that BLOB operands are cast to TEXT before the comparison is done." [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#like_doesnt_match_blobs]. Measured `x'0102' like x'0102'` = 1, `x'61' like 'a'` = 1.
- LIKE/GLOB pattern length > SQLITE_LIMIT_LIKE_PATTERN_LENGTH (default 50000) is an error at run time; measured message "LIKE or GLOB pattern too complex" [V-measured, https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_like_pattern_length].
- TOOBIG: SQLITE_TOOBIG when a string or BLOB "exceeds the compile-time or run-time limit" and for oversized SQL [V https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#toobig]. printf: the result is checked against LIMIT_LENGTH "only ... after all formatting is complete"; widths above SQLITE_PRINTF_PRECISION_LIMIT are "silently truncated" [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#printf_precision_limit]. Measured message "string or blob too big".
- Max string/BLOB length: "only support a string or BLOB length up to 2^31-3 or 2147483645" [V https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_length].
- Host parameters: "?NNN" uses the number after "?"; others number from 1 up; max index is SQLITE_LIMIT_VARIABLE_NUMBER [V https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_variable_number]. Measured error "variable number must be between ?1 and ?N" (N = the limit). **The oracle build allows 250000, not 32766.**
- Column limit covers "The number of values in an INSERT statement" and GROUP BY / ORDER BY terms [V https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_column]. Measured error "too many columns in result set".
- Compound limit: measured "too many terms in compound SELECT". [I] Multi-row VALUES may count toward it, since it "is implemented internally as a compound SELECT" [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#omit_compound_select]. Verify with the oracle.
- Expression depth: measured "Expression tree is too large (maximum depth N)". It also limits "the maximum nesting depth for subqueries and VIEWs" [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_limit_attached.html#sqlitelimitexprdepth].
- Function args: measured "too many arguments on function max" (limit 1000) [V https://sqlite.org/limits.html#max_function_arg].
- Joins limited to 64 tables, with no way to change it [V https://sqlite.org/limits.html].
- Error message for double-quoted non-identifier (DQS=0): measured `no such column: "abc" - should this be a string literal in single-quotes?`. The CLI also prints a caret at the token, i.e. error offset support [V-measured]. sqlite3_error_offset "assumes that the input SQL is UTF-8" and returns -1 if no token is referenced [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/errcode.html].
- Error message text stability: no doc statement of stability; it changed in 3.20.0 [V https://sqlite.org/changes.html]. [I] Match messages exactly only if deliberately pinned to 3.54.1.
- Collation names are case-insensitive: "Collation names that compare equal according to sqlite3_strnicmp() are considered to be the same name." Unknown collation gives SQLITE_ERROR_MISSING_COLLSEQ (257) [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/create_collation.html, https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#error_missing_collseq].
- FP_DIGITS: REAL to TEXT uses 17 significant digits by default (it was 15 before) [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/c_dbconfig_defensive.html#sqlitedbconfigfpdigits]. The changelog dates the change to 3.53.0, c3ref says 3.52.0 [V].
- 64-bit integers: range -9223372036854775808 to +9223372036854775807 [V https://sqlite.org/floatingpoint.html]. "Allow the value -9223372036854775808 as an integer literal" [V changes]. CAST of a float above the max gives +9223372036854775807 [V changes].
- foreign_keys and defer_foreign_keys are no-ops / reset at transaction boundaries: "This pragma is a no-op within a transaction"; defer_foreign_keys "is automatically switched off at each COMMIT or ROLLBACK" [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_foreign_keys, #pragma_defer_foreign_keys]. [I] Configure the oracle with FK on before any BEGIN.
- query_only: CREATE, DELETE, DROP, INSERT and UPDATE give SQLITE_READONLY, "However, the database is not truly read-only. You can still run a checkpoint or a COMMIT" [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_query_only].
- ROLLBACK with pending statements: "Pending write operations always fail with this error [SQLITE_ABORT_ROLLBACK] when a rollback occurs. A ROLLBACK will cause a pending read operation to fail only if the schema was changed within the transaction being rolled back." [V https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#abort_rollback].
- BEGIN IMMEDIATE guarantee: no SQLITE_BUSY until COMMIT once it succeeds [V https://sqlite.org/rescode.html#busy].
- Single-quoted identifiers are accepted (measured: `create table 't1'('a' int)` and `t1.'a'` work with DQS=0) [V-measured]. lang_keywords.html says a single-quoted keyword "used in a context where an identifier is allowed but where a string literal is not allowed ... is understood to be an identifier" [V https://sqlite.org/lang_keywords.html]. No switch disables this.
- DEFAULT clause identifiers: measured `create table t(x text default "abc", y int)` and `default abc` (unquoted) both succeed with DQS=0, and the stored default is the text 'abc' [V-measured]. Rules for this are on other pages (lang_createtable). [I] sql4s3 must refuse these forms; the oracle accepts them.

---

## 7. Conflicts, contradictions and gaps

### 7.1 Conflicts between the docs and the project's decisions

1. **No generated keys at all.** STRICT plus WITHOUT ROWID leaves no rowid, no INTEGER PRIMARY KEY aliasing and no AUTOINCREMENT, so applications must supply every primary key value. [V §3.1, I] `last_insert_rowid()` is always 0 on the oracle. [V https://sqlite.org/c3ref/last_insert_rowid.html, I]
2. **The docs recommend against some WITHOUT ROWID tables.** They advise against tables with a single INTEGER primary key, and against rows larger than about 1/20 of a page. [V https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html#wtu] This does not change SQL results. It affects the local SQLite cache if the cache mirrors the schema. [I]
3. **Change-capture hooks do not see WITHOUT ROWID tables.** The update hook does not fire for them [V https://sqlite.org/withoutrowid.html], and the pre-update hook is documented for rowid tables [V https://sqlite.org/compile.html#enable_preupdate_hook]. This matters for any cache design that relies on hooks. [I]
4. **DQS=0 does not fully match "double quotes are identifiers only".** The oracle still accepts `DEFAULT "abc"`, a bare `DEFAULT abc` and single-quoted identifiers. [V-measured] These become expected divergences.
5. **Several restrictions have no oracle switch:** STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID, no ANY, and no bracket or backtick identifiers. Rejecting PRAGMA statements needs an authorizer callback. [V §4.1]
6. **Schema tables do not follow the table restrictions.** `sqlite_schema` is a rowid table, `sqlite_stat1` comes from ANALYZE, and the JSON table-valued functions have `ANY` columns. Exposing any of them would break the "all tables STRICT and WITHOUT ROWID" model. [V §3.1, I]
7. **"No PRAGMA switches" does not cover read-only introspection.** WITHOUT ROWID detection and the table lists are available only through PRAGMA or pragma table-valued functions. [V https://sqlite.org/pragma.html] Introspection would need its own design. [I]

### 7.2 Contradictions inside the documentation

- **Release of the FP_DIGITS change.** floatingpoint.html and c3ref say 3.52.0. The release history says 3.52.0 was withdrawn and lists the change under 3.53.0. [V]
- **Number of primary result codes.** rescode.html says "The 31 result codes"; c3ref/c_abort_rollback says "one of 30 integer result codes". [V]
- **Result type of `%`.** lang_expr says it "casts both of its operands to type INTEGER". datatype3 says it "returns either INTEGER or REAL (or NULL) depending on the type of its operands". [V] Probe it.
- **`%p` in format().** lang_corefunc calls it "an alias for %X"; printf.html says it works like `%x`. [V]
- **replace() with an empty search string.** The 3.4.0 changelog says it returns NULL. The current page says it returns X unchanged. [V]
- **Conflict clause on a table CHECK.** The prose allows one, "For historical compatibility only", but the table-constraint diagram shows none. [V]
- **ALTER TABLE forms.** The diagram shows `ADD [CONSTRAINT name] CHECK(...)` and `DROP CONSTRAINT name` (3.53.0), but the prose does not describe them. [V-diagram]
- **json_insert and json_set with BLOBs.** They are said to reject any BLOB argument, yet JSONB BLOBs are said to be accepted wherever text JSON is. [V]
- **NULL in scalar max() and min().** Scalar max() is documented to return NULL if any argument is NULL; scalar min() is silent. [V]
- **Recursive triggers.** compile_options of the measured build lists `DEFAULT_RECURSIVE_TRIGGERS`, yet `recursive_triggers` reads 0. compile.html has no entry for this option. [V-measured, V]
- **GROUP BY name binding.** The rule "Identifiers in GROUP BY clauses always prefer output column names" comes from the 3.8.0 release notes, not from a current page. [V https://sqlite.org/changes.html] Probe it.

### 7.3 Not found in the documentation (probe the oracle)

- **Error message text.**
  - No page says that message text is stable; the 3.20.0 release changed it. [V]
  - These messages are not documented: syntax errors; a STRICT table with a disallowed type; a missing PRIMARY KEY in a WITHOUT ROWID table; CHECK failures; a nested BEGIN; COMMIT or ROLLBACK with no transaction; an unknown savepoint; an ORDER BY term out of range; a non-integer LIMIT; compound arms with different column counts; malformed JSON text.
- **The full STRICT coercion matrix.** Which value of which class converts losslessly into which column type. Only the '123' example and the word "lossless" are documented. [V]
- **Result column names** for anything other than AS aliases, bare columns and "a copy of the expression text". This includes VALUES, `*` after USING and NATURAL joins, subqueries and CTEs.
- **REAL-to-text details:** exponent format, the plain-to-exponent cutover, `-0.0`, and whether integral REALs keep `.0`.
- **Function edge cases:**
  - round() tie-breaking;
  - substr() with a start of 0;
  - whether ceil(), floor() and trunc() return INTEGER or REAL;
  - `%d` of a REAL in format();
  - NULL handling of hex, lower, upper, trim, replace, quote, like and glob;
  - the version that introduced sign().
- **Whether changes() counts** an UPSERT DO UPDATE, and whether rows deleted by REPLACE run foreign-key actions.
- **Timing and code of the STRICT check** on generated columns (3.51.0).
- **Whether `INTEGER(5)`** (a type name with a size) is accepted in a STRICT table.
- **The keyword-as-identifier fallback list.** The docs give the 147 keywords, but not which ones the parser accepts unquoted as identifiers, or where.
- **Whether `SELECT * FROM pragma_xxx()`** triggers the authorizer's PRAGMA action.
